**[Endosymbiosis and reductive evolution](tags/endosymbiosis.md)** (6) — how one organism comes to live inside another and depend on it, and how that ends — reductive genome evolution by drift, deletional bias or adaptive loss (including the Black Queen dependencies of free-living microbes), complementary loss across partners, nested symbioses and symbiont splitting and replacement, endosymbiotic gene transfer, protein import and host control of transmission, organellogenesis and organelle loss, and eukaryogenesis. The ecology of a partnership is ecology (ADR-055):

**[Learning theory](tags/learning-theory.md)** (29) — why learning generalizes and how it proceeds — compression and description length, Kolmogorov complexity and sufficient statistics, rate–distortion and PAC-style bounds, model selection; and training dynamics: solvable models of learning trajectories, stages, plateaus and phase transitions in training, and the implicit bias of optimisation. A work whose subject is how to train a model better is ML practice and goes to the anthology (ADR-036):

**[Compositionality](tags/compositionality.md)** (3) — how the structure and meaning of a whole come from its parts and how they are combined — the principle of compositionality and compositional semantics, the emergence of compositional structure in languages under transmission, and composition and binding in learned models (generalization to unseen combinations, attribute binding, composing distributions or concepts). A work on making a model generalize compositionally is ML practice and goes to the anthology (ADR-031):
[004](../../record/cases.d/CASE-004.md) · [009](../../record/cases.d/CASE-009.md) · [325](../../record/cases.d/CASE-325.md)

**[Mathematical statistics](tags/mathematical-statistics.md)** (13) — the theory of statistical inference read for itself — comparison and sufficiency of experiments (Blackwell, Le Cam deficiency), statistical decision theory, the sufficiency theorems (Fisher–Neyman, Basu, Bahadur) and bounds on estimation. Bayesian modelling is probabilistic-modeling (ADR-031):

**[Databases and data structures](tags/databases.md)** (1) — how stored data is organized so that it can be found and kept current — physical database design and index selection, materialized views and the choice of which answers to precompute, adaptive and self-organizing structures, lower bounds on lookup, caching and paging; and the cost of organizing ahead of use against organizing in use. What the status column means in this scheme — the words are luria's, the meanings are this project's.

| Status | | Means |
|---|---|---|
| `Active` | Active | In force — the current answer, and what a citation should normally point at |
| `Proposed` | Proposed | Not in force yet — an open question, so citing it as settled is the thing the report catches |
| `Deferred` | Deferred | Not in force and not being worked on; the question is real and the answer is waiting on something |
| `Superseded` | Superseded | No longer in force because something replaced it; the successor is named in the field, not the prose |
| `Rejected` | Rejected | No longer in force and nothing replaced it — kept because a rejection is worth being able to point at |

| # | Title | Summary | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| [CASE-001](../../record/cases.d/CASE-001.md) | Two reset matrices: noncommuting and classical | A30: on a two-state classical system, A resets to state 1 and B to state 2; AB = A and BA = B, so AB ≠ BA, and every state is an ordinary probability vector. Order dependence without nonclassical probability. | Active |
| [CASE-002](../../record/cases.d/CASE-002.md) | Northstar classification: who decides an incident is severe | A severe-incident rule with strong precedence, whose force depends on which team controls severity classification, plus a business-critical override held by senior management (Sections 5.3–5.5). | Active |
| [CASE-003](../../record/cases.d/CASE-003.md) | Northstar-A and Northstar-B: same policy, same evidence, different escalation authority | Two versions held constant in attitudes, policy, monitoring and evidence, differing only in whether a specialist's objection suspends deployment (Section 5.1). The paper's controlled contrast. | Active |
| [CASE-004](../../record/cases.d/CASE-004.md) v2 | Two people, an empty wallet: shared complicity, normative authority, and an underspecified relation | A97's prompt conditions for the text-to-image case: the same objects with different interpersonal configurations. Conditions A and B are the manuscript's §7 example; condition C, the natural control, was dropped. | Active |
| [CASE-005](../../record/cases.d/CASE-005.md) | The language-model experiments: frame commutator, trajectory-preserving selection, ICL against TTT | A45 §5, kept in outlines v3 and v4: order effects of opposed versus independent framings in an LLM, a comparison of three ways of choosing a translation against human judgement, and prompt-only versus test- time-trained adaptation. Proposed, not run. | Active |
| [CASE-006](../../record/cases.d/CASE-006.md) | Open Horizon | A fictional cooperative founded for openness and public benefit whose sustaining mechanisms come to outweigh its mission as it grows (A31): persistence through turnover with the founding intentions lost. | Active |
| [CASE-007](../../record/cases.d/CASE-007.md) | CoreWeave: a climate-concerned workforce building fossil-powered datacenters | The owner's case at U13: employees largely concerned about climate, a company whose datacenters, sited where energy is cheap, induce fossil fuel demand. Replaced by the fictional [CASE-025](../../record/cases.d/CASE-025.md) at the owner's suggestion (U15). | Superseded — by [CASE-025](../../record/cases.d/CASE-025.md) |
| [CASE-008](../../record/cases.d/CASE-008.md) v2 | Solidarity, condemnation and humour judged in pairs: the probabilistic version | A24: three judgements of Henley's poem, S (solidarity), M (condemnation), H (humour), elicited in pairwise contexts, asking whether the pairwise distributions are marginals of one global distribution. Replaced in the manuscript by a deterministic parity example. | Superseded — by [CASE-035](../../record/cases.d/CASE-035.md) |
| [CASE-009](../../record/cases.d/CASE-009.md) | Text → image → caption → image chains under four conditions | A97 §6: compare text-only, image-only, alternating, and alternating with explicit preservation of pragmatic constraints. The manuscript's Case III keeps alternating against text-only. Proposed, not run. | Active |
| [CASE-010](../../record/cases.d/CASE-010.md) v2 | Henley's rendering of Villon, "Booze and the blowens cop the lot" | W. E. Henley's rendering of a Villon ballade into nineteenth-century English thieves' slang, refrain "Booze and the blowens cop the lot" (U3). Already a radical translation, and deliberately opaque. The source of the manuscript's refrain family (§1, §10). | Active |
| [CASE-011](../../record/cases.d/CASE-011.md) | Hofstadter's mother's translation | A rendering of Marot that broke most of the formal constraints and that Hofstadter came to judge perhaps the best. Refutes fidelity as a count of preserved constraints (A4). Absent from the manuscript. | Active |
| [CASE-012](../../record/cases.d/CASE-012.md) | The modernized refrains of Henley's Villon | Invented modern refrains for Henley's ballade (A6–A9), each a different speaker: "You blow it all on sex and booze", "By Monday morning, you're broke again", "The party always gets its cut", "The high life eats your paycheck". Two survive as the manuscript's opening example (§1). | Active |
| [CASE-013](../../record/cases.d/CASE-013.md) | The reluctant writer | A writer sincerely committed to finishing an article while avoidance governs the afternoon; who later blocks distracting websites. The individual-scale case (Sections 1, 3.5). | Active |
| [CASE-014](../../record/cases.d/CASE-014.md) v2 | Microsoft, as the philosophical target | The real corporation the first framing asked about. Committed to as a recurring touchstone (A205) and then silently dropped from the drafts; retired here by status rather than left as a promise nobody kept. | Deferred |
| [CASE-015](../../record/cases.d/CASE-015.md) | The telephone game | The owner's case at U20: a message passed along a chain stays recognizable while drifting. Shows similarity, not equivalence, as the working notion, and opened the serial-reconstruction line (A50, outline v4's running case B, the manuscript's §8 and Case II). | Active |
| [CASE-016](../../record/cases.d/CASE-016.md) | The Dual State: normative and prerogative authority in National Socialist Germany | Fraenkel's analysis: ordinary legal norms kept governing large domains while a prerogative order held authority over whether they bound at all. Used for one structural point only. | Active |
| [CASE-017](../../record/cases.d/CASE-017.md) | E1: synthetic three-state stochastic computations (C2–C3) | Run at U23 ("run it"): hand-chosen row-stochastic matrices on three abstract states, seed 20261008, "not fitted to text, pretrained models, or human measurements". Executed; results are exact properties of the chosen matrices. None reached a later draft. | Active |
| [CASE-018](../../record/cases.d/CASE-018.md) | "You blow it all on sex and booze", said by a friend, a parent, a judge, a comedian | The same sentence attributed to different speakers (A12, A18, A24): one teases, another reproaches, another condemns, another entertains. Words held fixed, act changed. Survives as the joke/reprimand contrast (§2) and the social-alignment manipulation of Case III (§10). | Active |
| [CASE-019](../../record/cases.d/CASE-019.md) | "Hug from Doug" | A rendering of Marot that puts the translator's name in the slot where Marot put his own. Wrong as a historical translation, right as a relation. The exchange's first statement of relational invariance (A4). | Active |
| [CASE-020](../../record/cases.d/CASE-020.md) v2 | E3: the controlled inference harness, dry run only (C5) | Built at U25: four frame conditions (stable complicit, neutral, moralizing; shifting), 12 reset chains each, 5 generations, 240 calls, gpt-4.1-mini at temperature 0.7, seed 1729. Only a dry run was executed: "No model-generated observations were recorded." The design is the manuscript's Case II. | Active |
| [CASE-021](../../record/cases.d/CASE-021.md) | Wording, attributed speaker and judgement order: the proposed factorial study | Proposed at A21 and refined at A24, not run: Henley renderings × attributed speaker × order of judgement questions, with hierarchical, sequential state-update and quantum models compared. Becomes the manuscript's Case I (§10). | Active |
| [CASE-022](../../record/cases.d/CASE-022.md) | A rendering that keeps the solidarity–criticism ambiguity open, and one that settles it | A34 §4 and A48 §7.5: two renderings with similar static profiles, one of which stays sensitive to a later affiliative framing because it sustains the ambiguity, while the other has already fixed the speaker as condemning. The concrete mechanism behind dynamical fidelity; dropped at outline v4. | Active |
| [CASE-023](../../record/cases.d/CASE-023.md) | Northstar | A fictional software company whose stated policy gives reliability priority over release speed, and whose escalation and authority structures may make speed effective anyway. The paper's worked case, in several variants. | Active |
| [CASE-024](../../record/cases.d/CASE-024.md) v2 | A coral reef | The owner's second holobiont direction (U30): a reef as a coordinated ecological whole. Its job changed four times in the exchange; filed once so that each change is visible. | Active |
| [CASE-025](../../record/cases.d/CASE-025.md) | Verdant Compute | A fictional compute company whose decarbonization-minded workforce operates an organization whose market position drives fossil-fuel demand (A31). Shows the whole's operative direction diverging from the average of its members' intentions. | Active |
| [CASE-026](../../record/cases.d/CASE-026.md) | Modern slang that makes the insider sound like an outsider imitating the subculture | A35 §7: replacing Henley's argot with contemporary slang may keep the poem intelligible while making the speaker sound like an outsider, so the audience understands the observation and loses the complicity. Interpretive fidelity kept, performative fidelity lost. | Active |
| [CASE-027](../../record/cases.d/CASE-027.md) | Henley translated perfectly in the voice of an academic sociologist | A12's thought experiment: every term rendered exactly, every proposition kept, but the speaker made to sound like an academic sociologist. Something fundamental has changed, so keeping the proposition is not sufficient. | Active |
| [CASE-028](../../record/cases.d/CASE-028.md) | Stability AI: well-intentioned founders, near-total churn, generic successor | The owner's case at U12: a company founded by researchers and open-source developers that churned almost its whole staff in a year and became a generic corporate entity. Replaced by the fictional [CASE-006](../../record/cases.d/CASE-006.md). | Superseded — by [CASE-006](../../record/cases.d/CASE-006.md) |
| [CASE-029](../../record/cases.d/CASE-029.md) | The dynamical-fidelity research programme of the outlines (A35, A38, A40) | The factorial study grown into a programme over three replies: initial state and three model classes (A35), matched and divergent stimulus pairs (A38), pilot validation, classical baselines first and five falsifiers (A40). Proposed, not run. | Active |
| [CASE-030](../../record/cases.d/CASE-030.md) | E2: assistant-authored reconstruction chains with content-word overlap (C4) | Run at U24 ("do it"): three four-generation chains written by the assistant from a modernized Villon seed, scored by content-word Jaccard against the seed. The texts are authored with their intended trajectories; the numbers are exact properties of those texts. | Active |
| [CASE-031](../../record/cases.d/CASE-031.md) v2 | Marot's "A une Damoyselle malade" and Hofstadter's translations of it | A sixteenth-century get-well poem whose content is trivial and whose form is everything, translated dozens of ways by Hofstadter and others. The exchange's founding case, and the manuscript's opening one (§1). | Active |
| [CASE-032](../../record/cases.d/CASE-032.md) | E4: within-session chains, stable complicit against a shift to moral instruction | Run after the owner's U26 ("lol you ARE a text generation action. just delegate subtasks"): two three-generation chains written by the assistant in the same context. "The measured lexical similarities are near zero for both final texts"; one keeps complicity, the other becomes advice. | Active |
| [CASE-033](../../record/cases.d/CASE-033.md) | Driving home while daydreaming | The owner's case at U26: driving home with access to the road and to a daydream, arriving with no memory of the drive. Compartmented access, not unconsciousness. | Active |
| [CASE-034](../../record/cases.d/CASE-034.md) | The thermostat and the adaptive controller | Systems that regulate, and even alter their own regulatory parameters, without warranting attribution of an agent. The stock counterexample to will as regulation. | Active |
| [CASE-035](../../record/cases.d/CASE-035.md) v2 | Solidarity, condemnation and humour: the parity obstruction | The manuscript's §3 version: S = M, M = H and H ≠ S are each satisfiable in its own context but admit no global assignment. Stated to demonstrate an obstruction, not to describe actual judgements. | Active |
| [CASE-036](../../record/cases.d/CASE-036.md) v2 | Northstar emergency governance that sustains itself | A service failure activates emergency governance whose restrictions defer maintenance, generating anomalies that satisfy the emergency criteria and keep the restrictions in place (Section 6.1). | Active |
| [CASE-037](../../record/cases.d/CASE-037.md) v2 | The parity triangle with uniform weights: a no-signalling, strongly contextual model beside a family of point sections that glues | [CASE-035](../../record/cases.d/CASE-035.md)'s constraints given probabilities, at A129 §2, and set beside a deterministic family on the same cover that glues to (0, 1, 0). It shows that the obstruction is in the supports, not in the event sheaf. It appears three times (A129 §2, A161, A178's negative control), so it gets a code. | Active |
| [CASE-038](../../record/cases.d/CASE-038.md) v2 | TV Tropes: a community-curated catalogue of narrative tropes and their instances, such as the Gilligan Cut | The owner proposed it at U44 as a reference case. A155 read a trope as a relational pattern recognized across changes of characters, medium and wording, and pointed to Gala et al.'s dataset as a large set of instances already annotated. A191 §8 added five manipulations of one trope, the Gilligan Cut. v7 (A178) makes it Study B, and A218 keeps it as Study 5. The first time the thing turns up in the record. The catalogue is documented; the experiments on it are proposals. | Active |
| [CASE-039](../../record/cases.d/CASE-039.md) | Who crosses the meeting point first? Two craft near light speed, and the relativity of simultaneity | The owner's U47: two craft approach each other near light speed, and each frame is said to answer that it crosses the meeting point first, so the local interpretations cannot be glued. A164 corrected the physics. Read either way, the owner's own scenario has an invariant answer; only spacelike-separated events change order between frames, and there the frames are reconciled by a Lorentz transformation. Standard special relativity; the scenario is the owner's. | Active |
| [CASE-040](../../record/cases.d/CASE-040.md) v2 | Topic and act crossed: a factorial design that decorrelates what a text is about from what it does | A214 §8's design, which A218 names as the first experiment to run: topic (spending, relationships) crossed with act (teasing, reprimand), with lexical, judgement, uptake and intervention data, several factorizations, and four outcomes reported separately. A210 §5 gives a cross-corpus version. Proposed by the assistant; not run. | Active |
| [CASE-041](../../record/cases.d/CASE-041.md) v2 | FID and Projected GANs: one ImageNet-trained probe family used both to train a generator and to evaluate it | The owner pointed to FID at U50 and proposed FID with Projected GANs as a paired case study at U51. A184 framed the pair: FID evaluates a distribution through probes, and Projected GANs learn one under pressure from probes. The anthology's readings supply the fact the exchange lacked. The Projected GAN discriminator is ImageNet-trained, FID's features are ImageNet class evidence, and Kynkäänniemi et al. show that such a generator can match FID while doing worse in an independent feature space and with human judges. | Active |
| [CASE-042](../../record/cases.d/CASE-042.md) v3 | Collaborative filtering and topic models: latent relational structure recovered from interaction matrices, up to gauge, or uniquely under anchor conditions | Proposed as a laboratory by the owner at U57 and conceded as prior art after the owner's U58. Collaborative filtering recovers a user–item matrix's predicted relations with latent coordinates fixed only up to an invertible change; topic models recover individual topics only under nonnegativity plus separability or anchor-word conditions. It shows that recovery is possible and limited, not that a recovered component is a communicative kind. | Active |
| [CASE-043](../../record/cases.d/CASE-043.md) | Data written for a future reader and never read | The owner's variant of the tree in the woods (2026-10-11). Someone writes a record (a note, a log, a letter, a backup) for a reader they expect, and it is never read. Unlike the tree, the write was made for someone. Used to separate when a message's intended meaning is fixed from when its fidelity is measured. | Active |
| [CASE-044](../../record/cases.d/CASE-044.md) | The thermostat: Dennett's knife edge between agent and device | An ordinary thermostat holding a room at a setpoint. Dennett calls it the simplest artifact that can sustain the intentional stance, and Orseau et al. place it "on the knife edge": "if temperature is below the command, start heating" and "make sure the temperature is close to the command" are descriptions of comparable simplicity. It is the permissiveness objection's standard case, here given a code at its second appearance after [CLAIM-010](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-010.md). | Active |
| [CASE-045](../../record/cases.d/CASE-045.md) v4 | Measuring the dancing couple: is the pair a nearly decomposable block during the dance and not after it? | A proposed study, not run. Motion-capture several couples dancing an improvised partner dance to recorded music, and record the same people standing and talking after the song. Fit a linear model to the joint kinematics, with the music's beat as an exogenous input. Then ask whether the pair is a block whose gap beats its Occam factor during the dance and not after, whether it compensates under a perturbation, and whether the coupling runs both ways. | Active |
| [CASE-046](../../record/cases.d/CASE-046.md) v2 | A partner-dancing couple, lindy hop, tango or salsa, while they dance | The owner's case (2026-10-11). Two social dancers improvise together. The lead proposes through the frame, the follow interprets and adds styling, and each corrects for the other's balance and timing. Neither alone decides where the next step lands. Used to ask what the pair is while dancing, and only after that whether it is a self. | Active |
| [CASE-047](../../record/cases.d/CASE-047.md) | Gut microbes and the host's hunger and mood: a lower-level collective that can drive the person | The owner's example (2026-10-11). The human gut microbiome is a community of other organisms inside the person. Through metabolites, mimics of host appetite peptides, the immune system and the vagus, it can change hunger and affect: robustly in rodents, modestly and inconsistently in humans so far. Documented, with the evidence graded in [LIT-903](../../record/literature.d/LIT-903.md) and [LIT-915](../../record/literature.d/LIT-915.md). Used as the case of drive from a lower level that is itself a collective. | Active |
| [CASE-048](../../record/cases.d/CASE-048.md) v3 | The same film joke, seen as a child and again as an adult | The owner's case (2026-10-11): one person watches a scene in a family film or show as a child and again as an adult, and the joke means something different the second time. The artefact is unchanged, and so is the person in the sense of numerical identity. Only the receiver's state has changed. It is a test for whether meaning is fixed at the write or made at the read. | Active |
| [CASE-049](../../record/cases.d/CASE-049.md) | A tree falls in the woods with no one to hear it | The old puzzle, filed so its variants have something to vary. A physical event happens with no perceiver. Whether it "made a sound" turns on whether sound means the vibration or the hearing. Nothing in it was made for anyone, and that is the respect the storage variant changes. | Active |
| [CASE-050](../../record/cases.d/CASE-050.md) v2 | The all-hands: a corporate event as a synchronization mechanism for the firm | The owner's case (2026-10-11): a company all-hands or offsite as a mechanism that synchronizes a "corporate entity", a kind of self. The teams' priorities have drifted apart, and the event re-couples them. It is the firm-scale counterpart of the session's individual routes: entrainment by co-presence, a record written for later readers, and incentives realigned. Whether the firm so synchronized is a self is the open question, not something the case settles. | Active |
| [CASE-051](../../record/cases.d/CASE-051.md) | The absorbed writer | The reluctant writer ([CASE-013](../../record/cases.d/CASE-013.md)) on a third afternoon. The draft is going well and email stays open and unblocked, but it does not win, and no rule or device is invoked. A notification breaks the spell, and getting back in takes effort. It shows integration with nothing to integrate, beside the lapse and the precommitment. | Active |
| [CASE-052](../../record/cases.d/CASE-052.md) | Stokely Carmichael's two speaking styles, kept apart by audience and collapsed by broadcast | As boyd (2008) reports it after Meyrowitz (1985: 43): the civil rights leader Stokely Carmichael used very different rhetorical styles with black and with white audiences. While the audiences were separate the two styles did not conflict. Television and radio put both audiences in front of one speech, there was no neutral style, and his choice of one alienated the other audience. The roles did not change. The cover did. | Active |
| [CASE-053](../../record/cases.d/CASE-053.md) v2 | Contextuality tests on human meaning and judgement data: a held null, and positive results that miss pragmatic content or power | The published tests of contextuality proper on elicited human judgements, read together. The null that meets every condition is the record's own [LIT-264](../../record/literature.d/LIT-264.md): attitudinal and lexical data, none contextual under Contextuality-by-Default. Wang's thesis adds a Bell-type null on meaning judgements. The positive results miss a condition: Cervantes and Dzhafarov's "Snow Queen" is contextual with high reliability but its content is instructed attribute matching, and Lo, Sadrzadeh and Mansfield's Winograd schemas have the right content but a violation of about one standard deviation. No contextual pragmatic data are documented; "none in behavioural data" is too broad. | Active |
| [CASE-054](../../record/cases.d/CASE-054.md) v2 | Joking relationships: one kind among the Crow and in Africa with no route between them, descended between Crow and Hidatsa, and a transported tale beside them | Radcliffe-Brown defined the joking relationship as one comparative kind and placed the Crow joking-relative and Dogon and Thonga joking in it; no historical route joins the Crow to West or South-East Africa. Lowie records the same institution among the Hidatsa, a division of the Crow's parent tribe, and Tehrani's phylogeny confirms an Ibo "Little Red Riding Hood" as an oral translation of the European tale. Kind without transport, kind with descent, and a transport, on documented material, for [CLAIM-210](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-210.md). Partial: independence is a fact of geography no source states, and the kind is an analyst's. | Active |
| [CASE-055](../../record/cases.d/CASE-055.md) v2 | The discriminating fidelity study: renderings built to dissociate wording, proposition, static stance and transport structure, judged by named populations in two languages on stated tasks | A proposed study, filed on 2026-10-10 as the record's repair of [CLAIM-233](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-233.md), [CLAIM-232](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-232.md), [CLAIM-244](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-244.md), [CLAIM-226](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-226.md), [CLAIM-222](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-222.md) and [CLAIM-227](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-227.md). It restores [CASE-005](../../record/cases.d/CASE-005.md)'s three-way selection with human receivers, and adds a proposition-plus-force rival, a behavioural task-indexed criterion, predictors rated by other raters, named populations, an interlingual arm and a measurement stage. It has not been run, and its margins are not yet fixed. | Active |
| [CASE-056](../../record/cases.d/CASE-056.md) v3 | J. B. Phillips's "hearty handshake all round" for Paul's "holy kiss", and Nida's judgement that it keeps the greeting | Phillips renders all five New Testament "holy kiss" directives (Romans 16:16 and four parallels) as handshakes, which changes what the congregation is told to do; Nida (1964, p. 160) holds this up as equivalent effect, and the UBS handbooks call it a brave attempt. The documented case for the necessity half, [CLAIM-486](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-486.md) since the split of 2026-10-28, on expert judgement rather than reader evidence. The same handbooks document the sufficiency half, [CLAIM-061](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-061.md): where kissing is amorous, a literal "kiss" changes the act. | Active |
| [CASE-057](../../record/cases.d/CASE-057.md) v2 | Swapping the two persons of a dyad: nonreciprocal T/V address is changed by the swap and reciprocal address is not, with a constructed French–English dyad and a three-person teasing round | Built on a documented distinction. In Brown and Gilman's account of T/V address, power address is nonreciprocal (the superior says T and receives V) and solidarity address is reciprocal, so swapping the two persons changes the first act and leaves the second fixed. Two constructions extend it. A master-and-servant dyad in French and in modern English is changed by the swap in one realization and fixed by it in the other. A three-person teasing round gives a group whose automorphism can swap characters, so the anchoring of [CLAIM-249](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-249.md) has work to do. | Active |
| [CASE-058](../../record/cases.d/CASE-058.md) v2 | FactBank's source-relative factuality labels and Kiesling et al.'s stance-triangle annotation: one stance construct annotated reliably, the interpersonal ones not | FactBank labels one event's factuality separately for each source that reports it, so a proposition held fixed gets different commitment labels by source, and trained annotators agree at κ = 0.82. Kiesling et al. annotate Du Bois's stance triangle in Reddit threads, including an investment dimension defined as the split of animator from principal, and reach only α = 0.40 to 0.57, with investment and alignment below 0.4 outside one community. Together they bracket an answer to [QUESTION-076](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-076.md): commitment relative to a source is annotatable when defined by tests, interpersonal stance only moderately. | Active |
| [CASE-059](../../record/cases.d/CASE-059.md) | Tatiana's letter in Eugene Onegin III: вы to ты and back, lost in Kline and half kept in Spalding | Tatiana's letter to Onegin opens with the formal вы, breaks into the intimate ты at "я твоя", and returns to вы in its last lines; only the pronouns mark the moves. Kline (2009) has "you" throughout, so neither move is in the English; Spalding (1881) switches to "thou" and never returns. A documented candidate for the half of a change of cover in which the source decides a relational question that a rendering makes undecidable. The texts are attested and the loss is visible; native attestation is thin and there is no evidence on English readers. | Active |
| [CASE-060](../../record/cases.d/CASE-060.md) | Two identical unnumbered ballots in one tally: two votes that no relation in the structure of the vote tells apart | Constructed: two voters each put a single cross for the same candidate on identical, unnumbered ballot papers in one box, and both are counted. Within the relations that make a paper a vote, each bears the same relations to everything and none to the other, so they are not even weakly discernible, yet the count is two. Anchored on a documented norm that voids any paper marked so that the voter can be identified (UK RPA 1983, Sch. 1, r. 47(1)(c)). It forces the choice between [CLAIM-136](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-136.md)'s strict and weak versions, and bears on [CLAIM-138](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-138.md). | Active |
| [CASE-061](../../record/cases.d/CASE-061.md) v3 | Wonder-working power: one hymn phrase in a national address, heard as faith by those who sing it and as boilerplate by others | In the State of the Union of 28 January 2003 George W. Bush said "there's power, wonder-working power, in the goodness and idealism and faith of the American people", lifting the refrain of an 1899 gospel hymn verbatim. Churchgoers who knew the hymn heard the power of Christ; others heard boilerplate; an experiment with an ingroup and a religiously diverse outgroup measured the split. The same-wording variant of [CASE-064](../../record/cases.d/CASE-064.md), illustrating [CLAIM-224](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-224.md) without translation. | Active |
| [CASE-062](../../record/cases.d/CASE-062.md) v2 | Simplified texts for restricted and for full readers: a gain and a loss documented on different texts, documented nulls and a reversal, and a design that tests both on the same renderings | A constructed study, built on documented halves. Simplified texts, made from the source alone, raised comprehension for named restricted readers (adults and adolescents with autism; weaker second-graders), and automatic simplifications lowered it for fluent native readers, but in different studies on different texts. Edited subtitles did not help deaf viewers, and text reduction in captions helped the more proficient readers more. The stipulated design puts both populations on the same renderings, with probe items fixed in advance as answerable from both versions or from the original only. | Active |
| [CASE-063](../../record/cases.d/CASE-063.md) v2 | The English localization of Ace Attorney: a Japanese courtroom game moved to an alternate Los Angeles, with "textually accurate" set against "emotionally accurate" by its localizer | Capcom's Gyakuten Saiban (2001) became Phoenix Wright: Ace Attorney (2005) by moving the courtroom from Japan to Los Angeles. Its second localizer, Janet Hsu, names two fidelity targets in print, "textually accurate" against "emotionally accurate", and states the correspondence of situations she works to, an alternate Los Angeles where the California Alien Land Law of 1913 "was never passed". The first, Alexander O. Smith, states the criterion for re-performance. The reception is contested on record. No reader has been asked a report question and a re-performance question about the same lines. | Active |
| [CASE-064](../../record/cases.d/CASE-064.md) | Lindsey Stone's Arlington photograph: one post, taken by friends as a tasteless in-joke and by strangers as contempt | In 2012 Lindsey Stone posed at Arlington National Cemetery mock-shouting and giving the finger beside a "Silence And Respect" sign, part of a running joke of photos that disobeyed signs. Friends who knew the joke called it tasteless; four weeks later strangers read the same post as contempt for the war dead, and she lost her job. A change of audience with no translation and the token unchanged, illustrating [CLAIM-224](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-224.md). Partial: the token is a photograph, not wording. | Active |
| [CASE-065](../../record/cases.d/CASE-065.md) | Two satisfaction questions in German and in a back-translated Chinese version: an order effect that reverses across a rendering, with the Clinton–Gore poll as the scale of the 2ε bound | Haberstroh, Oyserman, Schwarz, Kühnen and Ji (2002), Experiment 2, asked German and Chinese students the same two satisfaction questions, in a back-translated Chinese version, in both orders. The part–whole correlation depends on order in the German administration and reverses in the Chinese one, and the authors explain it by a pragmatic mechanism. Only correlations are reported, so the case shows the bound's contrapositive in kind, not in number. Moore's Clinton–Gore poll, held through [LIT-834](../../record/literature.d/LIT-834.md), supplies the number: an order effect of total variation about 0.095, against a sampling floor of about 0.03 per order at poll sizes, so 2ε is about as large as the effect it would certify. | Active |
| [CASE-066](../../record/cases.d/CASE-066.md) | Dolly's slip into ты in Anna Karenina I.4, and three English translations that carry it three ways | In Anna Karenina I.4 Dolly says one sentence to Stiva with ты amid вы, and the narrator says so. English has no live T/V pronoun. Dole (1886) keeps the Russian word and glosses it; Garnett (1901) adds "Stiva"; the Maudes (1918) add "our children". The later two add words not in the Russian to carry the relation. Tolstoy himself says вы and its dictionary equivalent vous differ in coldness. A documented instance of the compensation mechanism; the evidence on what readers recover is translators' and the author's, not readers'. | Active |
| [CASE-067](../../record/cases.d/CASE-067.md) | The reception of Deborah Smith's The Vegetarian: the Booker judges' task, the Korean critics' task, and one critic's two verdicts | Deborah Smith's English version of Han Kang's 채식주의자 won the 2016 Man Booker International Prize, while Korean critics published lists of errors and charged it with embellishment. Each side's task is stated in print: the judges evaluate "the finished English-language work on their own terms", while assessment in Korea "does usually involve comparison". Charse Yun perceives what both sides perceive and gives two verdicts by changing task. Part of the split is access, not weight: some errors are invisible to readers without Korean. | Active |
| [CASE-068](../../record/cases.d/CASE-068.md) | Risk messages passed down diffusion chains: the evaluative signal kept and amplified while the content decays (Moussaïd, Brighton and Gaissmaier; Jagiello and Hills) | In two serial-reproduction experiments on risk messages, each link's message was coded for content and, on a separate scheme, for the evaluative stance of its statements toward the hazard. Content got shorter, less accurate and more divergent; the evaluative signal survived and drifted negative, and re-exposure to the balanced source did not undo the drift. The transmitters' own risk perception, measured by questionnaire, predicts the direction of the change. Documented chain evidence for evaluative stance; none for footing. | Active |
| [CASE-069](../../record/cases.d/CASE-069.md) v2 | Labov's recorded sounding sessions: ritual insult turning personal, with the same exchange shape and a different act | Labov's team taped sounding among Harlem peer groups. Boot answers David's taunt with a remark "related to A-5 in exactly the way that one sound is related to another", yet both are taken as personal insults: denied, objected to, wept over. Labov's rule puts the difference in a further relation, the shared knowledge before an audience that the proposition is untrue. A documented, non-institutional case for [CLAIM-151](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-151.md) in which the act follows a relation among the parties, and for [CLAIM-138](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-138.md), since the exchange relations alone match and the acts still differ. | Active |
| [CASE-070](../../record/cases.d/CASE-070.md) v2 | Voita, Sennrich and Titov's BLEU-tied translation systems that differ on keeping ты or вы across sentences | Two English-to-Russian subtitle translation systems score the same BLEU (32.40 and 32.38, not significantly different). On a contrastive test built from held-out films, which asks whether a system keeps the T or V form of "you" consistent across sentences, one is at chance (50.0%) and the other is right in 81.6% of cases. A documented instance, on a pragmatic distinction, of two realizations equal under a standard probe family and different under an independently built one. An ML paper that neither record holds. | Active |
| [CASE-071](../../record/cases.d/CASE-071.md) v2 | Kovacs, Deutsch and Freitag's metric bias in MBR decoding: translations a metric selects, that the metric and its neighbours rate better and human raters do not | Minimum Bayes risk and quality-estimation decoding pick, from 128 sampled translations, the one a neural metric scores best. That metric and other neural metrics rate the picks significantly better than greedy decoding; human MQM raters find them no better, and for four system and language-pair combinations significantly worse. [CASE-041](../../record/cases.d/CASE-041.md)'s result in text, by selection rather than training. The distinction lost is accuracy, not a pragmatic one; a stipulated English-to-Russian ты/вы variant states the design that would make it pragmatic. | Active |
| [CASE-072](../../record/cases.d/CASE-072.md) | Hafez's "Shirazi Turk" in English: one ungendered beloved rendered as a maid, as God and as a boy | The opening of Hafez's ghazal 3 names a beloved, "that Shirazi Turk", whose sex and nature the Persian leaves open. English forces a choice at the first possessive. Jones (1771) and Bell (1897) make the beloved a maid, Clarke (1891) makes it God, and Davis (2012) makes it a boy, and Davis says in print that his choice is arbitrary. A documented instance of the half of a change of cover in which the rendering decides what the source left open. The disagreement on the source is among translators and scholars; no reader agreement has been measured. | Active |
| [CASE-073](../../record/cases.d/CASE-073.md) | The Kia Provo: one concept-car name, a blank coinage to the motor press and taken as honouring the IRA in Northern Ireland | In March 2013 Kia showed a concept car at Geneva named the Provo, "from the word 'provoke'". The motor press found no meaning in the name; in Northern Ireland, where "Provo" names a member of the Provisional IRA, six MPs said it "has caused deep offence". Kia said it meant no offence, and withdrew the name for the UK and Ireland. One name, the speaker's intention fixed and declared, a new audience, and an unintended reading taken up and acted on: the accidental, verbal variant of [CASE-064](../../record/cases.d/CASE-064.md), illustrating [CLAIM-224](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-224.md). | Active |
| [CASE-074](../../record/cases.d/CASE-074.md) v4 | Twitch Plays Pokémon in democracy mode, with the running tally shown while the votes come in | The crowd of [CASE-075](../../record/cases.d/CASE-075.md) in democracy mode. Each move is the button that collects the most votes in a short window, and the running tally is shown while the votes arrive, so the current majority can sway the votes still coming. The owner judges it a better demonstration of a population with drift-diffusion decision dynamics than anarchy mode. Showing the tally adds feedback, and feedback moves the dynamics toward winner-take-all. | Active |
| [CASE-075](../../record/cases.d/CASE-075.md) v2 | Twitch Plays Pokémon in anarchy mode: one avatar steered through the map by the running aggregate of a crowd's inputs | In February 2014 a stream let everyone in its chat play one copy of Pokémon Red at once, with every typed button press executed in turn. The owner offers its navigation as a working approximation of an agent made of a population whose decisions follow drift-diffusion dynamics. The crowd's direction reorganizes continuously and is not switched. The record holds no source for the case yet. | Active |
| [CASE-076](../../record/cases.d/CASE-076.md) | Twin pattern-keepers: two systems keep the same pattern, one by its own responses and one through an external controller, and only removing the controller tells them apart | A10 §3.3's paired design, the case behind [QUESTION-086](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-086.md). Matching behaviour and persistence, different organization. Removing the controller destroys one system's pattern and not the other's. A10 then made it harder: the internal regulator at one scale is supported by other processes at another, so which side of the boundary the controller sits on depends on where the boundary is drawn. | Active |
| [CASE-077](../../record/cases.d/CASE-077.md) v2 | The reciprocal pair: two processes that grant influence only to each other under recursive delegation, so that each one's authority returns to it through the other | A2 §5's counterexample to the ban on self-grants: "Suppose D1 endorses D2, and D2 endorses D1." Under g = (1 − η)b + ηEᵀg, each operator's authority comes partly from its own grant returned after two hops. It came back in A6 as authority that "may circulate". Used to show that a ban on self-grants does not make authority independent of one's own grants. | Active |
| [CASE-078](../../record/cases.d/CASE-078.md) v3 | Who keeps placing on r/place: the proposed survival analysis of the released placement logs, testing whether participants return while their art survives and whether unaffiliated placement cools | Proposed by the owner on 2026-10-13, not run. On the released 2017, 2022 and 2023 logs, model each participant's hazard of stopping, with the survival of the art they placed into as a time-varying covariate, and estimate the rate of placements that fit no artwork over the event. The main threat is reverse causality: persistent defenders keep their art alive. So the design leans on destruction the participant did not cause, such as the 2022 moderator wipes and large raids. It is the test named in [CLAIM-274](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-274.md)'s defeater. | Active |
| [CASE-079](../../record/cases.d/CASE-079.md) v4 | r/place: a shared pixel canvas on which each Reddit user could place one pixel at a time under a cooldown, and communities built, defended and overwrote images, coordinating through the canvas itself as well as off it | The owner proposed it at U8 of the 2026-10-13 exchange: "every user can modify a single pixel once, and different subreddits coordinated to present certain images or even warred amongst each other over regions of the canvas". Reddit ran it in 2017, 2022 and 2023, and released the 2022 placement log. A8 read it as the exchange's two canvases made visible: the pixels as the meaning canvas, the plans, templates and alliances off the canvas as the influence canvas. First time the thing turns up in the record. The event and the data are documented. The readings of them are proposals. | Active |
| [CASE-080](../../record/cases.d/CASE-080.md) v2 | The skeptic who delegates: a critic that cannot overturn an emerging consensus gives its authority to a process that can generate alternatives | A3 §6's worked example. Four processes: an integrator and a decision- maker push toward a decision, a skeptic disagrees, and a generator can translate neglected evidence into new hypotheses. The skeptic does not try to win. It grants the generator influence, whose hypotheses change the decision landscape. Used to show delegation as a route to one's goals, and dissent made useful by a role. | Active |
| [CASE-081](../../record/cases.d/CASE-081.md) v2 | Self-maintenance learned for a designer's reward: a system that maintains itself because it was trained to, and later revises the policy and keeps maintaining itself without further instruction | A10 §6.1's boundary case, "a system that learns to maintain itself only because an external designer rewards that behavior", with its question whether the maintenance becomes endogenous "once the system can revise the policy and maintain the associated governance without continued external instruction". It is the case [CLAIM-288](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-288.md) is about, and the place where that objection could be answered. | Active |
| [CASE-082](../../record/cases.d/CASE-082.md) v2 | The sufficient posterior without its history: a Bayesian agent that keeps only its current posterior updates as well as one that keeps everything, and cannot say how it got there | A6's fourth case, which A6 called "probably the most important counterexample in the set". It grounds the redundancy half of [CLAIM-300](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-300.md) and one of [CLAIM-299](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-299.md)'s separations. A6's reserve case, revising the model class, is where it stops holding, and [CASE-087](../../record/cases.d/CASE-087.md) is that case. | Active |
| [CASE-083](../../record/cases.d/CASE-083.md) | A pseudorandom generator's output against uniform strings: almost disjoint in total variation, indistinguishable to every efficient test if the generator is secure | A15 §2's witness, which A15 recommended filing "with its cryptographic assumption explicitly stated". It grounds [CLAIM-302](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-302.md) and, by the record's own reduction, [CLAIM-304](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-304.md). Checked against Goldreich (note of 2026-10-15): the definitions match, and the separation needs the generator only for samplability. | Active |
| [CASE-084](../../record/cases.d/CASE-084.md) v2 | The perfectly accountable false theory: a community whose record traces every commitment and whose vocabulary leaves out the right explanation | A6's third case. Accountability without correctness, one of [CLAIM-299](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-299.md)'s three separations, and A6's "direct companion to [CLAIM-181](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-181.md) and [CLAIM-190](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-190.md)". | Active |
| [CASE-085](../../record/cases.d/CASE-085.md) v2 | The medication in the ambulance: a drug the EMT knew was stocked could not be found during a resuscitation, the paramedic changed the request, and afterwards said "If I can't find it, it isn't there" | The owner's own experience as an EMT-B on an ALS unit, told at U16. It illustrates [CLAIM-296](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-296.md) and is the source of [TERM-055](../../record/terms.d/TERM-055.md). Stipulated, not documented: the event is real, and the only source is the owner's retrospective account, which a reader cannot check. | Active |
| [CASE-086](../../record/cases.d/CASE-086.md) v2 | The retracted finding and its descendants: an experiment fails to replicate years after three conclusions were built on it | A7's sixth case, filed at the owner's U7 as an illustration and not a test ("one or two of those might be handy to keep on hand too"). Tracing descendants of a defeated premise, which [CLAIM-295](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-295.md) says a record of dependencies supports. | Active |
| [CASE-087](../../record/cases.d/CASE-087.md) | The discredited instrument and the second population: two pairs of readings with the same mean that the mean cannot tell apart once one instrument is discredited or a mixture is considered | A10 §2's paired construction, which A10 recommended filing ("That single paired construction makes the dependence of sufficiency on the task and model very easy to see"). It grounds the relativity half of [CLAIM-300](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-300.md). | Active |
| [CASE-088](../../record/cases.d/CASE-088.md) | The repeated objection and the argument nobody reuses: filing pays for itself after a few recurrences in one case, and never in the other | A11's CASE B and CASE A, the arithmetic of [CLAIM-303](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-303.md). | Active |
| [CASE-089](../../record/cases.d/CASE-089.md) v2 | The compensating evidence problem: two records with the same support graph in which discounting one support leaves odds of 20:1 or of 1.1:1 | A6's second case. It grounds [CLAIM-293](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-293.md): the same edges E₁ → H ← E₂, different Bayes factors for E₂, different revisions when E₁ fails. | Active |
| [CASE-090](../../record/cases.d/CASE-090.md) v2 | The rehabilitated hypothesis: an objection that displaced a hypothesis turns out to rest on a faulty measurement, and the hypothesis can be reopened because its defeat was recorded | A7's seventh case, the companion to [CASE-086](../../record/cases.d/CASE-086.md) and filed with it. Reopening an alternative when its defeater is defeated. A12's "defeat of a defeater" is the same case and is held here, as A12 itself proposed. | Active |
| [CASE-091](../../record/cases.d/CASE-091.md) v2 | A memory ablation for revision: synthetic inquiries with known ground truth, run under six kinds of persistent memory, with information and token budget controlled separately | A6's fifth case, refined from A3, A4 §4 and A5. The design [QUESTION-108](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-108.md) asks for. Not run. | Active |
| [CASE-092](../../record/cases.d/CASE-092.md) v5 | A minimal coupled meaning–influence model: two hypotheses, two retrieval specialists, a fixed balanced corpus, queries set by the dominant interpretation and conferral updated from apparent retrieval success | A22 §7 and A23 §XI, a simulation not yet built. It is how [QUESTION-100](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-100.md) would be answered and [CLAIM-297](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-297.md) tested, and what [CLAIM-278](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-278.md) asks of the operator framework: a prediction a simpler model does not make. | Active |
| [CASE-093](../../record/cases.d/CASE-093.md) v2 | Same marginal, different updates: two joint distributions over a hypothesis and a condition that agree on the hypothesis and disagree on how to revise it | A6's first case, made exact at A8. It grounds [CLAIM-294](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-294.md). | Active |
| [CASE-094](../../record/cases.d/CASE-094.md) | The LEGO bin: a brick owned in one copy and buried among hundreds of similar ones may as well not be owned, a black brick among white ones needs no bin of its own, and every brick can be found by going through the whole collection | The owner's own parable, told on 2026-07-02 and kept verbatim in Mathematics of Meaning, where the owner paired it with the EMT case this record holds as [CASE-085](../../record/cases.d/CASE-085.md). It is the second time that case's pattern has turned up, so it gets a code. It adds three things the EMT case lacks: similarity to binmates as the cause of the failure, copy count and frequency of use as what decides whether a bin is worth making, and exhaustive search as the reason presence is never in doubt. Stipulated: the collection is real, and its facts are taken on the owner's account. | Active |
| [CASE-095](../../record/cases.d/CASE-095.md) | Two builders sorting one collection: the brick that one bins as red and two-wide and the other as a curved connector has a bin that respects both, and what the builders cannot share is an arrangement, not a description | The example the owner's LEGO monograph (Mathematics of Meaning, "The Lego Parable") uses for contextuality, rebuilt. As descriptions the two sortings always share a joint refinement, the product bin, as [MOM-CLM-137](https://github.com/dmarx/mathematics-of-meaning/blob/main/record/claims.d/CLM-137.md) shows, so they illustrate the non-contextual case of [CLAIM-037](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-037.md) and [CLAIM-131](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-131.md). As arrangements, with a fixed number of bins, they compete, and which wins depends on the queries made and on what stands out within a bin. Fictional: the builders and the collection are invented. | Active |
| [CASE-096](../../record/cases.d/CASE-096.md) | The smoothie: milk makes it better for a while and then worse, unless there is more of everything else | The owner's line, the prompt the smoothie note in Part III of the LEGO monograph answers: "if I put milk in my smoothie, it gets More Good for a while until it gets Less Good, unless there's more of all the other stuff." The note's first model reads it right: a preference over the milk fraction, single-peaked, kept by scaling everything together. Its second reading, in signal detection terms, has the direction wrong ([CLAIM-309](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-309.md)). Stipulated: the preference is the owner's, taken on the owner's word. | Active |
| [CASE-097](../../record/cases.d/CASE-097.md) v2 | The monograph's castle topic, persistent across refinement by construction, persists as no eigenmode: in its rank-2 and rank-3 co-occurrence matrices the eigenvector nearest the castle topic changes rank and turns through about 51°, and every eigenvector mixes signs | Appendix H of Part III of the owner's LEGO monograph builds two co-occurrence matrices from nonnegative topics. One has topics for spaceship and castle; the other splits spaceship into wings and engines and keeps castle unchanged. The monograph reads castle's survival as a stable eigenmode and calls that archetype persistence. Computed here: the topics overlap (ship·castle = 0.21), so no eigenvector is the castle topic. The eigenvector closest to it is the second mode at rank 2 (cosine 0.873) and the first at rank 3 (0.926), about 51° apart. Persistence of a generating factor does not show as persistence of an eigenvector, which is what [CLAIM-148](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-148.md)'s identifiability limits predict. | Active |
| [CASE-098](../../record/cases.d/CASE-098.md) v5 | A long rivalry between two teams outlives every player, owner and supporter who first carried it: new fans inherit the antagonism, its history and its practices before they know why the game matters | From U2 of the exchange of 2026-10-16 on sports fandom (the owner's list of subcases). A2's reading: a mature rivalry is "a reproduced intercollective relation", carried by families, media and fan culture, that can survive the replacement of everyone on both sides. Stipulated: the record has no study of a particular rivalry. It shows a relation with its own continuity; it does not show that the relation is a system, an agent, or constitutive of either fandom. | Active |
| [CASE-099](../../record/cases.d/CASE-099.md) v3 | The two-hypothesis coupled meaning–influence model linearized at its symmetric state: a meaning bias m and an authority bias a, each stable alone, coupled by authority shaping interpretation (β) and interpretation shaping conferral (γ) | A14 §2 of the exchange of 2026-10-16. Near the symmetric state of the two-hypothesis, two-specialist model, (m, a) evolves by a 2×2 matrix J = [[α, β], [γ, δ]] plus noise. With nonnegative entries and α, δ < 1, the symmetric state is unstable exactly when βγ > (1 − α)(1 − δ), and the unstable direction mixes meaning and authority. Checked here. A construction about a linearization, not a fitted or simulated result. | Active |
| [CASE-100](../../record/cases.d/CASE-100.md) v2 | A fan redub of an X-Men cartoon, circulated online in 2006, put "I'm the Juggernaut, bitch!" into X-Men: The Last Stand three months later | The owner's case at U7 of the exchange of 2026-10-16, offered as a fandom "making something happen". Two fans made the parody; many people spread it for their own reasons; the film's makers put its line in at a reshoot. A7: an effect on an outside institution with no collective intention and no procedure behind it. Documented as to the sequence; the route by which the parody reached the decision, and whether the spreaders were X-Men fans or the internet at large, is not. | Active |
| [CASE-101](../../record/cases.d/CASE-101.md) v5 | Three simulated populations with the same turnover and the same aggregate statistics: one whose members respond alike to a common input, one that transmits its norms to its replacements, and one held together by an institution that selects and trains its members | Proposed three times in the exchange of 2026-10-16 (A11 §9, A12 §7 and A13 §5), as the place where criteria of individuality should disagree: Krakauer et al.'s temporal measures, Barnett and Seth's dynamical independence, Hoel's effective information and the block criterion of [CLAIM-157](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-157.md). The common-input arm is Bourrat's "reconstructed entity" ([LIT-005](../../record/literature.d/LIT-005.md)), which the record already holds and the exchange did not cite. Not run. | Active |
| [CASE-102](../../record/cases.d/CASE-102.md) v2 | A college program stays good, or bad, at a sport for decades while its whole roster turns over every four or five years | The owner's case, U3 of the exchange of 2026-10-16: "the team composition experiences extremely fast churn, and yet in spite of this real world ship-of-theseus situation, teams often maintain standing on similar timescales as professional teams (if not longer)". A3: it separates the persistence of a collective's identity from the persistence of its capacities, and the second needs a mechanism that regenerates them in each new population. Stipulated: the record holds no data on program persistence. The proposed test is residual persistence of performance across seasons with no player overlap. | Active |
| [CASE-103](../../record/cases.d/CASE-103.md) v3 | A fanbase keeps a reputation, for hostility or loyalty or knowledge, after the behaviour that earned it has changed, and outsiders and members both act on it | From U2 of the exchange of 2026-10-16. A2 separated three things that vary independently: many members have the trait; others believe the fanbase has it; that belief shapes how the fanbase behaves. A reputation belongs to a fanbase through its relations with audiences, and it can enter the process that produces its own evidence. It is a case for regulative attribution ([THEORY-137](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-137.md), [QUESTION-049](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-049.md)) beyond mental states. Stipulated; it shows no particular reputation was self-fulfilling. | Active |
| [CASE-104](../../record/cases.d/CASE-104.md) v3 | A fanbase is said to hate its owner or general manager, though it has no procedure for forming a view, some fans approve of the person, and nobody has counted | From U2 of the exchange of 2026-10-16. A2 separated four things the sentence could report: a statistical aggregate of dislike, a shared emotional orientation, a norm of opposition that a loyal fan is expected to meet, and a corporate judgment such as a supporters' trust demanding resignation. A fanbase can have the second and third without the fourth, and a vocal minority can set a public stance most members privately reject. The case for [QUESTION-119](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-119.md). Stipulated. | Active |
| [CASE-105](../../record/cases.d/CASE-105.md) | The Washington Senators: the original club became the Minnesota Twins in 1961, an expansion club took its name in Washington and became the Texas Rangers in 1972, and the Montreal Expos became the Washington Nationals in 2005, so franchise, city and supporters each have a different successor | The owner's subcase at U2 of the exchange of 2026-10-16, "the team formerly known as the washington senators". A2 corrected the framing: the name belonged to two franchises, and the city's present team descends from a third. A league can designate the successor franchise; it cannot designate the successor fandom. The lineages are documented; how supporters actually distributed their loyalties is not, and the case does not show that a fandom's identity branched. | Active |
| [CASE-106](../../record/cases.d/CASE-106.md) v2 | The two-hypothesis linearization with authority conferred by scores on committed predictions: when the shared meaning bends the specialists' predictions with weight κ and the reading of the evidence with weight κ′, the coupling of interpretation to conferral is γ = 4ηs(κ′ − κ̄) | Built for [QUESTION-121](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-121.md)'s formal question: is scoring operators on predictions committed before the evidence equivalent to Regime B? The construction keeps [CASE-099](../../record/cases.d/CASE-099.md)'s two-hypothesis linearization and replaces the score with squared error between each specialist's committed prediction and the evidence as read. The coupling γ then has the sign of κ′ − κ̄: corrective when the interpretation bends the predictions more than the reading of the evidence, null when equally, capturing when less. Checked symbolically, and the stability conditions against 200,000 random draws. Fictional by construction. | Active |
| [CASE-107](../../record/cases.d/CASE-107.md) v3 | Locked-in syndrome: consciousness preserved with anarthria and quadriplegia; in the classic form, vertical eye movement and blinking remain and carry communication, and in the completely locked-in state no motor channel remains | The owner's case, U2 of the exchange of 2026-10-16: "a lucid human who struggles with locked-in syndrome". It separates lucid judgement from limb and speech action. In the classic form it does not separate judgement from action as active inference defines it, since the eye movements it spares are that theory's model action. The completely locked-in state is the real test, and there any evidence of lucidity has to come through a channel that is itself an action. Visceromotor regulation and Damasio's internal 'as if body loop' survive paralysis, so the case never removes every contingency between intention and outcome. | Active |
| [CASE-108](../../record/cases.d/CASE-108.md) v4 | Robust governance under internal compromise: four governance architectures over cooperative, honestly dissenting and adversarial specialists, scored on attack success and on what the safeguards cost, with a variant that compromises the evaluators | A41's proposed study, from the exchange of 2026-10-17, on the owner's U41 question about insider threats. A multitask learner with three kinds of specialist (cooperative, honest but dissenting, adversarial) is run under unrestricted, reliability-weighted, role-limited and adaptive governance, and scored on lost legitimate capability, suppressed honest minorities, cost of safeguards and repair of governance after failure, as well as on attack success. Its sharpest variant compromises the processes that evaluate the others' competence, which tests [CLAIM-342](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-342.md)'s independent scoring. Federated learning is the natural testbed. Not run. | Active |
| [CASE-109](../../record/cases.d/CASE-109.md) v2 | Two people with the same demanding commitment, the same output and the same effort in the work, who differ in how their intrapersonal conflict is settled: one escalates it to deliberate override, the other's processes accommodate one another | The owner's case, U45 of the exchange of 2026-10-17, worked by A45 as Person A ("repeated escalation") and Person B ("local coordination") on one writing project. Both exert real effort and produce work of equal quality; only the route by which conflict is settled differs. It is the variation [CASE-013](../../record/cases.d/CASE-013.md) and [CASE-051](../../record/cases.d/CASE-051.md) lack: there one writer's effort varies across afternoons, here effort in the work is held high in both. It is used for [CLAIM-358](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-358.md). Stipulated, so it shows what the claim distinguishes, not that people differ this way. | Active |
| [CASE-110](../../record/cases.d/CASE-110.md) | Two members, one total: a coarse-graining by total output preserves an institution's performance, its survival and every prediction it makes, and still merges a state in which a member works within the agreed cap with one in which that member works far past it | A22's Construction C of the exchange of 2026-10-17, "Institutional performance versus constituent capability", which A22 proposed and did not write out, from A21 §5 and §16; written out here in its smallest form. Two members share twelve hours of work. Total output decides the institution's performance and its survival, so the total is sufficient for every institutional judgment, and the policies (6, 6) and (10, 2) have the same total. A member's claim not to work past an agreed cap of eight hours tells them apart, so by [CLAIM-350](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-350.md) no judgment on the total recovers it. Fictional by construction; it shows a possibility, not how often institutions are like this. | Active |
| [CASE-111](../../record/cases.d/CASE-111.md) v2 | A fold in boundary maintenance: in ḃ = μb + κb² − b³ with cooperative repair (κ > 0), an unmaintained and a maintained state are both stable for −κ²/4 < μ < 0, so maintenance lost past the fold is not restored by returning the parameter, and recovery slows to zero as the fold nears | A7 §4 of the exchange of 2026-10-17, after the owner's "flesh this out further": a normal form for the activity b that maintains a boundary, in which repair processes reinforce one another. Its equilibria, the bistable range, the hysteresis loop and the slowing of recovery at the fold are derived and rechecked here. It shows a capacity to maintain, and not only a state, being lost and not coming back when conditions do, which is [CLAIM-099](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-099.md)'s distinction in a model. A7's own caveat: "This is a proposed minimal model, not an empirical fit." [THEORY-136](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-136.md) and [THEORY-214](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-214.md) say why its early-warning consequence is weak evidence. Fictional by construction. | Active |
| [CASE-112](../../record/cases.d/CASE-112.md) v4 | Bistable authority with a tilt: in ẇ = μw − w³ + h, with w the balance of authority between two regimes and h a higher-order volition favouring one, for μ > 0 the operative regime changes only when \|h\| passes 2μ^{3/2}/(3√3), so a sincere and efficacious higher-order volition can move the balance without moving the regime, and which regime operates depends on the path of h | A8 §4 of the exchange of 2026-10-17, restated as A12's Proposition 3 and kept in A15. The saddle-node threshold and the hysteresis are derived and rechecked here. A8: "It's a countermodel showing why endorsement strength, effective governance, and behavioral transition can come apart." A8's "endorsement" is the reflective attitude, not the record's [TERM-053](../../record/terms.d/TERM-053.md) sense, so h is called a higher-order volition. It models authority between regimes; it does not revive will as an attractor ([TERM-003](../../record/terms.d/TERM-003.md), Superseded). Fictional by construction. | Active |
| [CASE-113](../../record/cases.d/CASE-113.md) v3 | A deep network under conventional training: competing losses, an external optimizer and a scheduler, with three architectures that move governance inward and crossed interventions that locate it | The owner's application case, U30–U31 of the exchange of 2026-10-17: a deep network during training, taken apart with the record's individuation and volition frameworks. A network is trained on tasks with conflicting gradients by an optimizer with its own state and a scheduler. A31's Systems A, B and C move the regulation of conflict from the optimizer into the learner by stages, and its crossed interventions say where a regulatory condition sits. Stipulated: the outcomes of the interventions are not known, except the optimizer arm in [THEORY-253](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-253.md)'s solvable model. | Active |
| [CASE-114](../../record/cases.d/CASE-114.md) v7 | The imagined owner | A27's Experiment C, of the exchange of 2026-10-17: two computational systems with equivalent distributed control and similar behaviour, one with a self-model that represents the actual division of labour, the other with one that represents a single centralized inner agent. If the second systematically misattributes control, that is a concrete model of illusory authorship, located in the attribution. Proposed, not run, and it tests attribution of control, not phenomenal properties. | Active |
| [CASE-115](../../record/cases.d/CASE-115.md) v2 | Two incompatible quadratic demands on one shared parameter, L₁ = (θ − 1)² and L₂ = (θ + 1)², frustrated at θ = 0 for every update budget, and the split of θ into task-routed θ_A and θ_B that makes both satisfiable | A33 §2 and §5, A35 §3.4 and A38 §4 of the exchange of 2026-10-17. Two demands on one scalar parameter: at θ = 0 the equal-weight gradient vanishes while each loss is 1, no update lowers both, and the local residual is Φ_r = ε for every budget r. A38 splits θ into θ_A and θ_B with the task context routed, and both demands are met at (1, −1). Used for the zero aggregate gradient, the residual, relief judged on the original demands, and differentiation. A38's caveat is kept: the split is "a division of functional jurisdiction", and it "does not automatically mean that two new autonomous individuals have formed". Fictional by construction. | Active |
| [CASE-116](../../record/cases.d/CASE-116.md) v2 | Three finite-state systems that dissociate slowness, member regeneration and transmission: a slow flip register no member reads (second eigenvalue 0.98), a register members read and rewrite that forgets fast (second eigenvalue 0.2, nonzero intervention effect), and a rule passed through an archive between two episodes that never run together | A3 and A4 of the exchange of 2026-10-17, after the owner's "continue" and "show me". Arm A is slow and not endogenous; arm B is endogenous and not slow; arm C transmits a rule with no continuous operation. The eigenvalues and intervention effects are rechecked here. Arm A shows what [CLAIM-327](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-327.md) and [CLAIM-331](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-331.md) already say in words, that slowness is not sufficient; arm B is the new direction, that slowness is not necessary, and it holds only for weak maintenance, in A4's words. Arm C is [CLAIM-287](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-287.md)'s dormant coalition and does not settle identity. Fictional by construction. | Active |
| [CASE-117](../../record/cases.d/CASE-117.md) v7 | Emergent specialization and recovery in a population of adaptive operators: identically parameterized operators whose contributions, influence and training opportunities co-evolve over a changing task distribution, compared across fixed and learned features, uniform, grant-based and contribution-based influence, coupled and decoupled training exposure, and imposed and endogenous diversity | The study proposed in the synthesis the owner relayed on 2026-10-18 for [QUESTION-083](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-083.md) and the questions filed with it. Operators differ only by initialization and experience. It measures whether stable niches emerge, whether specialists recover after long irrelevance, and whether the organization adapts after losing a dominant specialist. The decisive comparison holds the final diversity of operator usage roughly fixed while changing the mechanism that produced it. Ordinary mixture-of-experts routing is the baseline. Even positive results would not show self-constitution ([CLAIM-288](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-288.md)). | Active |
| [CASE-118](../../record/cases.d/CASE-118.md) v4 | Two competing components with interaction matrix A = [[1, ρ], [ρ, 1]]: with equal growth rates their invasion growth is 1 − ρ, the smallest eigenvalue of the Gram matrix, and with growth rates 1 ± δ the same matrix supports coexistence only when \|δ\| < (1 − ρ)/(1 + ρ), so feature geometry alone does not fix coexistence | From the synthesis the owner relayed on 2026-10-18, checked. The two-component Lotka–Volterra system with symmetric overlap ρ: invasion growth 1 − ρ equals λ_min(A), and κ(A) = (1 + ρ)/(1 − ρ), so overlap weakens recovery and ill-conditions the features together. Holding A fixed and setting r = (1 + δ, 1 − δ) gives the coexistence equilibrium n* = (1/(1 + ρ) ± δ/(1 − ρ)), feasible only when \|δ\| < (1 − ρ)/(1 + ρ). The same representation geometry supports coexistence under one supply and exclusion under another. | Active |
| [CASE-119](../../record/cases.d/CASE-119.md) v3 | Three demands each requiring one coordinate of an outcome to equal 1, with attainable outcomes (1,1,0), (1,0,1) and (0,1,1): every pair is jointly satisfiable and the triple is not, and a new composite policy that can also produce (1,1,1) relieves the frustration by enlarging the attainable set, with the acceptance sets unchanged and no demand suppressed | From the synthesis the owner relayed on 2026-10-18. A compatibility complex with all three edges and no filled triangle, the minimal structurally frustrated collection of [CLAIM-347](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-347.md) with three members. When the organization acquires a composite operation that can produce (1,1,1), ℛ(𝒪) ⊊ ℛ(𝒪′) and the complex gains the triangle: relief in [CLAIM-347](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-347.md)'s sense, by changing what is attainable, not by weakening a demand. The construction says such relief is possible; whether specialization produces it is [QUESTION-133](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-133.md). | Active |
| [CASE-120](../../record/cases.d/CASE-120.md) v3 | Six units with uniform coupling, A = αI + β(11ᵀ − I): their sum is exactly dynamically independent at every coupling tried, while a single unit and the sum of three are not, and no grouping of the units is a weakly coupled block | The n-unit form of A5's two-unit example in the exchange of 2026-10-19 on coarse-to-fine processes. The sum closes exactly; no proper group of units is a block. It is the closure-without-block cell of A5's two-by-two table, and the system [QUESTION-218](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-218.md) would test for compensation and entrainment. Fictional by construction. | Active |
| [CASE-121](../../record/cases.d/CASE-121.md) | One melody from a 1951 gospel record to a 2005 Katrina protest song: "I've Got a Savior", "I Got a Woman", "Gold Digger" and "George Bush Doesn't Care About Black People", each link a different relation, the message turned at every step | Boyle's chain in The Public Domain, ch. 6 ([LIT-1301](../../record/literature.d/LIT-1301.md)). The Bailey Gospel Singers' "I've Got a Savior" (c. 1951), reworded by Ray Charles as "I Got a Woman" (1955), re-sung by Jamie Foxx as the licensed hook of Kanye West's "Gold Digger" (2005), looped from that recording by The Legendary K.O. for a Katrina protest song. The melody and phrase pattern carry through; the object of praise turns from a saviour to a generous woman, a mercenary one, then a president's indifference. Each link is a different relation: rewording, licensed re-performance, sampling. The received origin story was wrong, and the melody found the real source. | Active |
| [CASE-122](../../record/cases.d/CASE-122.md) | Warhol's Orange Prince, licensed to Condé Nast in 2016 from a 1981 Goldsmith photograph: the Supreme Court judged the use by its purpose and the dissent by its meaning, and the same cover came out a substitute on one test and a new work on the other | Andy Warhol Foundation v. Goldsmith (2023), read in [NOTE-1019](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-1019.md). The experts agreed Warhol's silkscreen changed the photograph's meaning, from Prince's private identity to his public image. The majority held that this did not matter for the use at issue: a magazine cover illustrating a story about Prince serves substantially the photograph's own purpose, and new meaning counts only as evidence of a different purpose. The dissent measured what the copier added. One rendering, two measures of what it changed, two verdicts. | Active |
| [CASE-123](../../record/cases.d/CASE-123.md) v3 | The regulator and two accounts: a formally independent regulator weighs two parties' accounts of a risk, one party comes to supply more of the evidence it reads and the experts it trusts, and the credibility that party gains decides whose evidence is read next; with variants for a one-time perturbation, a press as the evaluator, and an objective that gives one property no weight | A20 §2 of the exchange of 2026-10-20, on the owner's U20 ("reflexive control and regulatory capture"), with A21 §3's press variant on the owner's U21 and A26 §4's evaluative variant. [CASE-099](../../record/cases.d/CASE-099.md)'s linearization with institutional roles: m is the lean of the regulator's reading, a the standing of the party it favours. A20: "This is an application of the formal model, not an established empirical account of any particular regulatory agency." No regulator, outlet or party is meant. | Active |
