| [CASE-124](../../record/cases.d/CASE-124.md) v4 | A maintained legibility lean as a third state of the linearized meaning–authority model: authority moves the lean r of the categories through which evidence is read, and r feeds interpretation or authority, with its own persistence λ | [CASE-099](../../record/cases.d/CASE-099.md)'s two-variable linearization, meaning bias m and authority bias a, given a third state r: the lean of a maintained legibility regime, the categories, reporting requirements and evidence channels through which evidence becomes readable. Authority moves r (ε), r moves interpretation (ν) or authority (μ), and r persists at rate λ. Two witnesses, both checked: one in which the two-variable loop is stable and the three-variable one is not, and one in which the system is stable but r holds a lean long after m and a would have decayed without it. | Active |
| [CASE-125](../../record/cases.d/CASE-125.md) v3 | A hidden governance bit: an output that is fresh fair noise whatever a fixed hidden bit K is, until an intervention h makes the output read K, so the output alone is exactly informationally closed under ordinary operation and keeps nothing about the response to h | A16 §3–4 of the exchange of 2026-10-20, from A15 §2's deterministic form, and A17's Proposition 1. A fair hidden bit K, fixed in time; under ordinary operation the output Y is fresh fair noise; under the intervention h = 1 it reads K. The output is exactly closed, and with h = 1 drawn with probability ρ its deficiency for the response to h is ρ bits, no better than a constant. It is the first instance with parameters of [CLAIM-122](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-122.md)'s supervisory test, which the exchange of 2026-10-17 gave only in words. Fictional by construction; K is a mechanism parameter, not an agent's governance. | Active |
| [CASE-126](../../record/cases.d/CASE-126.md) | Represented, authorized, self-authorized and self-maintained: three one-bit systems that agree while the interpretation is 0 and part under an intervention that sets it to 1, and two two-constraint systems with the same constant trajectory, one closed and one reset from outside, that part under one perturbation | A10's two constructions in the exchange on experience and interpretation, re-run here by script. Neither depends on parameters. The first separates whether an interpretation is acted on and who decides that it is. The second separates whether the conditions of acting on it are kept up by the organization or by something outside it. Fictional by construction, with production relations stipulated, not shown. | Active |
| [CASE-127](../../record/cases.d/CASE-127.md) v2 | United 173 and Crew Resource Management: a crew whose concern about the fuel did not reach the captain as a demand to act, and the training that followed, which asked for participative captains and assertive crews | The owner's case at U25 of the exchange of 2026-10-22: "the history underlying the evolution of Crew Resource Management". A cockpit had the information and lacked the organization to make it govern. The reform changed the organization of authority, not its holder. Not shown: that CRM caused the later fall in accidents, which the case does not test. | Active |
| [CASE-128](../../record/cases.d/CASE-128.md) v3 | The voluntary delegation: a person gives a friend the password to a distracting application during a calm period and is refused it later under temptation, while another person lives under the same restriction imposed without consent | A18's CASE C in the exchange of 2026-10-22, from the owner's request for cases on quiet subordinate processes (U18). A restriction on a constituent process can be an exercise of self-governance, not its defeat, so a non-domination test that counts every override as domination is too simple. [CLAIM-362](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-362.md) already admits warranted override; the case adds that the warrant can be the agent's own earlier authorization, and that it is read from history, not present behaviour. Not shown: where a legitimate precommitment ends and an unaccountable one begins. | Active |
| [CASE-129](../../record/cases.d/CASE-129.md) v2 | The alarm that learned and the alarm that was disabled: two systems that once warned too often are both quiet in ordinary operation, and only one responds when a new hazard appears | A18's CASE B in the exchange of 2026-10-22, written at the owner's request for cases on subordinate processes "quiet because they've been conditioned never to object" (U18). A quiet signal may be calibration or suppression, and ordinary operation cannot tell which. Not shown: how often either occurs in persons or institutions. | Active |
| [CASE-130](../../record/cases.d/CASE-130.md) v3 | Gracie jiu-jitsu against luta livre: two Brazilian grappling traditions whose contests were fought over the rules as well as under them, and whose matches placed them in a racial hierarchy as well as ranking them by results | The owner's case at U35 of the exchange of 2026-10-22: contemporaneous, geographically related schools of martial arts vying for regional, ideological, demographic and physical dominance. A35 and A36 took the Gracie–luta livre rivalry, from Penglase ([LIT-1391](../../record/literature.d/LIT-1391.md)). It is the record's first martial-arts material. Not shown: which tradition was technically better, which the case is evidence against asking in general; and the historical particulars, which are the replies' summary of Penglase, unread when the case was filed and read since (NOTE-1092). | Active |
| [CASE-131](../../record/cases.d/CASE-131.md) v2 | The graph that asserted an inference nobody made: filing one claim as supporting another made the record's link fixer write that the other rested on it, and the record answered by adding a relation | The record's own history, as A18 of the exchange of 2026-10-22 read it at the owner's prompting (U18): "the pre-existing representation didn't merely omit information; it positively misrepresented an inference". It is a move the grammar could not hold, caught and answered by amending the grammar. Not shown: that such moves are always caught. | Active |
| [CASE-132](../../record/cases.d/CASE-132.md) | One complaint, two taxonomies: a report of an unsafe practice is correctly classified both by equipment and by workers' obligations, the intake procedure needs one primary class, and the class chosen decides whether the report reaches a body that can change working conditions | A18's CASE O in the exchange of 2026-10-22, from the owner's request for cases on whether "some apparent conceptual disputes are really disputes about the allocation of limited representational resources" (U18). Two true classifications compete not for truth but for uptake. Not shown: when routing becomes an allocation of authority, which the case asks. | Active |
| [CASE-133](../../record/cases.d/CASE-133.md) v2 | American party realignment: each major party kept its name and organizational continuity across the twentieth century while its coalition and its stance on civil rights changed, and the change began in state parties, unions and constituencies before it reached national leaders | The owner's case at U29 and U30 of the exchange of 2026-10-22, in the replies' corrected form. A party is a carrier; its coalition and character can change while the carrier persists, and an orientation can move between carriers. Not shown: that the parties swapped perspectives, or that any present party continues a historical one's character, which are the owner's U29 framings the replies declined. | Active |
| [CASE-134](../../record/cases.d/CASE-134.md) v3 | Northstar's commander handoff: twelve hours into an incident the commander goes off shift and hands command to a colleague, and the response continues without disruption and with no one continuous through it | A22's variant of Northstar ([CASE-023](../../record/cases.d/CASE-023.md)), from the exchange of 2026-10-22, where the owner asked to revisit the engineering organization responding to a critical incident. Of the six variants A22 proposed it is the one the record did not already hold. It separates continuity of an organization from continuity of any person in it. Not shown: anything about whether the response is experienced. | Active |
| [CASE-135](../../record/cases.d/CASE-135.md) v2 | The Brookhollow Apartments fire, Wichita, 2023: a mayday after a stair roof collapses on two companies opens a second emergency inside a search for a trapped resident, and an independent review finds an unorganized rescue effort without finding that it changed the outcome | The owner's class of case, a fireground mayday inside an escalating response organized under the National Incident Management System (U23 of the exchange of 2026-10-22); A23 and A24's instance. It is the record's first real multi-regime emergency, beside the fictional Northstar ([CASE-023](../../record/cases.d/CASE-023.md)). It shows two regimes, rescue of a member and the original mission, needing to be held at once, and a review that recommends change. Not shown: that the department's safety culture changed, or what the review's recommendations were, since the report was not read. | Active |
| [CASE-136](../../record/cases.d/CASE-136.md) v5 | The doorway effect: memory for an object carried through a doorway into another room is worse than after walking as far within one, returning to the first room does not restore it, and outside the original laboratory's set-up the effect has not always appeared | The owner's case at U37 of the branch of the exchange of 2026-10-22, offered as one the record's account of perspective and grounding may at least partly explain. Reported studies, read first from their abstracts and since in full: an effect of event boundaries on accessibility, attributed to updating or to interference among event models, and replications that fail. Not shown: that the effect is robust, or that the record's account explains it better than event cognition already does. | Active |
| [CASE-137](../../record/cases.d/CASE-137.md) v3 | Clive Wearing: a musician left by encephalitis with almost no memory of his past and none of the last minutes, who still plays, sight-reads and conducts, keeps his attachment to his wife, and writes over and over that he has just become conscious for the first time | The owner's case, proposed at U26 of the exchange of 2026-10-22 ("if we haven't already examined the case of Clive Wearing, that might be another good addition"). The record had no amnesia case. It separates skill and short-horizon organization, which persist, from autobiographical continuity and experienced ownership of one's past, which do not. Not shown by it: that the unity of his present experience is an illusion, or that his denials of past consciousness are evidence of its absence. | Active |
| [CASE-138](../../record/cases.d/CASE-138.md) v3 | Challenger to Columbia: NASA corrected its launch decisions and its safety organization after 1986, and the Columbia board found in 2003 that related organizational failure mechanisms had recurred, while its lessons-learned system was little used in between | The owner's case at U25 of the exchange of 2026-10-22: NASA's mechanisms for documenting and learning from mistakes, with Challenger as the classic example. The record already reads the Columbia board's account ([THEORY-140](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-140.md), from [LIT-734](../../record/literature.d/LIT-734.md)). This case files the history around it: a warning whose standing changed as it moved, a correction made in the rules, and a capacity not maintained. Not shown: why the correction decayed, which [THEORY-140](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-140.md) explains and this case only displays. | Active |
| [CASE-139](../../record/cases.d/CASE-139.md) v2 | Chinese martial traditions through the Cultural Revolution: practices restricted, teachers persecuted and teaching driven underground, then after 1978 a reconstruction of Shaolin martial arts through heritage policy and tourism in which local practitioners and authorized institutions dispute who carries the tradition | The owner's case at U37 of the branch of the exchange of 2026-10-22: the "(various) perspectival interruption(s) caused by the Chinese Cultural Revolution", near the record's martial-arts material ([CASE-130](../../record/cases.d/CASE-130.md)). A case family, not one example: skills that survived in bodies while teaching became dangerous; practices that persisted under a different organization of authority; survival in marginal carriers; and a reconstruction that became a source of authenticity. Not shown: what was continuous and what was rebuilt, which is the question it raises. | Active |
| [CASE-140](../../record/cases.d/CASE-140.md) v2 | The attributed self: two people with similar dispositions hear the same negative characterization, one told it comes from a few critics and the other believing it speaks for nearly everyone who matters to them, so that the words are fixed and only their attributed breadth differs | A39's proposed test of the imagined audience, in the exchange of 2026-10-22, filed at the owner's request. It holds the content of a judgement fixed and varies only the social authority the hearer attributes to it. A39: "These cases distinguish the content of a judgment from the social authority attributed to it." As stipulated it predicts nothing; run with real people, it would test [CLAIM-415](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-415.md). | Active |
| [CASE-141](../../record/cases.d/CASE-141.md) v2 | The label that confirms itself: a pupil wrongly labelled disruptive is watched more closely, ordinary conduct is read as misconduct, the pupil's frustration produces real misconduct, and the label looks justified in hindsight | A39's second reading of "I am whatever you say I am", in the exchange of 2026-10-22, filed at the owner's request with its variant [CASE-142](../../record/cases.d/CASE-142.md). An attribution can produce the conduct that later seems to justify it, through the institution's response to the attribution, not through the pupil's coming to believe it. Not shown: how often labels work this way; A39: "That's a possible self-fulfilling process, not a universal consequence of stigmatizing labels." | Active |
| [CASE-142](../../record/cases.d/CASE-142.md) v2 | The label confirmed by resistance: a critic calls someone hostile, the person answers the characterization angrily, and the critic cites the anger as proof, so the judgement is confirmed by the response of someone who rejects it | A39's third reading of "I am whatever you say I am", in the exchange of 2026-10-22, filed at the owner's request as a variant of [CASE-141](../../record/cases.d/CASE-141.md). What differs is the route: the judgement is rejected, not absorbed, and the rejection is what confirms it. A39: "Here an interpretive regime partially manufactures the evidence it uses to justify itself." Not shown: that resistance always confirms; the person can also answer the judgement without the answer being read as evidence for it. | Active |
| [CASE-143](../../record/cases.d/CASE-143.md) v2 | Franklin's borrowed book: Benjamin Franklin, opposed by a new member of the Pennsylvania Assembly when he stood again as its clerk, asked to borrow a rare book from him rather than courting him, returned it with thanks, and found the man civil and later a friend | The owner's U1 of the exchange of 2026-10-22, "Ben Franklin Effect Explained", read at U2 as a "perspectival invitation". Documented in Franklin's Autobiography ([LIT-1402](../../record/literature.d/LIT-1402.md); the passage was read in the Project Gutenberg text on 2026-10-23), and the origin of the "Ben Franklin effect", that doing someone a favour can increase liking for them, tested by Jecker and Landy ([LIT-1419](../../record/literature.d/LIT-1419.md)). The standing is the anecdote's, as Franklin told it; the reading is the exchange's. | Active |
| [CASE-144](../../record/cases.d/CASE-144.md) v5 | The evaluator that stops tracking: two populations of specialists with identical capabilities and task streams, one scored against independent outcomes and one by an evaluator that weights evidence agreeing with its previously favoured specialists, after a shift that makes a marginalized specialist uniquely competent | Proposed in the exchange of 2026-10-23, "Framework Overview Request", at A5 (section 5) with A4's "corrigibility trap", after the owner asked which experiments could "kill multiple birds with one stone" (U4) and which questions simulation could answer (U5). One design bears on [CASE-092](../../record/cases.d/CASE-092.md), [CASE-108](../../record/cases.d/CASE-108.md) and [CASE-117](../../record/cases.d/CASE-117.md) at once, and separates four causes of a failure to adapt. A5's central question: "Under what conditions does an organization become unable to recognize or act upon evidence that its own organization of interpretation and authority has rendered inconsequential?" Not run. | Active |
| [CASE-145](../../record/cases.d/CASE-145.md) v5 | Perspective without collective agency: populations given the same structured norms, categories or interpretive training, some interacting and some acquiring similar dispositions independently, with and without any mechanism for collective decision or coordinated intervention | Proposed at A4 of the exchange of 2026-10-23, "Framework Overview Request", which called it "the most philosophically important new experiment". It asks whether a distributed organizational perspective can be identified without attributing a further collective agent, [CLAIM-418](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-418.md)'s separation tested, and bears on [QUESTION-260](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-260.md). A4's condition: it helps only if it says in advance what distinguishes a shared perspective from correlated responses or learned norms. Not run. | Active |
| [CASE-146](../../record/cases.d/CASE-146.md) v3 | Two friendships across a group line: in one, the friend becomes an exception and the person's view of the friend's group is unchanged; in the other, what happens with the friend changes how the person takes other members of the group, though the friendships are alike in warmth and in what each learns | From the exchange of 2026-10-22, "Ben Franklin Effect Explained", A4 and A5, on the owner's U4 about intergroup contact. A4: "A prejudiced person can befriend a member of a targeted group while preserving their prejudice toward everyone else. They simply classify the friend as an exception." A5 drew the two outcomes as "Exception formation" and "Category reorganization". Held fixed: the friendship and its favourable information. Varied: whether the friend's conduct is allowed to bear on the category. | Active |
| [CASE-147](../../record/cases.d/CASE-147.md) v3 | Conversation against argument: in Kalla and Broockman's field experiments, canvassers who exchanged personal narratives nonjudgmentally reduced exclusionary attitudes durably, while arguments alone did not produce comparable effects | Cited at A4 of the exchange of 2026-10-22, "Ben Franklin Effect Explained", as "suggestive evidence" for the prediction that cooperative enactment changes attitudes more durably than information. Kalla and Broockman (2020, [LIT-1407](../../record/literature.d/LIT-1407.md)), registered unread; the description is the exchange's and the abstract's, unchecked against the paper. The standing is the study's, not the reading's. | Active |
| [CASE-148](../../record/cases.d/CASE-148.md) v3 | Perspective transplantation: two populations matched in behaviour exchange different combinations of learned interpretive rules, specialist parameters, training histories, institutional rules and shared memory, and are then set where the transferred parts disagree with their new surroundings | Proposed at A4 of the exchange of 2026-10-23, "Framework Overview Request", as an extension of [CASE-101](../../record/cases.d/CASE-101.md). It asks which components carry a perspective's characteristic organization when it moves between carriers: [CLAIM-417](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-417.md)'s realization, character and lineage, tested by transfer. A4: "No transfer experiment can settle numerical identity merely by demonstrating causal continuity." Not run. | Active |
| [CASE-149](../../record/cases.d/CASE-149.md) | Dancing to the same or different music, seen or unseen: in dyads facing each other, shared music and visual contact were removed independently, and each synchronized different movements of the pair | Bigand et al. (2024), [LIT-1424](../../record/literature.d/LIT-1424.md), read in [NOTE-1093](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-1093.md). Filed from the owner's exchange "Framework Overview Request" of 2026-10-23 as an external result meeting part of the aim of the proposed study [CASE-045](../../record/cases.d/CASE-045.md). In 35 dyads, shared music and visual contact were varied in a 2 × 2 design; each raised synchrony in different movement components, with no interaction, and the timing of the synchrony could not tell the two apart. Not shown: the direction of coupling, a perturbation response, or anything about trained partner dancing with touch. | Active |
| [CASE-150](../../record/cases.d/CASE-150.md) v2 | Economic alignment and political alignment: Kleinman, Liu and Redding find, using China's integration into the world economy and falling air-transport costs as sources of variation, that countries whose welfare becomes more dependent on each other's productivity become more politically aligned | Cited at A6 of the exchange of 2026-10-22, "Ben Franklin Effect Explained", after the owner's U6 on trade and peace, as "a paper that makes the connection almost literally". Kleinman, Liu and Redding (2024, [LIT-1416](../../record/literature.d/LIT-1416.md)), registered unread; the description is the exchange's and the abstract's. The standing is the study's, not the reading's. | Active |
| [CASE-151](../../record/cases.d/CASE-151.md) | Wells Fargo's Community Bank, 2002–2016: unauthorized and unwanted accounts that the board's own investigation traced to the sales model, the performance management that enforced it and a decentralized structure, while signals of the harm reached people who could not act on them | The Wells Fargo sales-practices episode, as reported by the bank's independent directors (2017; [LIT-1425](../../record/literature.d/LIT-1425.md), read in [NOTE-1095](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-1095.md)). The report finds the root cause in the Community Bank's sales culture and performance management, sustained by a decentralized structure that deferred to the business line. It finds that leadership blamed individual employees, and that the signals of harm were held by investigators, regional leaders and control functions without authority to act, or filtered before reaching the board. It also names and sanctions individuals. Not shown: intent, the absence of individual culpability, or anything beyond the company's own account of itself. | Active |
| [CASE-152](../../record/cases.d/CASE-152.md) v2 | Absent attempt or unresponsive receiver: with Y ∼ N(ab, 1), a the strength of an attempted influence and b the receiver's responsiveness, the settings (a, b) = (0, 1) and (1, 0) give the same response distribution; K(a, b) = a²b²/2, the Fisher matrix has rank at most one and vanishes at the origin, and the real log canonical threshold is ½ with multiplicity 2, not the regular d/2 = 1, while changing b with a held fixed separates the two | The construction from the exchange of 2026-10-24 (workbench entry of 2026-10-24, U27), checked when filed. A two-parameter response model in which the observation depends on a product, the smallest singular model with a communicative reading. It shows a missing response failing to say whether nothing was attempted or nothing was taken up. | Active |
| [CASE-153](../../record/cases.d/CASE-153.md) v5 | The self-protecting sentry: a hidden world, safe or dangerous, a sentry process that favours sheltering and can block inspection with weight κg, an investigator that favours inspecting, which reveals the state with 90% accuracy at a small cost; train in danger, switch to safety, and compare a Bayesian baseline, a habit baseline, the learned-authority model, an independent audit and a protected investigator, then cross governance feedback, evidence restriction and correction rights in a 2×2×2 design | Proposed in the exchange of 2026-10-23 (workbench entry of 2026-10-24, U17), after the owner asked to "brainstorm some minimal toy models we could apply to simulate our abstraction for experiments. there may also be some readily available options that fit our constraints already." The smallest model of the protective lock-in in [CLAIM-446](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-446.md), built on [CASE-092](../../record/cases.d/CASE-092.md) and [CASE-099](../../record/cases.d/CASE-099.md). At U18 the owner pointed to [CASE-124](../../record/cases.d/CASE-124.md) as the better core, and the exchange moved there ([CASE-156](../../record/cases.d/CASE-156.md)). Not run. | Active |
| [CASE-154](../../record/cases.d/CASE-154.md) | One representation, two tasks, one bounded observer: with X₁, X₂ independent fair bits and an observer that reads one coordinate and cannot compute XOR, the raw pair U = (X₁, X₂) gives error 50% on Q_A = X₁ ⊕ X₂ and 0% on Q_B = X₁, and the compressed V = X₁ ⊕ X₂ gives 0% and 50%; a legibility regime can enable one perspective's distinction while disabling another's, with no task-independent sense in which it is better | From the exchange of 2026-10-24 (workbench entry of 2026-10-24, U22), after the owner said "let's work on those connections": a deduction from [CLAIM-199](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-199.md)'s XOR example, checked when filed. It ties bounded legibility to competing concerns: a representation that serves one task can destroy another's for the same observer, so a failure of one perspective under a legibility regime may be a resource limit and not an exclusion. | Active |
| [CASE-155](../../record/cases.d/CASE-155.md) v4 | Authority as a replicator population: for a distribution π of control over processes with mean qualities q, the expected Maynard-Cross update is π_a q_a / v, Bayes' rule when q is each process's likelihood; the Maynard dynamic is the Taylor dynamic slowed by 1/v, so the two share their orbits; mean quality climbs at the rate Var_π(q); and when a process's score rises with its own share, ℓ′ = ℓ + h + c(2π − 1) has an unstable threshold π* = (1 − h/c)/2, above which a process the evidence disfavours captures the organization | Filed at the owner's direction, 2026-10-24, after asking whether the tools of Soma et al. ([THEORY-899](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-899.md)) or related work could formalize the record's abstract governance dynamics. Four results, each checked when filed, two by script. It reads authority as the population vector of a replicator dynamic. Evidence-tracking authority is then Bayesian, and authority-dependent scoring gives lock-in with an explicit correction threshold. | Active |
| [CASE-156](../../record/cases.d/CASE-156.md) v2 | A legibility filter as the ν of the meaning–authority–legibility model: in a safe world where 15% of raw cues are alarms, an admission rule P(admit \| y, r) = σ(c + χry) with c = 0 and χr = 2 admits 88.1% of alarms and 11.9% of reassurances, so 56.6% of the admitted record is alarms with the world and the sensors unchanged; at symmetry the mean admitted evidence is χσ′(c)r to first order, so ν = η_m χ σ′(c); and r_t = λᵗr₀ + ε Σ λ^(t−1−j) a_j is an exponentially weighted history of authority | From the exchange of 2026-10-24 (workbench entry of 2026-10-24, U18–U19). The owner: "i think we already have a three variable system somewhere, where the third component is described as legibility", then "flesh this out further". A microfoundation for one of [CASE-124](../../record/cases.d/CASE-124.md)'s abstract couplings: selective admission of evidence, checked when filed. It gives r an observational semantics and reads r as the memory of past authority. Selection-aware inference is the control that the pathology has to beat. | Active |
| [CASE-157](../../record/cases.d/CASE-157.md) v3 | Boosting the vote: in the owner's 2014 model, voters of fixed accuracy drawn from Beta(2, 2) vote in a weight-averaged election, and every voter's weight is multiplied by 1 + f when their own vote was right and 1 − f when not, with a fraction replaced each round at weight 1; with full-information feedback the weighted result rises from 0.50 toward 0.98 as f grows, and churn only costs in a world that never changes; the model escapes the hive mind's absorbing states because every voter is scored, heeded or not, and it assumes away the capture its author predicted | Filed at the owner's direction, 2026-10-24, after the owner suggested taking inspiration from the experiments in dmarx/BoostTheVote. A thought-experiment written in R in 2014, read at commit b5bca8f and re-run here through a Python port (scratch/formal/boost_the_vote.py). It is the full-information counterpart of the replicator construction. What it shows, what it assumes, and the three-axis extension it suggests are recorded, and the extension was run on 2026-10-25. | Active |
| [CASE-158](../../record/cases.d/CASE-158.md) v4 | Audited governance against a contextual router after a reversal of expertise: specialist denoisers with frozen, separately trained operators propose for one shared latent at each step, and spatial grants are revised from independent held-out probes of every proposal, available only from the next episode, against a fixed allocation, a conformity-scored allocation and a contextual mixture-of-experts router; the question is whether governance does anything a router does not, and under full-information audits the record predicts it cannot | Proposed at A46 and reported at A47 of the exchange of 2026-10-24, continued, after the owner's "close the loop". It is [CASE-144](../../record/cases.d/CASE-144.md)'s design, run without citing it, with competence frozen so that only the allocation learns, and with a conventional router added as the rival that [CLAIM-278](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-278.md) requires. A47 reported that the router won on every seed. Its results come from a sandbox the share does not carry, and the record has not run it. What would make it informative is one arm per failure of [QUESTION-271](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-271.md). | Active |
| [CASE-159](../../record/cases.d/CASE-159.md) v2 | Authority geometry and response geometry: when an organization's response is the authority-weighted mixture p_π(y \| c) = Σ πᵢ pᵢ(y \| c), the Fisher metric of its responses is the pullback JᵀD⁻¹J of the categorical (Shahshahani) metric on the shares π and never exceeds it; it vanishes along any redistribution between processes that respond alike under the probes used, so two specialists who both answer yes with probability 0.8 in routine contexts carry no information about their shares there, while a rare context in which they answer 0.9 and 0.1 carries 0.64/(p(1 − p)) per observation, p = 0.1 + 0.8π | From the continuation of the exchange of 2026-10-24 (U28–U31), where it appears in four replies (A28 §3, A29's second priority, A30 §6A and A31's probe diagnostic); checked when filed. The categorical metric on authority shares is the one in which the replicator is a gradient flow ([CLAIM-444](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-444.md)). What an evaluator sees is the response mixture, whose Fisher metric is that metric pulled back through the processes' response profiles. Redistribution between processes that agree on the probes is invisible to them, and a probe on which they disagree restores it. The mixture counterpart of [CASE-152](../../record/cases.d/CASE-152.md)'s product model. | Active |
| [CASE-160](../../record/cases.d/CASE-160.md) v3 | Interacting accumulators on an influence network: if each perspective's continuous support for one of two interpretations follows dxᵢ = [dᵢ − λxᵢ + κ Σⱼ Wᵢⱼxⱼ]dτ + noise, with W irreducible and row-stochastic and ℓ its left eigenvector, then ℓᵀ(κW − λI) = (κ − λ)ℓᵀ, so the ℓ-weighted support L = ℓ·x is exactly an Ornstein–Uhlenbeck process with drift ℓ·d, rate κ − λ and variance ℓᵀΣℓ, and a drift-diffusion process at κ = λ; with tanh coupling and member noise the transition is at κ_c = λ/E[sech² ε], not at κ = λ | From the continuation of the exchange of 2026-10-24 (U28–U47). A36 derived it as the answer to the owner's U34 hypothesis, and A37, A42, A43 and A44 restated it as the population simulator's unit test. The case formalizes [CASE-074](../../record/cases.d/CASE-074.md)'s crowd as a linear network of accumulators: the hidden tally is the balanced case, the visible tally positive feedback. The closure is [THEORY-733](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-733.md)'s, the variance [THEORY-321](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-321.md)'s influence concentration and the balance [THEORY-068](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-068.md)'s, a combination no source makes. Checked when filed, and the reply's nonlinear transition corrected. | Active |
| [CASE-161](../../record/cases.d/CASE-161.md) v3 | Reciprocally produced, externally reset and feedback-only governing rules in a 32-member population: with ordinary trajectories identical, reciprocal production repairs the loss of one rule and not of both and fails after producer outages of 36 updates for audit and 40 for admission, after an audit-rule pulse the next audit integrity is 1.00, 0.18 and 0.00 in the three arrangements, and a self-dependent production graph does not regenerate a zeroed rule | Versions 1.6 and 1.7 of the Perspective Population Lab, dmarx/PerspectiveLab@7e2edc2. Two governing rules, the integrity of audit and of evidence admission, are kept up in three ways in the same 32-member population. Ordinary operation cannot tell the three apart, and loss, repair and producer interventions do. It carries [CASE-126](../../record/cases.d/CASE-126.md)'s second construction to a stochastic population with a different production law: here reciprocal production repairs a single loss, where the case's closed pair collapses. | Active |
| [CASE-162](../../record/cases.d/CASE-162.md) v2 | Capture and selectively admitted evidence in two lab realizations: in a four-variable model of authority, record and competence the equilibrium fold under self-scoring is at c ≈ 0.4247 while the declared history is trapped from c ≈ 0.4469, and in a 32-member event-level population, admission of cues that depends on the record reverses the sign of the admitted evidence and leaves the worse alternative in charge in 41 of 64 worlds, which exact inverse-propensity weighting brings to 0 of 64 below full selectivity | Versions 1.1 to 1.3 of the Perspective Population Lab, now held as dmarx/PerspectiveLab@7e2edc2. v1.2 is a deterministic four-variable model whose analytic fold and whose trapping of a declared history come apart. v1.3 is a separately coded event-level population in which the filtering of [CASE-156](../../record/cases.d/CASE-156.md) runs on sampled cues, and a known-propensity evaluator undoes it while admission stays positive. v1.1 adds capture and selective audits to [CASE-158](../../record/cases.d/CASE-158.md)'s design, and the router still wins. The sign reversal is [CASE-156](../../record/cases.d/CASE-156.md)'s; what is new is two running realizations, with their code. | Active |
| [CASE-163](../../record/cases.d/CASE-163.md) v2 | Identifying how governing rules are maintained from diagnostic probes: a predictor fitted under reciprocal production fails under self-dependent production with histories matched, an audit pulse with a short producer interruption identifies which of four known mechanisms applies and predicts withheld interventions, and fitting decay and threshold as well helps after the parameters shift and hurts at nominal ones, within a known catalog and with every probe run on a counterfactual reset | Versions 2.0 to 2.2 of the Perspective Population Lab, dmarx/PerspectiveLab@9f82bfd, on the maintained population of [CASE-161](../../record/cases.d/CASE-161.md). v2.0 shows that a response model does not transport across a change of production law that ordinary histories do not reveal. v2.1 and v2.2 infer the mechanism, and then its parameters, from probes, and predict interventions never probed. Everything is relative to a catalog of four known mechanisms whose equations the forecaster is given, and every probe is a separate branch from the same starting state. | Proposed |
| [CASE-164](../../record/cases.d/CASE-164.md) | A 192-state organization on which one kernel gives several closure verdicts: the description by agents and phase is informationally closed on the intact support and fails global strong lumpability, its deficiency for the next pair of rule integrities under a declared intervention mixture is 0.55·H₂(1/3) ≈ 0.5051 bits and zero once the maintenance mode is kept, and finite-sample predictive gains misread both a positive and a zero information defect | v1.8 of the Perspective Population Lab, dmarx/PerspectiveLab@7e2edc2: a finite organization small enough to enumerate, on which the senses of closure of [TERM-068](../../record/terms.d/TERM-068.md) are computed exactly side by side. The maintenance mode plays [CASE-125](../../record/cases.d/CASE-125.md)'s hidden bit: the intact dynamics are the same in every mode, and five of six interventions part them. v1.9, at @9f82bfd, calibrates finite-sample predictive gains against the exact values. It recombines distinctions the record already holds and claims no new theorem. | Proposed |
| [CASE-165](../../record/cases.d/CASE-165.md) v7 | A dynamic beta–Bernoulli latent-feature model of an organization's members: a finite Indian buffet prior over who holds which interpretive features, a sticky logistic transition for holding them, activations, and team, communication and authorization layers above; at the proposed size of sixteen features it falls well short of the Indian buffet process it quotes, its persistence term does not hold rare features, and its outputs cannot tell a feature one member holds at constant activation from that member's private map, or features whose holdings and activations always coincide from their sum | The owner's T22 ("maybe we could benefit from a restaurant process model, like an indian buffet model"), developed at T23 ("flesh out our proposed generating probability model further") into a full generative specification for the Northstar firm, and carried to transformer training at T24. It is the candidate method of [QUESTION-276](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-276.md) in its most worked-out form. Its facts are its definition, and four of them are not what T23 says: at K = 16 the prior expects 6.83 features, not αH₁₂ = 9.31; a held rare feature is kept with probability 0.16 a day; and having, using and splitting features are confounded in the reconstruction. It shows what such a model would have to get right before its features could count for anything. | Active |
| [CASE-166](../../record/cases.d/CASE-166.md) v3 | Northstar as a multilevel simulation: twelve employees with fixed frames in four departments form overlapping working groups that keep records and can receive delegated authority; as reported, a working group with delegated authority ties a conventional router given the same information and authority, nearly all of its advantage over departmental silos comes from fast authorization, and keeping the incident team's records through a commander handoff improves the company's estimate of its own state about eightfold while barely changing what it does | The owner's idea at T18 of the exchange "Experimental Infrastructure Review": "maybe employees are "pre-perspectives" or sub-perspectives, which can reorganize amongst themselves to form the perspectives that influence the higher level organizational behavior". T19 designed it and T20 reported running it as "PerspectiveLab v2.11 — Northstar". It is Northstar ([CASE-023](../../record/cases.d/CASE-023.md)) with [CASE-134](../../record/cases.d/CASE-134.md)'s handoff as one arm, and it adds the contrast [CASE-134](../../record/cases.d/CASE-134.md) lacks: the team's records kept or erased. The page carries no files and the lab holds no v2.10 or v2.11, so every number is reported, not reproduced. Not shown: that any organizational perspective emerged, which T20 itself denies. | Proposed |
| [CASE-167](../../record/cases.d/CASE-167.md) v4 | The nitroplast: UCYN-A, a streamlined nitrogen-fixing cyanobacterium in a marine alga, whose division is coupled to the host's and which imports host-encoded proteins, reclassified as an early-stage organelle in 2024 | Candidatus Atelocyanobacterium thalassa (UCYN-A), a metabolically streamlined nitrogen-fixing cyanobacterium known as an endosymbiont of a marine alga, is integrated into the alga's cell architecture and organellar division and imports proteins encoded by the algal genome. Coale et al. call it an early-stage organelle, the nitroplast ([LIT-1562](../../record/literature.d/LIT-1562.md)). Used as the case where the line between endosymbiont and organelle is drawn by stated criteria. | Proposed |
| [CASE-168](../../record/cases.d/CASE-168.md) v4 | Monocercomonoides: a eukaryote that lost its mitochondrion entirely after the organelle's essential jobs were removed in steps, its Fe–S cluster assembly taken over by a bacterial-type SUF system that its relatives share and its glycine cleavage system lost, and kept canonical eukaryotic complexity | The oxymonad Monocercomonoides lacks all hallmark mitochondrial proteins; its mitochondrial iron–sulfur cluster pathway was replaced by a cytosolic SUF system taken from bacteria, and the loss was secondary ([LIT-1567](../../record/literature.d/LIT-1567.md)). Its genome otherwise shows canonical eukaryotic complexity ([LIT-1574](../../record/literature.d/LIT-1574.md)). Used as the negative control: loss of function dissolving an integrated compartment, not deepening dependence on it. | Proposed |
| [CASE-169](../../record/cases.d/CASE-169.md) v4 | The Chagossians, 1965 to now: a people expelled from the Chagos Archipelago between 1967 and 1973 for the Diego Garcia base, dispersed to Mauritius, the Seychelles and Britain, whose practices, kinship and political organization continued or arose in exile while sovereignty over the islands was settled between states | The owner's case, U1–U3 of the exchange of 2026-10-26, "About the Chagossians". Britain separated the archipelago from Mauritius in 1965, expelled its inhabitants by 1973 for a US base on Diego Garcia, and the islanders' descendants live dispersed across three countries. The ICJ advised in 2019 that decolonization was incomplete; a UK–Mauritius treaty of 2025 would cede sovereignty and keep the base. A case in which territorial community, cultural transmission, political agency and the claims of the displaced, ordinarily bundled, were pulled apart by identifiable decisions. Not shown: that the Chagossians are one persisting agent, or which practices survived; the sources are registered unread. | Active |
| [CASE-170](../../record/cases.d/CASE-170.md) v3 | The East Wing of the White House, 2025–2026: the wing demolished for a ballroom before the legal challenge to the project was decided, so that the remedy still available was to stop new construction, not to keep the old building | The owner's case, U7 of the exchange of 2026-10-26: "the destruction of the East Wing of the white house and the courts throwing out the legal challenge to it". The object of review was destroyed before review concluded, so preserving it left the set of remedies, and the project under way became part of the argument for continuing it. A7 corrected the owner's premise: as it reported, the challenge was not dismissed on the merits but its injunction was stayed. Not shown: that the demolition was unlawful, or that the security concerns were pretextual; the court history is the exchange's. | Active |
| [CASE-171](../../record/cases.d/CASE-171.md) v6 | Mealybugs, Tremblaya and Moranella: a bacterium inside a bacterium inside an insect, amino-acid pathways patched together from three genomes and genes transferred from other bacteria, and an inner symbiont replaced several times while the transferred genes stayed | In the mealybug Planococcus citri, Moranella lives inside Tremblaya. Essential amino-acid pathways need gene products from both bacteria and the insect ([LIT-1565](../../record/literature.d/LIT-1565.md)). At least 22 genes the insect acquired from other bacteria, none from Tremblaya, fill symbiont gaps ([LIT-1569](../../record/literature.d/LIT-1569.md)), and the inner symbiont has been replaced several times across mealybugs while those genes persisted ([LIT-1572](../../record/literature.d/LIT-1572.md)). Used as the case where function is distributed across nested boundaries and constituent lineages turn over. | Proposed |
| [CASE-172](../../record/cases.d/CASE-172.md) v3 | Federal science and education in the United States, 2025–2026: grants cancelled, frozen and in part reinstated, staff departures, advisory committees ended and research programs suspended, so that funding, organization and scientific capacity could be restored, or not, separately | The owner's case, U4 of the exchange of 2026-10-26: "the full breadth of the harms incurred by the actions of the DOGE project, and the trump administration's broader assault on the scientific research and education establishment". A4 drew ten dimensions of harm and the question it took as central: "Suppose a grant is canceled and later reinstated. What, precisely, has been restored?" A case of a resource restored without the organization it supported, and of capacity lost without output falling. Not shown: the size of any loss. Every figure is the exchange's, from reports this filing has not read. | Active |
| [CASE-173](../../record/cases.d/CASE-173.md) v5 | Aphids and Buchnera: an obligate nutritional symbiosis in which neither partner reproduces alone, and the symbiont's genome reduced to a seventh of E. coli's | Buchnera lives in aphid bacteriocytes and is passed from mother to egg. Its 641-kb genome keeps the pathways for the amino acids its host needs and has lost most else ([LIT-1596](../../record/literature.d/LIT-1596.md)); its genes evolve fast and A+T-biased under Muller's ratchet ([LIT-1573](../../record/literature.d/LIT-1573.md)). The record's reading of Wilson and Barker reports Godfrey-Smith placing the pair as both an organism and a Darwinian individual ([NOTE-114](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-114.md)). Used as the case of high mutual dependence and high reproductive cohesion reached through reduction, with no gene transfer to the host reported in the works it rests on. | Proposed |
| [CASE-174](../../record/cases.d/CASE-174.md) v5 | Paulinella chromatophora: a photosynthetic organelle in the making, from an endosymbiosis independent of the plastids', its genome reduced to about a third of its coding capacity, and hundreds of host proteins imported into it, most encoded by the host's own genes rather than transferred from the symbiont | The amoeba Paulinella chromatophora's chromatophores descend from a cyanobacterium taken up independently of the plastids, about 90 to 140 million years ago by the review read ([LIT-1560](../../record/literature.d/LIT-1560.md); [LIT-1557](../../record/literature.d/LIT-1557.md)'s older estimate is 60). Their genome has lost about two-thirds of its original coding capacity ([LIT-1560](../../record/literature.d/LIT-1560.md); against [LIT-1621](../../record/literature.d/LIT-1621.md)'s comparator it kept 26% of a free-living relative's genes) and lacks essential pathways ([LIT-1621](../../record/literature.d/LIT-1621.md)). Hundreds of proteins are imported into them, mostly encoded by the host's own genes; transfer from the symbiont played "only a circumstantial role" ([LIT-1560](../../record/literature.d/LIT-1560.md)). Used as the case where host control emerges by import. | Proposed |
| [CASE-175](../../record/cases.d/CASE-175.md) v4 | CECOT, 2025: people removed from the United States into Salvadoran custody despite court orders, the US saying it could not bring them back and El Salvador's president that he could not send them, until coordinated transfers out showed the two governments together could | The owner's case, U7 of the exchange of 2026-10-26: "the DOJ ignoring court orders not to deport people to CECOT, then claiming they didn't have the authority to bring them back, while CECOT claimed they didn't have the authority to send them back". Physical custody, the control that selected and could release, and the duty to correct were held in different places, and each government's statement of what it alone could not do was offered as if it settled what could be done. Not shown: whether any official was in contempt, or intent; the later proceedings are the exchange's. | Active |
| [CASE-176](../../record/cases.d/CASE-176.md) v2 | The United States Institute of Peace, 2025–2026: its board removed and its building entered by DOGE staff with police assistance before any court ruled, nearly all staff dismissed, a district court's judgment that the takeover was unlawful stayed on appeal, and the institution renamed while its legal identity continued | The owner's case, U5 and U6 of the exchange of 2026-10-26: "examine the case of the US Institute for Peace through the lens of our framework", then "the involvement of the DC metropolitan police and FBI, and the judge's concern regarding the potential for police instigated violence in their ruling on whether or not to grant a TRO". Name, building and legal entity continued; staff, programs, leadership and partner relations did not. And enforcement settled who held the building before any court settled who held the authority. Not shown: what was lost for good, or the legal outcome, which is pending. | Active |
| [CASE-177](../../record/cases.d/CASE-177.md) v3 | Hodgkinia in cicadas: one endosymbiont splitting into two to dozens of interdependent lineages, with near-complementary gene sets, idiosyncratic fragmentation in Magicicada, and hosts transmitting up to six times as many cells to keep every lineage | In some Tettigades cicadas, the endosymbiont Hodgkinia split into two cell lineages with almost perfectly complementary gene sets, the functionality of one ([LIT-1563](../../record/literature.d/LIT-1563.md)), and it has done so at least six times in the genus ([LIT-1586](../../record/literature.d/LIT-1586.md)). In Magicicada it fragmented into twenty or more genomic circles per species ([LIT-1612](../../record/literature.d/LIT-1612.md), [LIT-1602](../../record/literature.d/LIT-1602.md)). Hosts compensate by transmitting more cells to each egg ([LIT-1590](../../record/literature.d/LIT-1590.md)). Used as the case where dependence and the number of parts grow with no new collective function, and the host pays. | Proposed |
| [CASE-178](../../record/cases.d/CASE-178.md) v5 | How the record's grammar grows: after its scaffold, this record filed documents on all 32 days, added vocabulary terms on 18, fields or relations to schemes on 8, whole vocabularies on 3, schemes on 1 and a remote project on 1, so the owner's ordering of levels holds by frequency; but each refinement came in the same pull request as the documents that needed it and, nine times in ten, an ADR, so the levels are rarer events of one loop of work, not slower processes of their own, and the grammar's rates fell while filing rose | Run on 2026-10-27 at the owner's direction, after [CASE-180](../../record/cases.d/CASE-180.md): "some of the most important rates are when and how schema refinement happens", with the ordering documents, vocabularies, scheme fields, schemes, projects. scratch/formal/schema_rates.py reads every change to luria.yaml in this record, in luria's own and in strata-g's. The ordering holds by days with an event. How the refinement happens: on demand, by decision, and nested, the rarer level arriving with the commoner ones in one pull request. | Active |
| [CASE-179](../../record/cases.d/CASE-179.md) v6 | A proposed study: one network of locally described units in which physical coupling, the correspondence maps that say which local states agree, and the apparatus that revises those maps are manipulated independently, to find which of compatibility, attraction, effective boundary constraint, persistent individuation and reliable revision come apart, and how much each costs to establish | The experiment the owner's review of the pull request named as "the cleanest way to investigate the central claim that interacting processes can generate the conditions of their own further organization", put together from A22 §10 and A23 §XIII of the exchange of 2026-10-27. It has three independent manipulations: the physical coupling, the correspondence maps, and whether and how an apparatus may revise those maps. Its readouts are global compatibility, attraction, effective boundary laws, robustness under intervention, the cost of recovering the structure, and whether revision improves prediction on interventions the apparatus did not choose. Not run. | Active |
| [CASE-180](../../record/cases.d/CASE-180.md) v3 | The record's own rates: over its first fifty days, measured from git, an entry's prose is revised about once in 22 days once drafting is over, its status once in 128, an authored relation gained or lost once in 132 and an authored relation retracted once in about 600, while no relation type has been redeclared; the layers' event rates are ordered as the exchange guessed, but only after the first day, when all of them change together, and the grammar is slow to revise without being slow to grow | Run on 2026-10-27 at the owner's request ("run your measurements against the record"), for the rate reading A12 and A13 gave luria's layers and that [CLAIM-476](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-476.md) left untested. Every non-merge commit to this record, read by scratch/formal/record_rates.py at commit a357048. The ordering (prose, then standing, then relations, then the grammar's types) holds in aggregate rates, by one to two orders of magnitude. It does not hold in the first day after filing, or for every scheme, and the machine's validation runs at the rate of the edits it follows, not on a scale of its own. | Active |
| [CASE-181](../../record/cases.d/CASE-181.md) | Frankfurt's unwilling addict: a man who has the desire to take the drug and the desire to refrain, wants the desire to refrain to be his will, and is moved by the craving anyway | Frankfurt (1971), [LIT-692](../../record/literature.d/LIT-692.md), read in full in [NOTE-545](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-545.md), p. 12. Filed by the status review of 2026-10-27 ([ADR-057](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-057.md)). [CLAIM-047](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-047.md) is grounded in this case and not in Frankfurt's account of ownership ([THEORY-120](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-120.md)), which is Proposed. The case shows that a regime can be had, and even preferred at a higher order, without being effective. What it is taken to show about identification and ownership is [THEORY-120](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-120.md)'s business. | Active |
| [CASE-182](../../record/cases.d/CASE-182.md) v2 | Two operators under one update rule, grants moving toward observed outcomes: scored every round, the more accurate one holds the lead durably whatever the start; scored only when it acts, under sharp selection, whichever gained influence early keeps it | Filed by the fourth status review ([ADR-057](../../record/decisions.d/ADR-057.md)) for [CLAIM-277](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-277.md), whose two loops the record had stated and not constructed. Two operators share one rule, with accuracies 0.8 and 0.6. Each round one acts, chosen with probability softmax(β·E), and the grant moves toward observed outcomes, E ← E + η(outcome − E). Nothing marks either one as an authority. Scored every round, the more accurate one ends with the larger grant in 200 of 200 seeds, even after a head start for the other (E0 = 0.3 against 0.9). With η = 0.01 the lead never changes hands in the second half. Scored only when it acts, with that same head start, the worse one is never displaced in 200 of 200 seeds at β = 30 or 100. At β = 8 the better one recovers, after a median of 460 rounds. The scored control at β = 100 entrenches no one. Code: scratch/formal/trust_loop.py. | Active |
| [CASE-183](../../record/cases.d/CASE-183.md) v2 | The anchoring measures: inbound references per code and turns per filed entry, counted in the record's own grammar | The two measures the nucleating-argument line proposes for its claims, filed as a design so that an argument can start from them. Turns per filed entry would be set against the drift the workbench records ([CLAIM-186](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-186.md)). Both are counted in codes, relations and statuses. Inbound references per code were counted on 2026-11-06 (CLAIM-185's note of that date); turns per filed entry have not been computed. | Active |

CLAIM-115 and CLAIM-139 are Superseded, by CLAIM-220 and CLAIM-204, whose conditions are stated on CASE-055. The criterion is human judgements of fidelity or of shared kind. The theory's predictor is a measure of judged pragmatic observables. The rival is a text-similarity score. It has not been run, and [CASE-055](../../record/cases.d/CASE-055.md) is the design the record filed to repair it. | Active |
| [CASE-185](../../record/cases.d/CASE-185.md) v3 | A proposed study: direct and relay translations of one set of source utterances through three languages, to find whether agreement between the routes, the task-relative loss on each route, and the preservation of response to later framing come apart, and whether correspondences fitted between the communities' response structures on some items predict receivers' judgements on held-out items, translators and situations | Put together from A4 §6, A5 §5 and A6's synthesis of the exchange "Paper assessment" (2026-10-28 to 29), which proposed carrying [LIT-1820](../../record/literature.d/LIT-1820.md)'s comparison of alternative paths into [CASE-055](../../record/cases.d/CASE-055.md) as independent translation routes. Not run; every margin is left to a preregistration. | Active |

| [CASE-191](../../record/cases.d/CASE-191.md) v2 | A construction of self-formation: an agent's own frustration at two demands one shared output cannot meet drives production of a sensor and a differentiating channel that then maintain each other; past a threshold of discrepancy (D ≈ 0.645 at unit drive) the capacity forms, outlasts the conflict, meets later conflicts of the same class up to D ≈ 1.047 with no frustration, and is lost at damage 0.5; a capacity upheld from outside is told apart by knockouts afterwards, but one installed by an outside pulse and then self-maintained is told apart only by a knockout during formation | Built at the owner's request ("build the [CASE-189](../../record/cases.d/CASE-189.md) extension") to test [CLAIM-532](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-532.md), the positive account of self-formation filed from U33 of the exchange "Assess SDT Coverage". scratch/formal/self_formation.py. The wording is A's, sharpened by the reader; adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |

A cited [CLAIM-346](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-346.md) for the distinctions, but [CLAIM-346](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-346.md)'s three obstructions are a different set; only the structural arm matches one of them, feasibility, and that is what the case illustrates of it. The observational arm is [CLAIM-535](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-535.md) and the governance arm is [CLAIM-357](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-357.md). It makes Guida, Minutola and Presti's P4 ([LIT-1218](../../record/literature.d/LIT-1218.md), [NOTE-1353](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-1353.md)) a weighted influence network, the reduction their reading said would be the record's contribution, with leverage as conferral ([TERM-103](../../record/terms.d/TERM-103.md)). Made at the owner's review, which invited a case for an alternative reading. The construction is the filer's; the script is scratch/formal/r3_flexibility_propagation.py. | Proposed |
| [CASE-196](../../record/cases.d/CASE-196.md) v2 | Three species whose every pair coexists identically for every strength b of a three-way term that vanishes on pairs: above b = 0.96 a missing species cannot invade a resident pair, above b = 1.76 the triple cannot persist from any start, and between them it persists from some starts and not others, so whether the triangle of the compatibility complex is filled depends on whether compatibility is declared as robust permanence or as a stable positive equilibrium | A8's Proposal A, from the exchange "Organizational Nucleation and SLT" (2026-11-04), answering the owner's U8 request for CASEs the record lacks. [CASE-119](../../record/cases.d/CASE-119.md) stipulates a complex with every edge and no triangle. This one derives it from ecological dynamics, with every pair's dynamics identical at every b. A stated two results. The reader confirmed both by simulation, derived the two thresholds, and found a bistable band A did not mention, 0.96 < b < 1.76. In that band the complex depends on which notion of compatibility is declared. The wording is A's, sharpened by the reader; adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-197](../../record/cases.d/CASE-197.md) v3 | The toy model of superposition with six features in two dimensions, with each feature's reconstruction loss taken as a demand: at the 5-gon critical point five features are met at 0.0158 and the dropped one only at 1/3, at the 6-gon every feature is met at 0.0482, so the posterior's 5 → 6 transition strictly enlarges the compatibility complex for common tolerances from 0.0482 to 1/3, loses its five-feature face below 0.0482, and at the critical points changes no Betti number, only which features can be met at all | A5 of the exchange "Organizational Nucleation and SLT" (2026-11-04) proposed this experiment: "decompose the toy model's population loss into feature-specific losses, define meaningful satisfaction thresholds, and construct the compatibility complexes associated with neighborhoods of the different polygonal phases". The reader ran it at the 5-gon and 6-gon critical points and in sampled neighbourhoods of them. Whether the Bayesian transition counts as relief depends on the tolerance. Where it does, the change adds a vertex rather than filling a hole. The stipulated part is the choice of demands and of one common tolerance. The title's results are the critical points'; the neighbourhood ones are sampled. The construction is A's, run and sharpened by the reader; adopting it is the owner's call. | Active |
| [CASE-198](../../record/cases.d/CASE-198.md) v2 | An architecture-mediated transition: moving between two configurations by first switching to a staging architecture under which the path is cheaper, moving, then switching back, lowers the peak resource need from 1.3 to 1.0 | A's proposal, from the pasted synthesis on constraint architectures (pasted by the owner on 2026-11-04, continuing the exchange on emergency readiness whose share link could not be fetched), Part 4: the architecture-mediated transition (q_A,z0)→(q_A,z1)→(q_B,z1)→(q_B,z0), with A's states A and B and a staging C. A gives the sequence only; the numbers are the filer's. Under the home architecture the move costs 1.3 at its peak; switching to a staging architecture that is dearer to hold but flatter to cross, crossing, and switching back costs 1.0. The bottleneck of a fixed architecture is not the bottleneck once changing the architecture is a move. The construction is the filer's, from A's architecture-mediated transition; adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-199](../../record/cases.d/CASE-199.md) v2 | Three configurations, all mutually reachable and so isomorphic in the path groupoid, with one-way costs U to L 1, L to U 3, U to R 3 and R to U 1: the additive cheapest-cost distance is directed and graded, d(L, R) = 6 against d(R, L) = 2, each configuration's cost-to-target profile recovers it exactly, and under peak costs the same moves give other distances | A's toy, from the owner's paste of 2026-11-05 (fourth), A's "Toward a resource-sensitive Yoneda theory of individuation" §§2 and 3: an origami mechanism's unfolded state U and two folded states L and R, with d(U, L) = 1, d(L, U) = 3, d(U, R) = 3 and d(R, U) = 1, the cheapest moves between L and R passing through U. A's table, its budget-2 example and its recovery formula d(x, y) = sup_p [d(p, y) − d(p, x)] are checked by the filer with python3 -I on all nine pairs. The filer adds the same moves under peak costs, where the distances change and mutual reachability within a budget is transitive, against the additive case, where at budget 3 it is not, so the toy is an instance of [CLAIM-581](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-581.md)'s contrast with directed moves. The construction is the filer's, from A's toy; adopting it is the owner's call. | Active |
| [CASE-200](../../record/cases.d/CASE-200.md) v5 | A transition that a budget reaches only by carry-over: a per-period budget of 1.0 holds either configuration at 0.7, the transition needs 1.3, and rolling over the 0.3 left unspent reaches it, while a rule that cuts or expires unspent funds forecloses it | A's proposal, from the pasted synthesis on constraint architectures (pasted by the owner on 2026-11-04, continuing the exchange on emergency readiness whose share link could not be fetched), Part 4: the budget recursion B_{t+1}=B̄+(ρ−α)L_t ≥ 1.3, which with B̄ = 1.0, L = 0.3, ρ = 1 and α = 0 reaches the 1.3 the transition of A's example needs. A gives the recursion and the values; reading L as the balance left unspent, ρ as the share rolled over and α as the share by which the budget-setter cuts the next budget per unit unspent is the filer's. The 0.3 is slack against current output, and it is what makes the transition possible. A rule that expires it (ρ < 1) or a ratchet that cuts on it (α > 0) forecloses the transition. It ties A's construction to the ratchet and to the question of allocation by observed activity. The construction is the filer's, from A's budget recursion; adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-201](../../record/cases.d/CASE-201.md) | Fixed material, variable usable capacity: one sheet of area 8 folded into k accordion panels fits a stowage footprint of 2 only when k is at least 4, supplies the required functional area of 6 only when unfolded, and carries the required load only when k is at most 8, so packing, deployment and load capacity select different designs from the same inventory | A's proposal, from the pasted synthesis on constraint architectures (pasted by the owner on 2026-11-04, continuing the exchange on emergency readiness whose share link could not be fetched), Part 4: an inextensible sheet has a fixed amount of material, and folding changes its footprint, volume, stiffness and functional area, so "A fold pattern converts the same physical inventory into different usable capacities." A gives the constraints A_material(z) ≤ B_material, V_stowed(z) ≤ B_storage and P_deployed(q,z) ≥ P_required without numbers; the numbers, and the stipulated load rule, are the filer's. One sheet of area 8 meets the material budget in every configuration, meets the stowage budget only folded and the area requirement only unfolded, and the tightest packing fails the load requirement. A's heavy-rescue reading: a company standing by and dispatched has the same inventory in a different configuration. The construction is the filer's, from A's fixed-material resource model; adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-202](../../record/cases.d/CASE-202.md) v2 | A proposed study: two small constrained mechanisms that agree under a restricted family of probes and differ internally, probed at rising budgets, to find when they become distinguishable, what the distinguishing operations cost and whether the distinctions survive a change of architecture, with controls that separate finding a difference from making one | A's proposal, from the owner's paste of 2026-11-05 (latest), A's "Yoneda, individuation, and resource-bounded objects" §8: extend [CASE-179](../../record/cases.d/CASE-179.md), which manipulates coupling, correspondence maps and the apparatus that revises them, with "an explicit individuation-by-probing component". Two mechanisms that agree under restricted probes and differ internally are probed as the budget and the probe repertoire grow; the readouts are when they come apart, at what cost, and whether the distinctions survive architectural change; the controls separate "discovering a difference and creating a difference", and the probes are chosen as [CLAIM-322](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-322.md) requires. A's target result, identical static compatibility and low-budget profiles with different higher-budget reachability or maintenance, has a seed in [CLAIM-583](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-583.md)'s pair. The design is A's, with the seed and the readouts' tie to [CLAIM-574](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-574.md) the filer's; adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-203](../../record/cases.d/CASE-203.md) | The U.S. fire incident reporting guidance on cancelled dispatches, 2023: units assigned, arrived and turned back are recorded, while a call a department could not answer for lack of resources is not an incident at all, so the data omit the failures that most concern readiness | Filed at the owner's request on 2026-11-04, to account for what the reading of the U.S. Fire Administration's NFIRS coding guidance ([LIT-2084](../../record/literature.d/LIT-2084.md), read in [NOTE-1573](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-1573.md)) surfaced and the record lacked. The National Fire Incident Reporting System records, for each incident, every unit assigned, whether it arrived, and whether it was cancelled en route; but a call the department knew of and could not answer for lack of resources is not an NFIRS incident, and recording it in a separate database is recommended, not required. So the national incident data carry committed but unused capacity and leave out unmet demand. The record's second fire-service case, beside [CASE-135](../../record/cases.d/CASE-135.md). The wording is the filer's; adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-204](../../record/cases.d/CASE-204.md) | A positively curved origami vertex: its popped-up and popped-down states lie in components that no rigid, non-self-intersecting motion joins, so no budget reaches one from the other, and only widening the admissible moves reconnects them | Filed at the owner's request on 2026-11-05, to file "one or more CASEs for origami as a toy model of a resource bounded constraint configuration space". Berry, Lee-Trimble and Santangelo ([NOTE-1605](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-1605.md)) show that near the flat state a rigid vertex with small positive Gaussian curvature, faces forbidden to pass through each other, has its popped-up and popped-down nappes in disconnected components; Foschi, Hull and Ku ([NOTE-1606](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-1606.md)) find degree-4 modes disconnected whenever the sector angles do not sum to 2π. With no admissible path, the bottleneck budget is infinite for every resource and every budget. The authors report, anecdotally, that letting faces pass through each other reconnects the nappes; Jules et al. ([NOTE-1617](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-1617.md)) give a second instance, states reached only by a move outside the loading protocol. The case where no budget helps and only the admissible-move set does. A mechanical specimen; its organizational reading is an analogy. Adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-205](../../record/cases.d/CASE-205.md) | Twenty folding cells and three actuators: with each actuator driving a fixed group of cells, the configurations the actuators can command form a three-dimensional slice of the twenty-dimensional set the mechanism admits, and which profiles lie in it is fixed by the grouping, not the count | A's proposal, from the pasted synthesis on constraint architectures (pasted by the owner on 2026-11-04, continuing the exchange on emergency readiness whose share link could not be fetched), Part 4: "Suppose a structure contains twenty foldable cells, but only three independently driven actuators." A's model is q̇ = G(q)u, F(q)=0, ‖u‖ ≤ U_max, with m < n inputs. The grouping (cells 1–7, 8–14 and 15–20, one actuator each) and the profiles are the filer's. The commanded configurations are a three-dimensional slice of the twenty-dimensional set; a uniform profile lies in it, a step at cell 10 does not, and a regrouping of the same three actuators puts the step in and other profiles out. "A capability can exist kinematically without being practically controllable." The construction is the filer's, from A's limited budget of control authority; adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-206](../../record/cases.d/CASE-206.md) v3 | A single flat-foldable degree-4 vertex under a footprint budget: its two folding modes meet only at the flat state, so passing between folded states needs the full flat footprint of 1.866, more than twice what either folded state at 150° takes | Filed at the owner's request on 2026-11-05, to file "one or more CASEs for origami as a toy model of a resource bounded constraint configuration space". The minimal toy: one rigid flat-foldable vertex with sector angles 60°, 90°, 120° and 90°, whose configuration space is two folding modes meeting only at the flat state, and a declared resource, the plan area of the smallest convex tray that holds it. Folded states cost 0.75 and 0.875; every switch between them passes the flat state, which costs 1 + √3/2 ≈ 1.866, so the bottleneck budget is 1.866 and the reconfiguration slack 0.991. At a budget of 1.5 the sublevel set cuts each branch near flat and both states are held but neither reaches the other. Its form follows Foschi, Hull and Ku's equations as read in NOTE-1606, with coefficients the filer computed and checked and the two-mode structure derived in the case; it also draws on Abel et al. ([NOTE-1589](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-1589.md)), Chen and Santangelo ([NOTE-1614](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-1614.md)), Stern, Pinson and Murugan ([NOTE-1571](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-1571.md)) and Mirth et al. ([NOTE-1616](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-1616.md)). A mechanical toy; its organizational reading is an analogy. The construction is the filer's. Adopting it is the owner's call. | Active |
| [CASE-207](../../record/cases.d/CASE-207.md) v2 | The square twist: a crease pattern with no rigid folding motion folds once facet bending is paid for, so which configurations are connected depends on the energy available | Filed at the owner's request on 2026-11-05, to file "one or more CASEs for origami as a toy model of a resource bounded constraint configuration space". Silverberg et al. 2015 ([NOTE-1554](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-1554.md)): the square twist's crease pattern is isostatic, and rigidly it has only two isolated states, unfolded and folded, yet it folds by bending its facets, a hidden degree of freedom with its own energy scale. The authors' thesis is that the number of degrees of freedom changes with the energy scale probed; their kinematic skeleton, with bending capped at 10°, has a critical plane angle near 23° separating a connected configuration space from a disconnected one. Read as a resource model, the configuration space is indexed by the budget rather than fixed, and widening the admissible moves is itself priced, which joins the vertex under a budget ([CASE-206](../../record/cases.d/CASE-206.md)) to the curved vertex whose moves no budget widens ([CASE-204](../../record/cases.d/CASE-204.md)). A mechanical study; its organizational reading is an analogy. Adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-208](../../record/cases.d/CASE-208.md) | A prestressed Kresling stack: reordering the cells' energy barriers from outside opens the 8 of 56 transitions between stable states that the barrier order blocked, while the 16 that pass through other stable states are not made direct | Filed at the owner's request on 2026-11-05, to file "one or more CASEs for origami as a toy model of a resource bounded constraint configuration space". Zang, Zhao, Misseroni and Paulino ([NOTE-1569](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-1569.md)): a stack of three bistable Kresling cells has 8 stable states and 56 ordered transitions; 32 are feasible, 16 cannot be made without passing through other stable states, and 8 are blocked by the order of the cells' energy barriers. Springs and handles, applied from outside, prestress the cells and reorder the barriers in place, which makes the 8 blocked transitions feasible; the 16 are not opened. Read as a resource model, the barriers bound the transitions, and the blocked ones are opened by changing the architecture, not by a larger load: the physical counterpart of [CASE-198](../../record/cases.d/CASE-198.md). Jules et al. ([NOTE-1617](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-1617.md)) show the same dependence on the order of thresholds in a Kresling memory. A mechanical study; its organizational reading is an analogy. Adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |

The times are the filer's: a 30-minute job at A, 40 minutes' travel to B, and B's deadline at 60 or 90 minutes. Simultaneous implies sequential, which implies separate; the squad is separately feasible at both deadlines, sequentially only at 90, simultaneously never. "Its repertoire is broader than its simultaneous service capacity." The construction is the filer's, from A's table of simultaneous, separate and sequential capability; adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |

| [CASE-212](../../record/cases.d/CASE-212.md) v2 | Three demands and four configurations: each pair of demands is jointly met at some cost, the pairs enter the budgeted complex at budgets 1, 2 and 3, and the triple never does, so raising the budget relieves scarcity and leaves the frustration where it was | A's proposal, from the pasted synthesis on constraint architectures (pasted by the owner on 2026-11-04, continuing the exchange on emergency readiness whose share link could not be fetched), Part 4: the resource-parameterized complex K(b). A gives the definition and a flat-foldable vertex as its mechanical case, without numbers; the four configurations, their costs and the demands they meet are the filer's. Two of the missing pairs at budget 1 are scarcity; the missing triple is frustration.
