| [CASE-213](../../record/cases.d/CASE-213.md) v3 | Provisional dispatch and cancellation of a specialized rescue resource: a capability is mobilized under uncertainty, then released without performing its specialized task | A's proposal, from the exchange on emergency readiness (2026-11-04; its share link could not be fetched, and its text was pasted by the owner), section 1 of A's "Proposed additions to Nucleation PR [#401](https://github.com/dmarx/nucleation/issues/401)". A heavy rescue squad is dispatched to a reported collision before the first engine has established whether extrication is needed, and is cancelled when the engine finds it is not. Waiting costs A + T minutes; provisional dispatch costs max(A, T), so it saves min(A, T). A constructed case: the USFA page [LIT-2084](../../record/literature.d/LIT-2084.md) is a real-world analogue, not its documentation. | [CASE-214](../../record/cases.d/CASE-214.md) v2 | A one-degree-of-freedom precision stage: five independent constraints leave one usable motion, and two designs with the same freedom space, one with every constraint doubled, differ in disturbance error and in drive force | A's proposal, from the pasted synthesis on constraint architectures (pasted by the owner on 2026-11-04, continuing the exchange on emergency readiness whose share link could not be fetched), Part 1: a one-degree-of-freedom stage, freedom dimension 1 and constraint dimension 5, and "compare designs with identical freedom spaces and different stiffnesses". Built here as a linear stiffness model with two designs, the second adding a redundant twin to every constraint: the freedom space is the same, the disturbance error halves and the drive force doubles, so neither design dominates. Hopkins's theses supply the fact the construction turns on, that redundant constraints change stiffness, load capacity and stability and leave the kinematics alone. An analogue, static, of [CLAIM-345](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-345.md)'s point, which A proposed to extend by a dated note rather than a new claim. The construction is the filer's, from A's one-degree-of-freedom stage; adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-215](../../record/cases.d/CASE-215.md) | In A's branching assembly model, one unit of supply placed on the nucleating step or on the completing step gives yields 71.1 and 62.7 %, and the same integrated exposure delivered early, evenly or late gives 70.6, 58.8 and 24.8 % when nucleation uses it, but 45.4, 58.8 and 65.3 % when it traps the unnucleated scaffold and completes the nucleated one | The assistant's construction of the case A proposes in its fourth synthesis on DNA origami, pasted by the owner on 2026-11-05: "Two equal-cost nucleating interventions with identical target compatibility but different assembly yields", together with A's Prediction 5, on the order of allocation at equal total expenditure, here read as equal integrated exposure. A's model is used (U → N at α, U → K at β, N → F at γ, N → K at δ; baseline 1, 0.5, 2, 0.25), with F and K absorbing. A unit of a component's supply doubles the rate of the step that component takes part in. The target and its compatibility are the same in every arm. Computed with python3 -I by matrix exponentials over a horizon of 4. Whether early or late supply is better turns on which steps the supplied component enters, which is [CLAIM-598](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-598.md)'s point in time instead of amount. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-216](../../record/cases.d/CASE-216.md) v2 | A four-state annealing toy, unfolded, nucleus, folded and a misfold trap, with free energies and transition states linear in temperature: the fraction folded at one hour peaks at 90.4 % at 318.6 K among constant temperatures, at sixteen hours it is at least 90 % from 313 to 323.5 K, and five minutes at 327 K followed by 318 K leaves 98.5 % folded at the hour, which no constant temperature does; the chance of having reached the folded state agrees to four places | The assistant's, on the owner's request of 2026-11-05 to connect the record's inquiry to DNA origami. A minimal model of thermal annealing in which the resource that pays for crossing barriers, temperature, also sets the free energies and barriers it pays for. Four states: U (unfolded), N (a nucleus, as in Schneider, Möritz and Dietz's compacted intermediate), F (the folded target) and M (a misfold trap). Each state and each transition state has an enthalpy and an entropy, so its free energy is linear in temperature, and rates are Arrhenius. Computed with python3 -I by matrix exponentials of the rate matrix. Reachability of the target within a time budget is a window in temperature, not an up-set, whether it is counted as the fraction folded at the horizon or as the chance of having reached the folded state by then; more time widens the window; a two-step schedule reaches what no constant temperature reaches in the same time. The parameters are chosen to show the effect and are not fitted to any origami. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Active |
| [CASE-217](../../record/cases.d/CASE-217.md) v2 | A four-state branching model of assembly, unassembled, productive nucleus, trap and folded, in which three designs with the same target yield 59.3, 76.2 and 29.6 %, and scaling every supply by the same factor leaves the yield unchanged only while the competing exits from each state have the same molecularity | A's model, from A's second synthesis on DNA origami, pasted by the owner on 2026-11-05, §2, with the assistant's extension. From U the system enters a productive nucleus N at rate α or a trap K at rate β; from N it completes to F at γ or falls into K at δ. With K and F absorbing, P_F = α/(α+β) · γ/(γ+δ). A's three designs (baseline, guided nucleation, competing early binding) share the target and yield 59.3, 76.2 and 29.6 %, checked with python3 -I. A notes that scaling α and β together leaves the first branch unchanged. The assistant adds that this holds only when both exits have the same molecularity. If entering the trap from N binds a further molecule and completing does not, a uniform increase of supply by λ gives P_F = α/(α+β) · γ/(γ+λδ), which falls from 59.3 % at λ = 1 to 19.0 % at λ = 20. The rates are A's invented illustrative parameters, not measurements. The model is A's; the extension is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Active |
| [CASE-218](../../record/cases.d/CASE-218.md) | A two-stage toy of nucleus stabilization, nucleation s/(1+s) and completion 1/(1+s²/4), whose final yield peaks at s ≈ 1.11 with 40.2 %, so that stabilizing the nucleus beyond the optimum makes it easier to form and lowers what forms | A's toy, from the fourth synthesis on DNA origami pasted by the owner on 2026-11-05 ("Predicting which organizations will successfully nucleate"), Prediction 3. Yield Y(s) = P_N · P_F\|N has an interior optimum. A's figures at s = 1.10 are nucleation 52.4 %, completion 76.8 % and yield 40.2 %, with the optimum near s = 1.11; they are checked with python3 -I, the optimum at s = 1.1134. The functional forms are A's, invented to show the mechanism. Dunn et al.'s weakened seams and Majikes et al. 2021's blocked fold are the molecular instances of the descending side. The wording is A's; adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |

Starting from the authors' shrub-spot pattern, hollow rings form when the shrub depletion radius is contracted by hand, whether or not grass is present. Without contraction no rings form. With shrub recruitment left on, no rings form. With grass present the rings decay to almost none by 2,500 steps; without grass they stay unchanged. The paper's account, in which grass competition constrains the shrubs and the hollow sustains them against "the siege by surrounding grasses", is not what the model does. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |

| [CASE-264](../../record/cases.d/CASE-264.md) v3 | Two workshops with the same dependence and the same integration, the part able to withdraw and renegotiate in one and its terms set at the controlling agent's discretion in the other | The assistant's, the discriminating case for [CLAIM-690](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-690.md), offered at step 12 of the filing of "About Plantation Paradise" ([#488](https://github.com/dmarx/nucleation/issues/488)) and accepted by the owner on 2026-11-13. Two workshops, sense-1 arrangements ([TERM-074](../../record/terms.d/TERM-074.md)) run by a controlling agent R, each with a constituent P. The whole needs P as much in both, P needs the whole as much in both, P gave up the same capability to join in both, and P is a constituent in both. Only control over the terms differs: in A, P can withdraw and renegotiate; in B, R sets P's terms and P cannot leave. The design is the review's; the model and numbers are the filer's. Two organizations decide at the same rate with the same error share, rules, appeal rights and initial corrective capacity, and perform identically until correction is attempted. Errors can be corrected only once the governing rule is revised, after T days. In A execution leaves corrective capacity intact; in B each decision uses some of it up. A can still correct all accumulated errors for T up to 100 days, B only up to 20. It separates three mechanisms: throughput (accumulated corrections exceed capacity), specification lag (T) and endogenous foreclosure (the decisions themselves reducing capacity). The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |

| [CASE-307](../../record/cases.d/CASE-307.md) | The record's history from 2026-10-07 to 2026-11-22, counted at matched age, shows claims with inbound rests_on or grounds edges taking substantive revisions about 1.45 times as often as claims with none, and the fall in revision rate over an entry's life tracking its age, not its edges | [LEAD-199](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-199.md)'s count, as it specified. Population: the 859 claim files on staging, followed through renames and formerly: fields, over the record's whole history (2026-10-07 to 2026-11-22, about 46 days). A revision is substantive if it changes the title, role, warrant, defeated_if or the body outside dated notes, after normalising away link markup, directives, cited codes and citation-only parentheses; schema backfills and bot commits are excluded. Exposure: the replayed count of other entries naming the claim in rests_on or grounds. Result, at matched age (Mantel-Haenszel by age bin): claims with any inbound edge are revised 1.47 times as often as claims with none (95% interval 1.22 to 1.77; 1.45 stratified by age and calendar week; 1.62 by risk-set matching over seven days), and title and status changes rise too. The raw fall from before to after the first edge (ratio 0.58) is the filing-day burst: it vanishes when each claim's first day is dropped (1.04). The 6+ group has 4 to 7 events and an interval including 1. Classifier precision by hand-check: about 0.7 by intent, 0.9 by the letter. 148 of the 303 revisions after a first edge fall within a day of it, so edges and revisions come in the same working sessions. | Proposed |
| [CASE-308](../../record/cases.d/CASE-308.md) | A small assembly model whose scaffold forms and holds a nucleus, withdrawn for good at chosen states: the drop in the chance of completing falls to under 0.05 two units into eight only where the bound units hold the nucleus and it speeds their binding, stays at 0.10 or more to the last unit where they do not hold it, and under withdrawal at a clock time falls to zero in every case as runs finish | [LEAD-095](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-095.md) asked for a Markov chain in which support can be withdrawn at chosen intermediate states ([QUESTION-398](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-398.md)) and the nucleus's relations switched on as a state and as a rate modifier separately ([QUESTION-397](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-397.md)). The chain has a nucleus, present or not, and M = 8 other units; a scaffold forms the nucleus fast and stops it dissolving; withdrawal at a state is permanent. A's measure D(s) = q_supported(s) − q_withdrawn(s) was computed exactly by linear solve at every state, in four mechanisms. With both feedback (bound units stabilize the nucleus) and a rate effect (the nucleus triples the binding rate), D falls from 0.085 to under 0.05 at n = 2 and to 0.002 at n = 7, while the chance of completing after withdrawal is already 0.88 at n = 2: independence before completion, not completion. With the rate effect and no feedback, D falls only from 0.35 to 0.10 and the scaffold is needed to the end; with neither, D rises from 0.13 to 0.22 in this parameter set, though not in most of a random sweep. Withdrawal at a clock time shows a drop that falls to zero in every mechanism, because runs finish: A's rival, "the system merely completed before withdrawal", is real under timed withdrawal and excluded by withdrawing at states. With the scaffold absent, switching the nucleus's state and rate effects separately shows both raising the committor at matched progress, with only the binding rate (Δk = 2 against 0) telling them apart. The model is the assistant's and its rates are invented. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |

| [CASE-311](../../record/cases.d/CASE-311.md) | The record's individuation criteria applied to the Game of Life block and eater, by computation: the block repairs any one lost cell and nothing else, the eater only the glider strike it is shaped for, single-flip coupling inside the block is zero while a birth on its border kills it, and the two blocks of the earlier two-glider collision share two cells throughout | [LEAD-069](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-069.md)'s study. The block and eater 1 of [CASE-260](../../record/cases.d/CASE-260.md) and [CASE-259](../../record/cases.d/CASE-259.md) are run in Conway's Game of Life against each individuation criterion the record holds. Both are fixed points. The block returns from each of its four single deletions and from no birth on its border or pair of deletions. The eater returns from none of its single deletions but from the glider strike in its lane, in all four phases. Coupling read as sensitivity to one flipped cell is zero inside the block at every horizon and large from its border, in the gun too. The eater's inside coupling beats its border's at one generation and not at five. In [CASE-260](../../record/cases.d/CASE-260.md)'s collision, the block that is destroyed and the block that lasts occupy the same four cells, two of them live throughout. Random soups give the cleaner transient: an isolated block with all four cells dead two generations later. The idea of the Life cases is the owner's. It is [CASE-155](../../record/cases.d/CASE-155.md) part 4's walk with one change: the interpretation is a second state, as in [CASE-099](../../record/cases.d/CASE-099.md), which authority shapes and which the authority score never reads. Scoring is then independent of the interpretation ([CASE-099](../../record/cases.d/CASE-099.md)'s γ = 0) and not of the loop's state, since the score rises with the incumbent's share. Above the threshold π* = (1 − h/c)/2 the disfavoured process keeps its authority and the interpretation settles on its side against the evidence; with no share-dependence it does not. Checked by script (scratch/studies/lead-121/). The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-313](../../record/cases.d/CASE-313.md) | Removing the core of Ito and Kaneko's coupled-map network: in 12 of 12 seeds the periphery refills it to its former size and one-way structure within a million steps; half a core removed is made up from the periphery; a core made rare in influence is recovered by its own members | [LEAD-065](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-065.md) asked whether [QUESTION-132](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-132.md)'s candidate case, Ito and Kaneko's network of identical logistic maps with normalized, similarity-reinforced weights ([LIT-1842](../../record/literature.d/LIT-1842.md), [NOTE-1414](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-1414.md)), refills its core when core units are removed. The model was reimplemented at the paper's parameters (N = 100, a = 3.97, c = 0.12, δ = 0.1) and grown for 3 × 10⁶ steps in 12 seeds. It reproduces a core of 15.6 ± 1.2 units by the paper's snapshot criterion, and 18.2 ± 1.1 by time-averaged outgoing weight above the mean, with 1.6 ± 2.6 periphery-to-core links (paper: 2). Five arms then ran for 10⁶ steps. With the whole core deleted, former periphery units formed a new core of 17.2 ± 0.8 units. The control that deletes as many periphery units formed one of 17.0 ± 1.1. The new core had 2.9 ± 4.8 periphery-to-core links. With a random half of the core deleted, 8.8 ± 0.8 periphery units joined the 8.5 survivors. With every weight given to the core cut to 10⁻³ of its value, the core's own members made up 92% of the core 10⁶ steps later. The differentiation recovers. Which units hold the core is set by the dynamics, not fixed by who held it first. One parameter point, one system size, 12 seeds. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-314](../../record/cases.d/CASE-314.md) | Refitting Tatel and Ackerman's posted data by task type reproduces their headline slopes and finds accuracy declining by 0.16 SD a month on laboratory tasks against 0.04 on medical and dental tasks, and not measurably on sports or military and transport tasks, with every type declining faster over short intervals | [LEAD-124](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-124.md) asked for decay estimates by skill type from Tatel & Ackerman (2025). With the authors' own specification and their posted independent-samples data (1,273 effect sizes), the main slopes are −0.076, −0.063 and −0.061 SD a month (accuracy, speed, mixed), the abstract's 0.08, 0.06 and 0.06. By task domain, accuracy declines by 0.164 a month on laboratory tasks, 0.043 on medical and dental, 0.032 on miscellaneous and 0.009 on military and transport, and sports improves by 0.015 (robust SEs 0.012 to 0.024). Capped at six months, laboratory 0.54 and medical and dental 0.12. The laboratory–real-world gap holds at every cap; every slope shrinks as longer intervals are admitted. | Proposed |
| [CASE-315](../../record/cases.d/CASE-315.md) | A readiness simulator run under spend-matching and under carry-over: a heavy rescue squad whose budget is set to the last month's peak is lost for good in every run within months where it is sent only on confirmation, in a fifth of runs within nine years where it is sent provisionally to each of six reports a month, and never where it drills each month or the unspent budget rolls over | [LEAD-080](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-080.md) asked for A's simulator of [QUESTION-394](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-394.md) to be run twice, once with each month's budget set to the last month's peak spending ([CLAIM-577](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-577.md)) and once with carry-over, to see whether the heavy-rescue response of [CASE-213](../../record/cases.d/CASE-213.md) stays reachable. The simulator has one or two squads, reported collisions of which a tenth need extrication, Poisson arrivals, a first-unit assessment right nine times in ten, travel from distance, mutual aid, skill that halves in about seven months without practice, and [CLAIM-577](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-577.md)'s capacity of 0.7 to hold and 1.3 with a squad out. Under spend-matching the squad is lost for good in every one of 300 runs, by a median of month 2 to 8, when it is sent only on confirmation or reports are few (two a month), and in 20 % of runs within nine years when it is sent provisionally to every one of six reports a month; drilling every month keeps it in every run. Rolling over the unspent part keeps it in every run; rolling over 60 % of it loses it as fast as spend-matching. [CASE-200](../../record/cases.d/CASE-200.md)'s carry-over rule never loses it for good but, used every month, leaves it reachable in half the months. So the claim's prediction holds, and a quiet period turns out to mean a month in which nothing at all mobilizes the squad, not a month without a true need. The model is the assistant's and its rates are invented. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-316](../../record/cases.d/CASE-316.md) | Same oversight, the acting institution's speed varied, in a queueing model run over twenty seeds: at ten times the speed the share of objectionable actions stopped before they became irreversible fell from 0.99 to 0.40, and to 0.04 when speed also multiplied the volume of actions; precommitted limits brought back about the share they foresaw and automatic suspension up to the share detected in time, each keeping most of the speed's benefit, while pauses brought it back only by giving up the speed, and not at all when volume rose | [CASE-285](../../record/cases.d/CASE-285.md) (A33 §10 C) proposed two arrangements whose overseer has the same authority and resources and whose acting institution differs only in how fast it can make actions irreversible, then precommitted limits, automatic suspension and pauses added in turn. [LEAD-068](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-068.md) asked for it by simulation. The assistant built a queueing model: actions arrive, one in ten objectionable; the overseer detects each after a random delay, evaluates it with four reviewers first come first served, and an objection suspends; the time to irreversibility has mean 30 days divided by the speed factor. Over 20 seeds, at ten times the speed the share of objectionable actions stopped in time fell from 0.994 ± 0.003 to 0.396 ± 0.019, gradually and from well before A23 §3's timing condition fails at the means; where speed also multiplied the volume of actions, it fell to 0.043 ± 0.003. In the fast arm, limits brought back the share of the gap they covered (0.25 to 1.0) keeping 0.92 to 0.99 of the speed's benefit; suspension brought back at most 0.54, the share detected before irreversibility, keeping 0.91 to 0.99; pauses brought back 0.49 for a loss of a third of the benefit and all of it only by giving up all the benefit, and under the volume reading nothing at all. The model and its rates are the assistant's and invented, so it shows what holds in the model, not in any institution. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |

Scoring components by agreement with the update made ([CASE-092](../../record/cases.d/CASE-092.md)'s Regime A) captured influence in 99% of runs and left a requirement violated, though some allocation met them all, on 0.95 of steps; scoring each by its own demand's residual (Regime B) gave 0.05, and the allocation oracle 0. For each, an accounting was written from the source and a checkout of the record at the filing's parent commit, under the read-and-file skill's accounting step and a codebook fixed beforehand, then scored item by item. Second, one two-layer ReLU network trained on four independent skills of frequency ∝ i^(−2), under all three optimizers, from order-one and from small initial scale, three seeds each. At order-one scale, changing only the optimizer from gradient descent to sign descent or Adam made the weaker skills rise late instead of fastest at the start (level at half their learning time 0.71 against 0.47 and 0.43) and cut skill 4's learning time relative to skill 1's from about 23 to about 5.5; the clean Domino form did not appear, and the skills interfered. Of the 2024 measure ([LIT-005](../../record/literature.d/LIT-005.md)), Â of the patch rises with λ and C2's variance falls to zero (0.028 to 0.000), from the least invariant candidate to the most; C1 passes every candidate at every λ. Of the 2023 measure ([LIT-049](../../record/literature.d/LIT-049.md)), δC\|E is exactly zero for the patch partition and its 24 refinements at every λ, so it registers the scaffold's partition before any endogenisation and never registers the endogenisation. Both pass counterfeits: a candidate that absorbs the external scaffold passes C2 at λ = 0, and in a common-input control with no interaction δC\|E passes all 203 partitions while Â ranks the environment-driven patch above a single cell. (2) Gaussian niches on 12 resources: operators started 10⁻³ apart differentiate. In 22 of 27 runs they reached distinct traits meeting Theorem A, with the Jacobian equal to −DH to 10⁻⁹. Against residents that re-adapted they recovered too, more slowly: a median 0.29 of the frozen rate, and as low as 0.0003. Theorem C's hypothesis held in none of the 64. Eight Stuart–Landau units on a ring with one chord, each edge carrying a phase-offset map, were run in the design's regimes. Under a shared drive, uncoupled and coupled networks both lock (phase-locking value 0.9985 and 0.9996) and their observed offsets glue (holonomy below 10⁻⁴), so timing and gluing cannot tell them apart. Displacing the drive of one member by π/2 moves the others by 0.2 per cent of that member's shift without coupling, 30 per cent at coupling 0.3 and 58 per cent at 1.0; a kick to one member reaches the others at 0.010 against 0.12 to 0.14. Withdrawing the drive leaves the coupled self-sustained network coherent (order 0.996) and the uncoupled one not (0.47). Long suppression of a reciprocally maintained capacity becomes atrophy, short suppression does not. Revision by consistency reaches coherence without improving prediction; revision checked against held-out kicks cuts the held-out error from 1.06–1.81 to 0.14–0.34. | [CASE-334](../../record/cases.d/CASE-334.md) | Compensation, entrainment and the block test measured by computation in two closed modes that are not blocks, the kink of a phi4 lattice and the locked cluster of a uniformly coupled Kuramoto population, beside a two-block control: the members compensate in all three, the cluster's entrain, and the block test on members singles out only the control | [LEAD-083](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-083.md)'s study. Three arms share one protocol: [CLAIM-157](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-157.md)'s block test on the members, compensation read as in a jaw-load experiment (a sustained load on a few members, the form's task variable read with the others free and with them frozen), and entrainment read as [CLAIM-172](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-172.md) reads it, by timing. In the kink of an overdamped phi4 lattice the sites within about one unit of its centre compensate fully, and the set that compensates moves with the kink. Coupling between sites is uniform, and the best block ratio of a segment holding the kink falls as the square of the lattice spacing, from 1.57 to 0.11. In a Kuramoto population with uniform coupling, the 156 locked oscillators of 200 compensate (0.97) and entrain (0.91), and on the coupling constants every subset of 156 scores exactly as they do. A two-block control compensates and entrains inside each block and not across, and is the only form the test picks out. The study and its wording are the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |

Causes at level 3 or above: 2 of 30 postmortems (7%) against 7 of 10 reports; level 4 or above: 0 against 5. Action items at 3 or above: 4 of 30 (13%) against 9 of 10. Under the rule fixed before coding, [CLAIM-750](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-750.md)'s prediction holds in the pilot. | [CASE-337](../../record/cases.d/CASE-337.md) | A dynamic beta–Bernoulli feature model fitted to a linear organizational simulator against a shared-driver null matched on within-group correlation: it finds more overlapping features, better aligned with the groups, when the members never interact; neither of its intervention readings is right in both worlds; the communication graph alone predicts cuts and pulses where the coupling is real and invents coupling where a driver is; adding the features does not remove the invented coupling, and observing the driver does | [LEAD-144](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-144.md)'s study, the "Option A" [CASE-165](../../record/cases.d/CASE-165.md) recommends, in one linear family. Twenty-four members in six overlapping working groups, with a communication graph built from the groups, are simulated in three worlds: coupled along the graph; driven by one persistent external driver per group with no interaction; and half of each. The driver's gain is tuned so that every world shows the same within-group correlation, 0.30. CASE-165's finite beta-Bernoulli model (K = 16, α = 3) and four comparators are fitted to 600 days of each world and scored on interventions none was fitted to: a pulse to a member, and the cut of a group's internal ties. Over seven organizations, the feature model finds more features under the null (11.1) than under coupling (8.7), better aligned with the groups (Jaccard 0.72 against 0.44); read as causes of members it is exact under the driver and misses every cut under coupling, and read as realized by its holders it is wrong in both. The communication graph alone is right under coupling and invents coupling under the driver (a predicted cut effect of −0.18 where the truth is 0); the features added to it leave that error (0.21), and observing the true driver removes it (0.006). One-step fit ranks the models the same way in every world. The design, code and wording are the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-338](../../record/cases.d/CASE-338.md) | Audited governance against a router matched on audits, delay and cost, built in numpy: frozen low-rank specialist denoisers after a reversal of expertise, with one arm each for cycles, no common scale, capture, bandit feedback and continued training; governance wins only against a router weaker in a stated respect, and the best router matches or beats the best governance in every arm | [CASE-158](../../record/cases.d/CASE-158.md)'s design, built and run for the first time in the record, with the router given the same audit records, the same one-episode delay and the same number of probes as governance, and one arm per failure of [QUESTION-271](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-271.md) plus continued training. Under full information governance beats the contextual router at two of three temperatures, but loses at the one both do best at, and a router that sees only the region id, as governance's grants do, beats it on 12 of 12 seeds at every temperature. The router wins the cycles and capture arms. Governance wins the no-common-scale arm only by standardizing each member against its own history; given the same standardization the router ties it. Under bandit feedback, a 10% floor on auditing lets governance beat a router with the same floor, not the router without one. Continued training entrenched neither. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-339](../../record/cases.d/CASE-339.md) | Three simulated evaluative contexts with transport derived by fit on shared commitments give holonomy indistinguishable from the identity in 3000 draws of the null, a holonomy angle equal to the sum of the contexts' internal comparison angles in the planar case, and the spherical excess for local charts of a curved space of profiles; noise gives a measurable null, and few anchors per overlap let it flip orientation | The numerical side of [LEAD-067](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-067.md): the construction of [CLAIM-907](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-907.md) run in numpy (seed 20261122).
