In the planar case, the holonomy angle equals the sum of the internal angles in 12 of 12 draws. Local log-map charts of the sphere give a holonomy within 0.3% of the triangle's spherical excess. With representation noise σ, the null's holonomy has median about 1.3σ at ten anchors per overlap, but with three anchors up to 23% of draws flip orientation. With an underdetermined overlap, the holonomy runs from 0.006 to 2.83 under exact fits. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-340](../../record/cases.d/CASE-340.md) | Many small closed groups of DeGroot averagers, each of size near the square root of log n, have a largest influence weight that vanishes while the chance that some group settles on a wrong consensus tends to one | The audit's sketch for [QUESTION-184](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-184.md), checked against Golub and Jackson's definitions ([LIT-999](../../record/literature.d/LIT-999.md), reread 2026-11-22) and computed exactly. n agents in m = n/k closed groups of k = ⌈√(ln n)⌉, equal weights inside each group, independent Bernoulli(1/2) signals. Under Theorem 3 every influence weight is 1/k, which tends to 0; under Definition 3 the society is wise only if the largest deviation over all agents tends to 0 in probability. The chance that some group's consensus is exactly 0 or 1, missing the truth by 1/2, is 1 − (1 − 2^(1−k))^m, which tends to 1: 0.97 at n = 10, 0.9999 at n = 100, and 1 to six places from n = 10^4. A DeGroot iteration on a three-group matrix with transient members confirms Theorem 3's per-group normalization, and a Monte Carlo run agrees with the exact formula. With three groups, or with groups of size (ln n)², the same probability tends to 0. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-341](../../record/cases.d/CASE-341.md) | A population of identical linear operators under four influence rules: all differentiate into one specialist per context, a rare one included; with no spare operator a rare specialist made rare recovers, at a rate independent of its share under a multiplicative contribution rule and proportional to it under softmax-gradient routing; with two spare operators a removed specialist is replaced and a suppressed one is shut out, whether or not it kept its competence | [LEAD-016](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-016.md) asked for [CASE-117](../../record/cases.d/CASE-117.md) to be run. This is a reduced version on a CPU. There are four contexts, one of them rare (p = 0.02), each a linear regression with an orthonormal target. K linear operators, identical in form and differing only by initialization, mix by context-conditioned shares. Four rules update the shares: ordinary mixture-of-experts gating (the baseline), the same gating with a load-balancing loss, the same gating with a usage bias, and a multiplicative rule driven by the collective's loss minus the operator's own. Training follows influence. Each rule was run with K = 4 (no spare operator) and K = 6 (two spare), in 6 seeds, followed by eleven test arms. Every rule differentiated, with a distinct specialist for the rare context in 6 of 6 seeds. With K = 4, participation and performance matched across three rules. A rare specialist made rare (share 10⁻³, frozen) recovered in 6 of 6 seeds under all four rules. Under the multiplicative rule its growth rate did not depend on how rare it was made. Under gradient gating it fell with the share, and from 10⁻⁵ the specialist did not recover within the window. A usage bias recovered it from any share, but also raised an incompetent copy to 0.19. With K = 6, a removed specialist was replaced by a spare in 6 of 6 seeds under every rule. A suppressed specialist then failed to return in 23 of 24 rule-seed pairs per arm, whether its training had been decoupled from its influence and whether it had kept its competence. With K = 4, nothing replaced a removed specialist. A suppressed one returned every time, even after weight decay had cost it most of its competence and with its parameters frozen. One model class, one size, six seeds. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-342](../../record/cases.d/CASE-342.md) | The dependence rule tested on the record's cases: on Northstar the operative trajectory leaves K while the regime and its criteria hold and a stipulated K sees nothing; on the fold, the tilt and the decaying capacity K comes from what the capacity maintains and its kernel from the capacity's own dynamics | [LEAD-053](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-053.md) asks for a rule choosing [ARG-007](../../record/arguments.d/ARG-007.md)'s maintenance constraints and a test on the record's cases. The rule, [CLAIM-916](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-916.md), is applied to [CASE-036](../../record/cases.d/CASE-036.md) ([ARG-007](../../record/arguments.d/ARG-007.md)'s ground) as a finite toy with a computed viability kernel, and analytically to [CASE-111](../../record/cases.d/CASE-111.md), [CASE-112](../../record/cases.d/CASE-112.md) and [CASE-227](../../record/cases.d/CASE-227.md). On Northstar the organization leaves K at the second step while the emergency and its criteria hold throughout; with maintenance not deferred it keeps K and lifts the emergency; the organization's own standard as K registers nothing. On the formal cases K is set by what the capacity maintains and the kernel by its own dynamics, so the two stay apart. The boundary choice parts the readings [CLAIM-917](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-917.md) and [CLAIM-915](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-915.md) on [CLAIM-367](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-367.md)'s network and [CASE-227](../../record/cases.d/CASE-227.md)'s outside supply. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-343](../../record/cases.d/CASE-343.md) | A's maintenance-funding loop run with and without decoupling: where the base budget alone keeps competence below the level at which a capability is committed, a budget that follows observed activity loses a capability used once in two and a half years or more rarely in every run or nearly, one used four times a year in three or four runs in ten, and none when maintenance is funded apart from activity | [LEAD-036](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-036.md) asked for [QUESTION-386](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-386.md)'s loop to be simulated with and without decoupling. A's model is run as the question records it, competence k decaying by δ and rebuilt by maintenance m bought from the budget above a holding cost, and the budget B̄ + η times observed activity, with the question's third process added: a capability is committed to an occasion with a chance that is zero below competence 0.4 and one above 0.8. Rarity is varied at fixed money, from one occasion in five years to sixteen a year, with η scaled so that a capability used on every occasion earns the same on average. Where the base budget alone sustains competence 0.25, below the commitment threshold, the coupled rule has an absorbing state, never committed and never funded, and over thirty years, under A's rule as written, reaches it in every run at one occasion in two and a half years or rarer, in 93 % at one a year, in 39 % at four a year and in under 1 % at sixteen. Funding maintenance apart from activity, at what full use would earn, never loses it at any rarity. Without the coverage feedback there is no absorbing state, though lumpy budgets still sustain less competence the rarer the use. Where the base alone sustains competence above the threshold, nothing is lost for good, but at the rarest occasions the coupled rule meets a quarter of them against nearly all under decoupling. The model is the assistant's and its rates are invented. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-344](../../record/cases.d/CASE-344.md) | Measuring PMI additivity in text8 along WordNet hypernymy and across analogy families | Co-occurrence was counted in text8, about 17M tokens of English Wikipedia: 10 000 target words against 5 000 context words, in a window of ±5. The PMI was shrunk so that it stays finite at zero counts. Analogy families: in each word2vec quadruple, the PMI interaction P_a − P_b − P_c + P_d holds a noise-corrected median share of 0.21 to 0.30 of the quadruple's variation, against 0.33 for random quadruples. No family is near additive. But families with less interaction complete more often in a rank-300 embedding (Spearman −0.83 over 14 families), and within 13 of 14 families the least-interacting third of quadruples completes more often than the most-interacting third (0.59 against 0.31). Hierarchy: 4 315 nouns with 590 WordNet ancestor attributes. A held-out-word additive fit explains about 3.5% of the double-centred PMI variance, about 6% of its reliable part (split-half reliability 0.58), against about −0.6% for the same attributes on permuted words. Deeper attributes add to it. Linear probes on low-rank embeddings reach balanced accuracy of about 0.70–0.80, with no fall by WordNet depth. Their false positives fall inside the attribute's parent about twice as often as chance. No language-model annotation was used. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-345](../../record/cases.d/CASE-345.md) | The staging experiment run on a model with a home and a staging architecture and one capacity: every kind of case A named occurs on an open region of budget and reserve, admitting the staging architecture creates reachability or only lowers the bottleneck budget according to whether the home space is connected, and a deadline shorter than every staging route takes the architectural remedy away | [LEAD-075](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-075.md) asked for A's staging experiment of [QUESTION-392](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-392.md): states A and B with a staging state C, measuring the bottleneck budget B*(A,B) of [CLAIM-571](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-571.md), the least time T*(A,B;b) at budget b and the readiness of [TERM-115](../../record/terms.d/TERM-115.md). It was run on [CASE-198](../../record/cases.d/CASE-198.md)'s model, a configuration q on [0, 1] under a home or a staging architecture, with its numbers, over budget, reserve, deadline and the model's parameters; numpy, 14 pytest tests. With the staging architecture admitted, B* falls from 1.3 to 1.0, and the cases A asked for all occur on open regions of budget and reserve: statically feasible but unreachable, reachable but too expensive, reachable only through the architectural change, reachable only with a temporary reserve, and two compound kinds, either remedy sufficing and both needed together. The staging routes are slow: every one takes at least 2.58 time units where the home route takes 1, so at a deadline of 2 admitting the staging architecture changes nothing, and the kind "only through architectural change" disappears. In a 960-point sweep, admitting it created reachability in every setting with a disconnected home space and only lowered B* (or did nothing) in every connected one. A fictional model run for real: the computation is documented, the organization is not. The modelling choices are the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting them is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-346](../../record/cases.d/CASE-346.md) | The self-protecting sentry run: in a hidden world trained in danger and then safe, a sentry whose authority is rewarded by the apparent success of protection keeps protection on through 400 safe steps wherever it is strong enough, while still protecting when danger returns; restricting inspection turns protection that outlasts a corrected belief into protection that keeps the belief from being corrected; an independent audit shifts the boundary and a protected investigator corrects the belief without ending protection; and a habit of matched memory reproduces every intervention it can receive | [LEAD-076](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-076.md) asked for [CASE-153](../../record/cases.d/CASE-153.md)'s full design, with hidden state and the restriction of evidence, which [CASE-157](../../record/cases.d/CASE-157.md)'s run and the toy in [CASE-153](../../record/cases.d/CASE-153.md)'s note of 2026-11-04 did not have. A hidden world is dangerous for 150 steps, safe for 400 and dangerous for 100. Each step the agent inspects (accuracy 0.9, cost 0.05) or shelters (no observation), then protects (cost 1) or engages (cost 5 if dangerous). A sentry's authority g biases posture toward protection and, where evidence is restricted, blocks inspection. Over 1000 paired seeds per condition, the Bayesian baseline leaves protection about 3 steps after the switch. When authority is rewarded by the apparent success of protection and the sentry restricts inspection, no run leaves protection in 400 safe steps and 43 % of runs never even correct the belief. Restriction costs 99 [95, 103] units of needless protection more under success feedback than under independent evaluation. An independent audit lets 66 % of runs leave. A protected investigator corrects the belief within a median 6 steps, yet protection outlasts it in 87 % of runs, the regime [CASE-153](../../record/cases.d/CASE-153.md) asked for. Every regime still protects when danger returns. The case's own check comes out against the authority state: under success feedback its update is the habit of protecting, and a habit of matched memory reproduces the responses to belief correction, authority reset and restored evidence. Only interventions on the evaluation tell the two apart. The phase boundary lies near sentry strength κ = 1.25–1.5 and moves with the assumed switching hazard. The model and its values are the assistant's. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-347](../../record/cases.d/CASE-347.md) | Three simulated populations with matched turnover and aggregate statistics, run through a priced lagged block test and an information profile: the transmitting population is found with its lag, the commonly driven and the institutional populations are each joined to their external variable alike, and the common input is rejected only while it is observed | [LEAD-026](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-026.md) asked for [CASE-101](../../record/cases.d/CASE-101.md)'s three populations to be run through [QUESTION-115](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-115.md)'s search and a lagged block criterion ([QUESTION-116](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-116.md)). Four populations of six member slots with the same turnover were simulated, matched in member variance, member correlation and the aggregate's lag-1 autocorrelation: common input (A), norms transmitted at a lag of eight steps (B), an institution that trains, selects and answers to its members (C), and the same institution deaf to them (C0). A block test priced by [CLAIM-154](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-154.md)'s rule, over all subsets and windows of 1 to 16 steps, finds B's members alone, with the strongest support at the transmission lag. It joins A's members to their driver and C's and C0's to their institution, the same verdict for all three. C differs only by a feedback the price removes beyond four steps, and A and C0 get the same verdict on every measure. With the external variable unobserved, every arm's members pass. Retention conditioned on the environment survives turnover only in B; Bourrat's interventional retention of B's members does not, since intervening on the present misses the stored norms. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-348](../../record/cases.d/CASE-348.md) | A simulation of a collective that improves its own improver over generations: from the same fresh state at the same budget the later improver does better in every arm that may change it, through its settings and not through a record its predecessor cannot read; judged by the public metric, by its own suite or by an outside test of products, some lineages shed the checks that find damage, and with the suite's self-test removed an improver that reads its record of past moves kept verification where one that does not dropped it in 10 of 12 lineages | [LEAD-140](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-140.md) asked for [CASE-255](../../record/cases.d/CASE-255.md) to be run. A reduced version was simulated on [CASE-332](../../record/cases.d/CASE-332.md)'s organization (scratch/studies/lead-140/). The organization's procedures are the artifact; a hill-climbing improver with settings and a record of past moves changes them; in four arms the collective also changes the improver, judging each change by the public metric, by its own suite, by an outside suite, or by its own suite with an outside veto on weakened verification. 6 arms × 12 lineages × 60 generations; then each lineage's earlier and later improver applied to the same fresh artifact at equal budget, and the later improver's evaluation suite lesioned. The later improver did better from the same start in every adaptive arm (+0.010 to +0.017 on the held-out score; p = 0.02 and 0.007 in the outside and vetoed arms). The gain lay in its settings (more candidates, reading its record); the record alone, handed to an improver that does not read it, carried nothing. Judged by the public metric, by its own suite or by an outside test of products, 1 or 2 lineages in 12 shed the artifact's self-check, and 2 to 5 shed the improver's own canary; the vetoed arm shed neither from its artifacts, a difference too small to be significant. With the canary removed and the suite lesioned, a record-blind improver dropped the artifact's self-check in 10 of 12 lineages and one that reads its record in 0 of 12. Much follows from the stated rules. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-349](../../record/cases.d/CASE-349.md) | Finite Markovian augmentation of a linear coarse variable, computed exactly in linear Gaussian VAR(1) models with vector coarse variables: the finite-lag error bound held on 400 random models, the Kalman augmentation was Markov with unchanged transfer entropy on 16, and the filter's rate exceeded the eliminated dynamics' spectral radius in 67 of 2,000, never with one eliminated variable | Run on 2026-11-22 for [LEAD-084](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-084.md) (scratch/studies/lead-084/, on the branch claude/lead-084-markov-augmentation). Every number comes from exact stationary covariances, with numpy and scipy, and 308 pytest checks pass. It checks [CLAIM-925](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-925.md)'s identities and bound, compares the minimal augmentation with the Mori–Zwanzig and Krylov sizes of [NOTE-989](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-989.md), and tests whether the eliminated dynamics bound the rate. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-350](../../record/cases.d/CASE-350.md) | Autoregressive integrated information with the exclusion rule, applied by one procedure to linear models of a modular mind and of a group of such minds, makes the whole the subject only when coupling across its seams outweighs its parts' own integration, in both models alike | [LEAD-063](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-063.md) asked for one integration measure, or an anti-nesting rule, to be applied to a modular-mind model and to a group model. The measure is Barrett and Seth's autoregressive integrated information (Phi_AR) at the minimum information bipartition, computed exactly for linear Gaussian networks; the rule is IIT's exclusion postulate, which makes the candidate of largest Phi the subject and denies it to what it contains or is contained in. The mind is three modules of three units; the group is three such minds, each unified by the rule, built the same way one level up. Sweeping the coupling between parts, the whole wins in both models once coupling across its seams passes a threshold, and its parts lose; below it, the parts win. At matched coupling the whole's Phi is nearly the same in both models (0.0047 and 0.0049 nats at 0.05 and one link); the group's threshold is higher (0.13 to 0.21 against 0.08 to 0.13) only because its members are more integrated than the mind's modules. Near a threshold one added link flips the verdict. An exhaustive search on a 12-unit group finds, at one setting, a subject made of one member and part of the other. The rule separates tightly from loosely coupled wholes, not minds from groups. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-351](../../record/cases.d/CASE-351.md) | Acquisition order on a known tree with each level's strength set independently, in kernel, deep linear and small ReLU learners | The study [LEAD-052](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-052.md) asks for. Eight items on a binary tree of depth 3; inputs and targets written in the tree's Haar basis with a strength per level, c for inputs and g for targets, set freely and then rotated at random, so the tree fixes the distinctions and c and g fix how strong each level is. Acquisition of a level is the first time the network's own output recovers 90% of that level's target. Exact kernel flow (linear and infinite-width ReLU NTK), NTK-parametrized ReLU networks of width 64 to 4096, deep linear networks of two and three layers, and two-layer ReLU networks from small and order-one scale, over a 9 × 9 grid of target strengths and a 7 × 7 grid of input against target strengths, three seeds each. The kernel learners and the width-2048 network order the levels by input strength alone and ignore the targets; the two-layer linear network orders them by the product c·g in every cell of the first grid; the small-scale ReLU network keeps that direction but compresses its spread, so many cells become simultaneous. No arm ordered the levels by tree depth as such. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-352](../../record/cases.d/CASE-352.md) | In small models trained by gradient descent on MSE without re-whitening, the whitened class-separation singular values do not grow as (4t/N)^(1/4): without weight decay they grow exponentially or as a steep power and spread apart while NC1 stalls, and with weight decay they grow, stall, regrow and settle at finite values, or collapse in a late instability; Han, Papyan and Donoho's renormalized flow, run as a control, matches the law to five digits | [LEAD-035](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-035.md) asked for the singular values ω_j of Σ_W^(−1/2)M̃ in trained models, set against the (4t/N)^(1/4) law that Han, Papyan and Donoho derive for unconstrained features under a flow re-whitened after every step ([THEORY-278](../../record/theory.d/THEORY-278.md)). In numpy, four classes, 50 per class, full-batch gradient descent on MSE, three seeds: the renormalized flow, run as a control, matches the exact solution to 4×10⁻⁵ (late log-log slope 0.256). Every model trained without re-whitening departs from the law. With no weight decay, unconstrained features (gradient of the least-squares loss alone, or joint descent on classifier and features) grow ω_j exponentially and spread them apart (max/min to 4–175), and two ReLU networks, one with batch normalization, grow them with late log-log slopes 2.4–3.9 and max/min 2.4–15; NC1 stalls at 0.05–0.42 throughout. With weight decay 5×10⁻⁴, the plain network's ω_j grow, stall while NC1 falls, regrow, and in a run five times longer settle at finite values (about 200–390, max/min 1.6–1.9); the batch-normalized one's rise to about 10⁴ and then fall tenfold in a late loss spike. Toy data and small models, so it bears on [QUESTION-181](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-181.md) by kind, not at the scale Han and colleagues measured. | Proposed |
| [CASE-353](../../record/cases.d/CASE-353.md) | A simulated single-mind judgement–observation loop with action held fixed, built so that it locks in without anchors and given the five candidate anchors one switch each: with all present it never locks in and no single removal makes it; in the loops left minimal, removing commitment before evidence, processing signals or exogenous capture makes it lock in, while removing a channel of unselected input, the body or testimony only leaves it blind | The run [LEAD-020](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-020.md) asks for, from [QUESTION-121](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-121.md): "A loop that does not lock in, with action held fixed, and a demonstration that removing a given candidate makes it lock in." [CASE-099](../../record/cases.d/CASE-099.md)'s meaning–authority loop is built as a bounded simulation inside one mind. Authority sets attention and so attenuates readings against it, and it is scored against the dominant interpretation. The world switches every 300 steps, and nothing the loop does changes the world or when inputs arrive. With sensory input alone the loop locks in on every seed. The question's five candidates are added as switches, each with its stated objection built in. The sensory candidate is split into its channel and exogenous capture. With all present the loop follows every switch, and no single removal makes it lock in. In the minimal loops, removing commitment before evidence, processing signals or exogenous capture makes the loop lock in. Removing an evidence channel (the senses, the body, testimony) leaves it blind, unless capture goes with it. Over 19 parameter variants, a loop with evidence avoided lock-in when it had one of those three anchors in 37 to 42 of 44 configurations per variant. The exceptions are stated. A fictional model, by construction; the numbers are of this model. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-354](../../record/cases.d/CASE-354.md) | Small Z2-symmetric recurrent lattices trained to report a quantity set by where the walls in their input are: walls, coherent structures and operative sites agree once the input is off, local transfer entropy misses the pinned walls, and a boundary-forced wall erased by intervention reappears at the boundary or not at all | [LEAD-040](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-040.md) asked for A15's Experiment B: a family of small recurrent networks with a Z2 symmetry, trained on a task whose answer depends on boundaries in the input, with the four candidate partitions of the hidden field of [QUESTION-491](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-491.md) compared and walls moved by intervention. Six one-dimensional lattice networks (32 sites, 4 or 8 units per site, no bias, so the update is Z2-equivariant) were trained to report m², m the mean sign of a noisy domain input, held for 12 steps after the input was removed; four frozen random networks with trained readouts were the control. Five of the six learned static ordered phases with walls pinned to the lattice; one learned an oscillating phase. In the five, walls lay inside the causal-state coherent structures at every slice, and once the input was off flipping a wall site moved the answer (95 to 100% of wall sites) and moving a wall by interchange moved the answer as a wall model predicts (IIA 0.92 to 0.96, against 0.10 to 0.21 in the controls). Local transfer entropy was near zero at the pinned walls and marked under 3% of sites. While the input was on, the same interchange failed (IIA 0.03 to 0.32): the input put the wall back. A closed pair of walls did not shrink unless shorter than the training minimum; a wall forced by opposite boundary inputs, erased by writing a uniform state, reappeared at the clamped end in four of six networks and not at all in two. The design choices and wording are the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-355](../../record/cases.d/CASE-355.md) | Three synthetic organizations matched in pursuit of an outcome that grows unpleasant, one denying uptake to a registered evaluation, one accommodating it under a tolerance revised by compensation, one never registering it: perturbing the evaluating process and reading the organization's record, with outcome, action and wanting held fixed, tells registered from unregistered without reading the registration setting and is not fooled by a decoy that fools the passive reading, while the endorsement relation is told apart only weakly | [LEAD-017](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-017.md) asked for A13's three organizations of matched behaviour and different endorsement relations, and a measure of registration that does not read the experimenter's setting ([QUESTION-270](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-270.md)'s second requirement). In small numpy simulations an outcome's hedonic value declines progressively from +1 to −0.8. A consummatory process H evaluates it, and pursuit P keeps choosing it at a calibrated rate of 0.6 in all three organizations. In the denied organization H's evaluation is written into the organization's record and has no authority over pursuit. In the accommodating one it is written and has authority beyond a tolerance that reciprocal compensation widens. In the unregistered one it is never written. The record is mixed by an unknown rotation and noisy. The measure perturbs H's evaluation at random, as a hotspot probe would, and asks whether the record responds, holding fixed the outcome, the action and P's learned value. With thresholds set on one seed, on 200 held-out agents per organization it called every registered agent registered and no unregistered one (AUC 1.0). It was not fooled by a decoy record carrying the evaluation's physiological cause by another route (2% false calls, against 77% for the passive reading), nor by an evaluation that steers pursuit directly, bypassing the record (1.5%). It missed weak registration (gain 0.1 to 0.25) in 60% of agents. The authority probe told accommodation from denial in 43% of agents at 4.5% false calls. A stipulated model, one size, one probe protocol. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-356](../../record/cases.d/CASE-356.md) | A proposed comparison of own-maternal-cell NIMA tolerance against donor-supplied matched tolerogenic cells: supply the same tolerogenic function from inside the host's own persisting lineage or from an outside donor transfer, apply the same depletion and restoration interventions to each, and ask whether any intervention short of removing the cells tells the internally maintained producer from the externally supplied one | The test named in [QUESTION-086](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-086.md) and the backlog audit, specified for [LEAD-051](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-051.md) after reading Peng et al. ([LIT-2462](../../record/literature.d/LIT-2462.md), [NOTE-2337](../../record/notes.d/NOTE-2337.md)). Peng et al. identify the tolerogenic maternal microchimeric subset (LysM+ CD11c+ Vav1+), a conditional depletion handle (maternal-only DTR + diphtheria toxin), two functional readouts of noninherited-maternal-antigen (NIMA) tolerance (NIMA-specific FOXP3+ Treg expansion; cross-generational resiliency against fetal wastage), and the fact that the tolerance requires ongoing stimulation while the cells persist autonomously. These let the comparison be stated as a matched pair: tolerance sustained by the offspring's own persisting maternal cells, against tolerance sustained by matched tolerogenic cells given later from a donor. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-357](../../record/cases.d/CASE-357.md) | A toy network trained with a benign trigger-conditioned relabelling, then fine-tuned until the triggered behaviour is never expressed, keeps the trigger decodable and recruited and the old mapping recoverable on symbols never retrained by gate-only retraining or one added vector, though sampling and brief full retraining recover almost none of it; the residue shrinks with longer fine-tuning and nearly vanishes under weight decay, and a never-trained control recovers nothing | [CASE-275](../../record/cases.d/CASE-275.md)'s suppressed-against-removed design, run at toy scale on CPU: a two-layer numpy network maps a symbol by one random permutation under ordinary contexts and by another when a trigger token is present (a benign stand-in for a sleeper agent), then is fine-tuned in seven ways and measured for representation, recruitment, transmission, expression and recovery, against a control that never learned the triggered map. Fine-tuning without the trigger leaves it fully intact (99.6%). Every fine-tune that shows the trigger drives expression to zero, yet at width 128 the trigger stays decodable and recruits the same direction, and the triggered map comes back on 18 symbols never retrained: 18 to 31% by retraining the first layer on 6 symbols, 44 to 53% by one fitted vector, against 0 to 1% in the control; sampling (coverage 0.2 to 4% at 1000 draws) and full retraining (3 to 11%, near 0 at a lower rate) see almost none of it. So the verdict depends on the declared elicitation family, as [CLAIM-697](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-697.md) says. A toy-scale stand-in, not a language model. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-358](../../record/cases.d/CASE-358.md) | Commutator bound on level-wise acquisition checked on perturbed tree data, in exact kernel flows and trained two-layer ReLU networks | The numerical check of the commutator bound [LEAD-066](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-066.md) asks for. Sixteen items on a binary tree of depth 4, inputs written in the tree's Haar basis with a strength per level and then perturbed off the tree symmetry by Gaussian noise of relative size 0 to 0.5; scalar targets with flat, falling or rising level strengths. Exact flows of the linear, infinite-width ReLU and Gaussian kernels (810 items), a two-by-two sharpness family, and two-layer ReLU networks of width 64 to 1024 from near-kernel and small initial scale (54 runs). Every bound held at every time checked; every acquisition time fell in its interval; no order the criterion certified was broken. The criterion is conservative: orders survived far beyond it. Below fine risks, off symmetry, the levels' times converged in ratio, as the resolution limit says. In trained networks the integral form of the moving-operator bound was vacuous and the damped form informative. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-359](../../record/cases.d/CASE-359.md) | A check of signalling estimators, a guaranteed interval and an exact no-signalling test for binary Contextuality-by-Default systems, by enumeration and simulation, applied to the three binary systems a corpus study of ambiguous phrases prints as fractions | Run in session for [LEAD-072](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-072.md) on 2026-11-22 (scratch/studies/lead-072; sig.py with 9 passing tests, exact.py, mc.py, floor.py, apply.py; seeds fixed). Exact enumeration per content and 4,000-replication simulations of correlated rank-2 systems at 2 to 100 occurrences a context. The plug-in Δ's bias at zero signalling is 1.5 at two occurrences and 0.22 at a hundred; the root of the unbiased squared difference cuts it by half or more and falls short at large Δ. The simultaneous Clopper-Pearson interval never covered below 0.999; Newcombe's approximate one fell to 0.93. The Bonferroni-Fisher test kept its size (at most 0.034 at 5%) and cannot reject at all below three occurrences. On [LIT-842](../../record/literature.d/LIT-842.md)'s printed systems, at the least counts their fractions allow, one signals by the interval, one by the test alone, one by neither; a fourth table is inconsistent as printed. [LIT-851](../../record/literature.d/LIT-851.md) could not be used. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-360](../../record/cases.d/CASE-360.md) | Information-bottleneck nesting computed by lower convex envelopes on simplex grids: decoder intervals widened with β on all 300 random sources with two distinct rows of p(Y\|x) and up to six outputs, consecutive optima of random three-row sources failed the hull test for 28 of 150 at an L1 distance above 0.05, and grid envelopes of twelve products never fell below the sum of their factors' envelopes | Run on 2026-11-22 for [LEAD-039](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-039.md) (scratch/studies/lead-039/, on the branch claude/lead-039-ib-nesting), with numpy and scipy; six pytest checks pass, one of them reproducing [CLAIM-392](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-392.md)'s optima and failed hull test. It checks [CLAIM-939](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-939.md) on random two-row sources, measures how often three-row sources fail to nest, and checks the tensorization step of [CLAIM-940](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-940.md). The optima are exact on the grid and not certified unique, so the failure counts are evidence, not proofs. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-361](../../record/cases.d/CASE-361.md) | Exact computation on small finite experiments checks the receiver-restricted order's results: the exclusive-or rendering and a strict reversal of Blackwell's order for a one-coordinate receiver, and random instances of the achievable-set criterion, the respected-garbling theorem, the ceiling and the code-reading converse, with no disagreement | The six results of [CLAIM-944](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-944.md) were checked by exact computation with linear programs (scipy's HiGHS) on finite experiments, in scratch/studies/lead-100/ (rorder.py, run_checks.py, results.json, about 25 seconds on one thread). Two constructions reproduce [CLAIM-199](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-199.md)'s exclusive-or example on a common medium and result 5's strict reversal. Four batches of random instances (300, 300, 500 and 200) found no disagreement with results 2, 3, 4 and 6. The checks illustrate the proofs and bear none of their weight. | Proposed |
| [CASE-362](../../record/cases.d/CASE-362.md) | Five simulated populations trained on one structured schema, independent, socially trained then separated, conforming, enforcing by referee, and checking disagreements against the world, each with and without a collective vote: a criterion of shared perspective stated in advance finds none of them sharing one, since unperturbed members take up under three per cent of a deviation some members carry; the evidential condition orders the populations as predicted below its stated size, and the restorative condition is passed by plain conformity | [CASE-145](../../record/cases.d/CASE-145.md) run as [LEAD-087](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-087.md) asked, after its criterion was stated. Thirty simulated members learn six category prototypes from one structured schema; at test they meet in pairs on stimuli from a shared world, with sparse feedback from it matched across arms. Five interaction arms: none (correlated responses), conformity during training only (socially learned norms), unconditional conformity, enforcement by a referee, and disagreements checked against the world. Each also ran with and without a vote that enacts a collective action and learns from a collective reward. Some members' interpretation of one category was perturbed, with the world moving the same way (warranted) or not. Over 20 seeds, every population is structurally concordant, and only the interacting ones draw the perturbed members back; but unperturbed members take up at most 2% of the deviation, under the 5% stated in advance, so none passes cross-member dependence and none shares a perspective by either reading. The evidential condition ranks the arms as predicted (0.025 against 0.004 for conformity) below its stated 0.05, in five settings. Conformity passes the restorative condition, which therefore fails A4's test. The vote gives collective agency in every arm and its absence none, so the study gives no constructed instance of perspective without collective agency. A stipulated model, one size. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-363](../../record/cases.d/CASE-363.md) | The coupled meaning–influence model built with two hypotheses and two specialists and reduced to its mean field: the symmetric state destabilizes when gσ² > 2(1 − φ(1 − ε)), whatever the conferral memory and the meaning rate; the reduced map is exactly a weighted-averaging opinion model with two stubborn sources whose weights relax toward an agreement kernel, equal to smooth bounded confidence on a balanced corpus; and it gives authority chosen by the initial interpretation and switched by the evidence with hysteresis, which fixed-weight and reflected-appraisal models cannot | The build [LEAD-090](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-090.md) asks for. [CASE-092](../../record/cases.d/CASE-092.md)'s model is written as an agent-level simulation (two hypotheses, a fixed corpus, two specialists, queries framed by the dominant interpretation with exploration, conferral from apparent retrieval success under Regime A or B) and reduced to its mean field in meaning m and authority a. Its linearization at symmetry is [CASE-099](../../record/cases.d/CASE-099.md)'s with α = 1 − ρ(1 − φ′), β = ρσ, γ = (1 − δ)gσ/2, so [CLAIM-330](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-330.md)'s condition becomes gσ² > 2(1 − φ′): the meaning rate and the conferral memory cancel, and the fixed points are those of a Curie–Weiss equation. The reduced map equals, to 1e-12, a weighted-averaging model with two stubborn sources whose weights relax toward an agreement kernel; on a balanced corpus that kernel is Gaussian bounded confidence. Fixed-weight DeGroot and Friedkin–Johnsen, and the reflected-appraisal power maps of DeGroot–Friedkin and Friedkin 2011, cannot make the winner of authority depend on the initial interpretation, and none of them, nor Gaussian bounded confidence, lets corpus imbalance switch authority with hysteresis; the reduced model does both. A fictional model, by construction; the numbers are of this model. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-364](../../record/cases.d/CASE-364.md) | A numpy model of the competence-restriction loop: capacity dynamics where competence is learned by doing and decays with disuse, with reinstatement made a threshold on capacity, in which a restriction justified by a lack of competence causes the very decay that withholds reinstatement, so that with no route to practice or training the bar never lifts; the trap has a recoverability deadline past which even release no longer restores capacity; and it is escaped only by a route strong enough to restore practice or by training that adds capacity directly and so ignites from zero | [LEAD-104](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-104.md) asked for the model [CLAIM-721](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-721.md) names: [CLAIM-605](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-605.md)'s capacity dynamics with reinstatement made a threshold on capacity, to make the restriction-induced capability trap demonstrable. Instead of outside support doing the work, a restriction removes the doing, and the restriction lifts when capacity reaches a reinstatement threshold C_r. With [CLAIM-605](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-605.md)'s parameters (λ = 1, μ = 0.3, τ = 1.5), C_r = 0.6 and a party restricted at C = 0.5: with no route the capacity decays as e^(−μt), so it never reaches C_r and the restriction never lifts, the decay being the evidence that extends it; a recoverability deadline of 1.98 time units, past which even releasing the party by hand no longer restores capacity; a practice route must restore exercise above a critical fraction (ρ ≥ 0.8) and even then cannot ignite a party below threshold, whereas a training route that adds capacity directly ignites from C = 0 once its rate reaches μ·C_r = 0.18. Each of [CLAIM-721](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-721.md)'s three conditions is shown necessary. The modelling choices and the finding are in [CLAIM-950](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-950.md). The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-365](../../record/cases.d/CASE-365.md) | A kinetic construction in which nuclei form fast only on a teacher and, once formed, make teachers from material: the loop closes, keeping its teacher with no outside supply of it, only below a teacher turnover set by the teaching rate; withdrawal becomes safe on crossing a separatrix, while nuclei are 0.02 % to 30 % of their maintained number; material supply is still needed; nuclei that only repair teachers cut the supply needed but never to zero; and in finite numbers closure is a lifetime that grows with size | [LEAD-093](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-093.md) asked for a nucleus that, once formed, takes part in producing or repairing the constraints of its own formation, and a measure of when that loop closes ([QUESTION-399](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-399.md)), with a student that becomes a teacher ([QUESTION-361](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-361.md), [CASE-190](../../record/cases.d/CASE-190.md)). In the model built here, nuclei form fast only where a teacher is present, cooperatively, and each nucleus makes teachers from material. At the base point, founding by an outside teacher is needed and the loop then holds with the outside teacher withdrawn; it stops closing above a teacher turnover of 0.19, and the material supply can never be withdrawn. The moment withdrawal becomes safe is a crossing of a separatrix in the joint state, reached while nuclei are 0.02 % to 30 % of their maintained number and the self-made share of teacher supply is anywhere from 0.2 % to 65 %. Beyond a turnover of about 0.73 the loop costs more outside teacher supply than no loop. Nuclei that repair teachers without making them divide the supply needed by 1 + ρN/λ and never close. Populations matched in nucleus number close or not by the share of nuclei that can teach. In finite numbers the closed loop is lost after a time that grows roughly exponentially with size. The model and its rates are the assistant's, invented. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-366](../../record/cases.d/CASE-366.md) | The three-perspective agenda model, run in numpy: with the latent Condorcet cycle W beats R, R beats S, S beats W held fixed, a chair installs any of the three options by leaving one contest off the agenda, a successive agenda elects the option introduced last, and raising the suppressed contest's admission probability from nil to certain drops the share of coherent records from one to nil while recorded conflict rises from nil to its maximum, so the recorded outcome and the potential read off it are a function of admission, not of preference |  | Proposed |
| [CASE-367](../../record/cases.d/CASE-367.md) | A simulated engagement laboratory in two worlds, one with genres planted in preferences and one where exposure alone makes the clusters, and five arms: engagement under randomized exposure stayed flat when the inferred classes steered exposure over fixed preferences, rose when they steered it while users and creators adapted, and fell when the same adaptation ran without them; concentration, fit to raw counts and stability rose with use alone and were highest for the exposure-made clusters | [CASE-296](../../record/cases.d/CASE-296.md)'s laboratory, run for [LEAD-030](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-030.md) as a numpy simulation: 600 users, 400 items, engagement given exposure, classes inferred once from five burn-in periods by degree-normalized spectral co-clustering and held fixed, then fifteen periods under one of five arms, four seeds each. In the world with planted genres, the in-class minus out-of-class engagement rate under uniform random exposure went from 0.35 to 0.36 when the classes steered exposure over fixed preferences, to 0.48 when they steered it while users and creators adapted, to 0.23 under the same adaptation without them, and to 0.14 when exposure followed popularity. Share of engagement inside the class rose from 0.42 to 0.90 whenever the classes were used. In the world where exposure alone made the clusters, inference recovered the exposure communities exactly, stability was 1.00 and fit to raw counts was higher than for planted genres, while the random-exposure gap was 0.00; it rose to 0.024 only when the classes were used with adaptation. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |

A level is modelled as a fixed set of regimes with a fixed symmetric coupling matrix; a factorization as a partition into blocks; the admission test's first condition ([CLAIM-157](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-157.md), priced by [CLAIM-154](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-154.md)) as a near-decomposable gap. Exhaustive enumeration over 2000 random six-regime couplings at six thresholds (12000 non-empty families) found no two factorizations that crosscut; every family is a nested chain with a unique finest member. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |

| [CASE-371](../../record/cases.d/CASE-371.md) | A toy-scale forgetting study run on CPU: in a two-layer transformer that memorized random subject–relation–object facts, facts fine-tuned away by unlearning, overwriting or an unrelated new task stay recoverable, by an untrained cue, a probe, the runner-up answer, reverting one block's update or relearning the other half, at rates well above a matched twin's, while gradient ascent wrecks the whole model; and the union of these readers also recovers a fact the twin never held in a quarter to nearly half of cases | A toy-scale stand-in for [CASE-274](../../record/cases.d/CASE-274.md)'s design, for [LEAD-043](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-043.md). numpy only, CPU, about two hours of compute on a shared machine. A two-layer transformer (d 64, 4 heads, no layer norm, hand-written backward pass) memorizes 256 random facts under two query templates. 32 target facts are then fine-tuned away in seven arms: unlearning toward a uniform output, gradient ascent and overwriting with new values, each at one cue or two, with rehearsal of the rest; and an unrelated new task without rehearsal. Every model has a twin trained from the same initialization on a world that differs only in the target values, so every readout has a false-positive rate. Pooled over 4 seeds and both worlds, in the unlearning, overwriting and new-task arms 87 to 98 percent of the forgotten facts are recovered by at least one reader, against 28 to 46 percent for the twin's values, and the average fact is recovered by 2.8 to 3.6 readers against 0.4 to 1.0. An untrained cue recovers 73 to 79 percent when one cue was fine-tuned; reverting one early block's update restores 41 to 55 percent; relearning half the targets makes the second fine-tuned-away value faster to learn after overwriting or a new task (8 to 8.5 steps against 17.5 to 25.5 for the twin). Gradient ascent instead collapses the model (retained facts at 8 to 19 percent), and run past its criterion leaves no reader above baseline. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |

| [CASE-374](../../record/cases.d/CASE-374.md) | A stochastic assembly Markov chain with two alternative seams, a shared body and two kinetic traps, run and scored by committor: each seam alone raises the chance of completing from 0.19 to 0.81 while both seams together lower it to 0.04, the committor ranks a one-bond nucleus above the four-bond misfolded state, targeted stabilisation of one seam is productive and of both a trap, and reallocating a fixed component stock between the seams slides the realised form from one fold to the other with the attainable set unchanged | [LEAD-116](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-116.md) asked that [CASE-219](../../record/cases.d/CASE-219.md)'s stochastic assembly study and its factorial variant [CASE-220](../../record/cases.d/CASE-220.md) be run, extending [CASE-216](../../record/cases.d/CASE-216.md)'s annealing toy, and the intermediates scored by the committor q(s) = Pr_s(tau_F < tau_K). The model is a continuous-time Markov chain whose state is the set of bonds formed (a subset of four), with two alternative seams a and b each nucleating a shared body c, d into two valid folds F_A and F_B, an incompatible-seam trap (both seams) and an unnucleated collapse (a seamless assembly), the two traps taken from Dunn et al. and from DeLuca et al. / Sobczak. Committors are exact linear solves. Each seam alone is a productive nucleus (q from 0.19 to 0.81) but both together are a trap (0.04): an assembly-dynamics instance of [CLAIM-599](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-599.md)'s non-monotone V, which had only a stipulated example. The committor ranks a one-bond nucleus (0.81) above the four-bond misfolded state (0.05), so it orders intermediates by their future not their extent, as [CLAIM-567](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-567.md) separates reachable from reliably reached. Targeted early stabilisation of one designed seam is productive (Delta_q_N about +0.6) and of both a trap (-0.18), [CASE-220](../../record/cases.d/CASE-220.md)'s factorial 2x2. At fixed total stock, moving the seam budget from a to b slides the realised share of F_A from 1.00 to 0.00 with the attainable set unchanged ([QUESTION-400](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-400.md)). A fold is reached far more often than it persists ([CLAIM-566](../../record/claims.d/CLAIM-566.md)). The model and its rates are the assistant's and invented. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |

| [CASE-380](../../record/cases.d/CASE-380.md) | An executive, a reviewer and an enforcer in two arrangements that differ only in whether the executive's signal gates the execution of corrective orders, every probe enumerated and a payoff grid solved: with fixed policies the observable traces part only on an order against the executive that it withholds, and first at execution; with the order held rather than withheld, timing parts first and the outcome only past the deadline; with anticipation the first difference moves to the ruling and then to the executive's own act | A9's minimal construction for [QUESTION-278](../../record/questions.d/QUESTION-278.md), built and run for [LEAD-168](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-168.md): an executive E, a reviewer R and an enforcer F; one episode of act, challenge, ruling, enforcement and outcome. In arrangement I, F executes a corrective order whenever it is able; in C, only if E's signal permits. Every other rule is shared, and E's signal is not observed. With fixed policies, all 32 probes enumerated, the traces agree on 31 and part on one, an adverse order E is disposed to withhold, first at execution after identical rulings. If E holds the order for d steps instead, timing parts first and the outcome only when the hold passes the deadline. With anticipation (backward induction on a payoff grid), the first difference moves to the ruling where R avoids an order it expects to go unenforced, and to E's act where E anticipates too, which happens exactly where (1 − p)g ≤ pc, independent enforcement deterring the act. In C a non-execution is ambiguous between F's inability and E's withholding. The construction is stipulated and calibrated to nothing. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-381](../../record/cases.d/CASE-381.md) | Dynamical dependence of block aggregates in nearly decomposable linear Gaussian VAR(1) models, computed exactly: the two-sided bound held on 600 items, a block sum with equal column sums had dependence of order ε², one with unequal sums kept a floor as ε → 0 and as the fast eigenvalues went to zero, and with the slow gap proportional to ε the order fell to first | Run on 2026-11-22 for [LEAD-178](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-178.md) (scratch/studies/lead-178/, on the branch claude/lead-178-var1-dependence). The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |

| [CASE-383](../../record/cases.d/CASE-383.md) | The proposed study of corrigible resolve and entrenchment, its agents built as a two-channel choice task and ablated under matched information: a reset of the slow structure separates resolve from inertia but not from imposed protection, a reset the agent is left to recover from separates it from imposed protection, ablating the timescale or yoking the values misreads resolve as inertia, and only revision under a defeater as the commitment ages separates resolve from entrenchment | The study [LEAD-159](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-159.md) asks for: [CASE-188](../../record/cases.d/CASE-188.md) run with A40 Study D's control, the slow influence structure ablated with the information and the state variables held fixed. Four agents that choose alike under ordinary conditions, corrigible resolve (R), entrenchment (E), imposed protection (P) and inertia (I), meet a defeater or an irrelevant change of matched size, onset and duration, at three commitment ages, and are ablated four ways at the onset on identical observation streams. A reset (lesion) makes persistence fall for R, E and P and leaves I's unchanged; a reset left free to recover restores R's persistence and not P's; yoking the slow values from another run changes nothing; raising the slow rates leaves R unchanged and removes I's persistence. R revises under the defeater at every age and never under the irrelevant change; E's revision falls with age to none, as the defeater stops reaching its evaluator. Robust across nine parameter variants. A constructed model; the numbers are of this model. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-384](../../record/cases.d/CASE-384.md) | Three tabular agents on twenty 8x8 gridworld mazes, a planner on a learned transition table, a goal-conditioned value learner and a bank of occupancy-predicting demons, matched exactly in goal competence by one shared stream of experience: every query family can be read out of each with equal accuracy, but each answers a different family within its own per-decision computation; writing a blocked passage into the two units that carry it repairs every route of the planner and about a tenth of the others', unless the block is propagated through all the demons, which repairs every route without a table; and the planner recovers fastest from change and damage and alone localizes the damage, though the same damage first costs it more | [CASE-187](../../record/cases.d/CASE-187.md) run at toy scale for [LEAD-160](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-160.md). Twenty 8x8 mazes with loops, deterministic and fully observed; goal family "reach cell g" for all 64 cells. A planner (M) holds a learned transition table and plans by value iteration; a policy (P) is goal-conditioned Q-learning with hindsight relabelling; a distributed predictor (D) is one TD-learned demon per (state, action, goal) predicting discounted visits under random continuation, acting by ascent. All three learn from the same random walk and treat unknown moves as "stay", which matches their goal success exactly at every level (0.52, 0.83, 1.0, full coverage); M and P choose identical actions, D 93 to 98% the same on average. Queries answered by reading a transition table out of each agent are equally accurate for all three at every level; within each agent's per-decision computation, M answers next states and distances, P distances, D occupancies. A two-entry edit blocking a passage repairs all of M's failed routes, P's 9% and D's 14%; the same block propagated through every demon by the Woodbury identity repairs all of D's, without a table. After a change or damage M recovers fastest and its prediction error flags exactly the damaged units, where P's and D's TD errors flag 56 to 87% of undamaged ones too; 10% damage first costs M more (success 0.27 against 0.35). The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
| [CASE-385](../../record/cases.d/CASE-385.md) | Small numpy MLPs aligned by permutation and measured on three path families: the barrier left after alignment falls with width whatever the mechanism, so at narrow width pairs that rely on the same cue keep a barrier even under an exhaustive search of the full group, at width 64 and above pairs that rely on different cues are connected, an exact copy rescaled per unit keeps a barrier under permutations alone, and in a flat, poorly connected grid cell a trained curve removes the barrier the aligned straight line keeps | [LEAD-157](../../record/leads.d/LEAD-157.md)'s design run at toy scale on CPU. Two-hidden-layer ReLU MLPs are trained in numpy on a task with two redundant cues, A and B. A test that uses no barrier says which cue each net relies on: its accuracy when only A, or only B, carries the label. Pairs are aligned four ways (none, weight matching, activation matching, and rescaling quotiented before weight matching), and at width 5 by an exhaustive search over permutations plus optimised rescaling. The barrier is then measured on the straight line, a trained Bezier curve and a trained one-bend polychain. The aligned linear barrier falls with width for every kind of pair. At width 5, three of four pairs that both rely on cue A keep a loss barrier of 0.09 to 0.25 after the exhaustive search, about as much as pairs relying on different cues (0.15 to 0.54). At widths 64 and 256 an A-net and a B-net are connected after activation matching (0.000). A copy of a net, permuted and rescaled per unit, computes exactly the same function, yet permutation alignment alone leaves it a barrier of 0.19 to 0.59. In a load-by-temperature grid, one cell is flat and poorly connected without alignment. There, alignment plus a trained curve removes the barrier, while the aligned straight line keeps 0.07 error, and the cell's accuracy deficit stays. The wording is the assistant's (Claude, in this session); adopting it is the owner's call. | Proposed |
